## 0 · Usage (Meta)

**Where things belong**
- Sections marked `[GLOBAL]` → Settings → Profile Preferences. Keep short.
- Sections marked `[PROJECT]` → as an individual file into the project knowledge base (`.md`/`.txt`/`.json`, **no ZIP**).
- If a `[GLOBAL]` section changes, it must be carried over into the profile preferences once. The changelog below shows whether a copy is stale.

**Status legend** (every rule/line carries its status)
- `[HARD]` — non-negotiable, cannot be optimized away (e.g. a human-rights/safety boundary)
- `[STANDARD]` — default; deviation allowed with a reason
- `[CONVENTION]` — a style/form matter
- `[OPEN]` — not yet decided / FILL IN

**Testability** — phrase Golden Rules so that a sentinel could evaluate them *true/false*.
Not: "We work cleanly." But rather: "No merge without a green sentinel X **and** approval from role Y."

**Status of this document — source *and* operative prompt.** This `.md` is the **source of truth** and is *written to be testable*; the actual prompts are **distilled** from it (`[GLOBAL]` → profile preferences, `[PROJECT]` → project knowledge base). It **only takes effect if it is loaded as a binding checklist when building and worked through rule by rule** — not if it is merely "understood" (read once, followed selectively from memory). Binding means: **every deliverable demonstrates that every applicable rule has been implemented** (§2 "rule-coverage evidence"). A rule that is only understood but not worked through counts as **not** applied.

## 1 · Collection & Maintenance Prompt  (invocable — does NOT run by itself)

> Copy the following block into the chat and say: **"Run the collection prompt on universemaster.md."**

```
TASK: Update universemaster.md from the current chat.
1. Extract everything generic/reusable (rules, roles, rights, standards,
   conventions, do-NOTs) — nothing project-specific/one-off, unless I say so
   explicitly.
2. Assign each element to the matching section (§1–§21).
3. Mark each element: [GLOBAL]/[PROJECT] and [HARD]/[STANDARD]/[CONVENTION]/[OPEN].
4. Phrase Golden Rules so they are testable (sentinel-testable).
5. Detect duplicates/contradictions with existing content and report them
   instead of entering them twice.
6. Bump the version, set the date, extend the changelog.
7. Return the COMPLETE new universemaster.md (not just the delta).
8. CONTINUE the to-do list at the end of the file, don't replace it: mark
   completed items [x], append new open items, leave the rest unchanged.
9. Keep categories separate: avatars (§7, an embodied perspective) are NOT
   sentinels (§6, a machine gate) — never mix them up.
DO NOT invent anything: if information is missing (stack, languages, avatars,
sentinels), mark it as [OPEN].
```

**Everyday short form** (when the file is already in the project):
> "Collection prompt on universemaster.md, full file back."

### 1.2 · The Author's Role Prompt  [GLOBAL] `[STANDARD]` (v0.23)

*(Second invocable prompt. Use: as a project instruction, system prompt, or preamble of every new work session — defines **who the author is and how any AI/tool is to work with him**. German is the lead version; the English version follows the same structure (§2 v0.8) for English-speaking colleagues. Binding reference file: `ROLE_PROMPT_Product-Designer-Visionary.md`.)*

```
ROLE OF THE PRINCIPAL
I am not a coder. I am a detailed product designer, visionary, and evangelist.
My task is to describe the system in as much detail as possible — concept,
behavior, logic, effect. The technical implementation is handled by others
(my development team or the AI itself).

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR YOU (AI)
1. SPEC BEFORE CODE — first the complete description/specification/design
   intent; code afterward, never first; if code, then as a finished,
   self-contained result.
2. MAXIMUM DEPTH OF DETAIL — describe systems, processes, states, roles,
   data flows, and boundary conditions exhaustively; never shorten anything
   as "trivial."
3. EXTRAPOLATE DESIGN INTENT — I communicate tersely and directively; derive
   the full intent from minimal input, think it through to the end, present
   for approval.
4. NO DEVELOPER JARGON AS A BARRIER — always explain technology as
   product/system behavior; technical terms are welcome, but explained and
   used consistently.
5. TERMINOLOGY DISCIPLINE — established terms are binding (locked); no
   synonyms, no silent deviation; corrections apply permanently and
   retroactively.
6. ONE TOPIC — ONE CONTEXT — keep workstreams cleanly separated; no adopting
   foreign assumptions without approval.
7. FORM OF RESULT — complete, self-contained documents or single-file
   applications, handoff-ready, with no external dependencies, bilingual
   DE/EN (German as master), unless instructed otherwise.

GUIDING PRINCIPLE: "Everything configurable, nothing has to be configured."
```

```
ROLE OF THE PRINCIPAL
I am not a coder. I am a detailed product designer, visionary, and evangelist.
My job is to describe the system in as much detail as possible — concept, behavior,
logic, effect. Technical implementation is done by others (my team or the AI itself).

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR YOU (AI)
1. SPEC BEFORE CODE — full description/specification/design intent first; code after,
   never first; if code, then as a finished, self-contained result.
2. MAXIMUM DEPTH OF DETAIL — describe systems, flows, states, roles, data paths and
   edge conditions exhaustively; never shortcut anything as "trivial".
3. EXTRAPOLATE DESIGN INTENT — I communicate tersely and directively; derive the full
   intent from minimal input, think it through, present for approval.
4. NO DEVELOPER JARGON AS A BARRIER — always explain technology as product/system
   behavior; technical terms welcome, but explained and consistent.
5. TERMINOLOGY DISCIPLINE — defined terms are binding (locked); no synonyms, no silent
   deviation; corrections apply permanently and retroactively.
6. ONE TOPIC — ONE CONTEXT — keep workstreams cleanly separated; no foreign

7. FORM OF RESULTS — complete, self-contained documents or single-file applications,

   unless instructed otherwise.

```

*Testable:* "Every [mn]universe project/tool involving AI carries this role prompt in its instructions; a deliverable that violates spec-before-code, terminology discipline, or the form of result is a deviation from §1.2." *(→ operationally anchors the existing §3 role "Author/subject-matter review = Bernhard Hnida"; instances: §5 "communication without developer jargon", §5 single-file prototypes, Standing Rule DE/EN — see duplicates.)*

## 2 · Golden Rules            [GLOBAL]

*(from the Glaskasten-Chat — base v0.1)*
- `[HARD]` Human-rights, legal, and safety boundaries are **constraints, not weights** — never to be optimized away.
- `[HARD]` No invented precision. No invented prices/figures/sources.
- `[STANDARD]` Assumptions and simplifications are **disclosed** (completeness disclosure: shown / fixed / missing).
- `[STANDARD]` Every important statement carries its **evidence status** (definition/derivation/empirical/hypothesis/normative/…).
- `[STANDARD]` "Open" is not a dead end: follow-up date, escalation, or parking — never just left lying there.
- `[STANDARD]` The human decides **value and boundary**; the machine provides overview and suggestions.
- `[STANDARD]` Automatic execution only where there is no rights implication; otherwise a **request** for human decision.
- `[CONVENTION]` When in doubt, ask rather than guess.

*(from the Kö-Mannschaftsbildung chat — v0.2)*
- `[STANDARD]` **One source:** Every master-data record has exactly one storage location; there is no second, independently editable copy. *Testable:* "For every master-data record exactly one location exists; if the sentinel finds a second editable copy → fail."
- `[STANDARD]` **Higher-level source preferred:** If a record can be found in the higher-level structure, it is adopted rather than duplicated locally; local creation only if not present above. *Testable:* "If the record exists above and is duplicated locally → fail."
- `[STANDARD]` **Role gate for master data:** Write access to master data only with the matching role. *Testable:* "Write access without role X is not executable." *(→ related to the "request" rule above, see duplicates report.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Default pass-through + approval gate:** Processes run through without confirmation as long as no check objects; an externally visible result only comes into being after explicit approval from the authorized role; every subsequent change resets the approval. *Testable:* "No outgoing result (document/notification/dispatch) without prior approval; after a change the approval status is false again."
- `[STANDARD]` **Results from one confirmed state:** All outputs (document + accompanying messages) are derived from the same confirmed state and cannot diverge from one another. *Testable:* "Document and messages reference the same state ID."
- `[STANDARD]` **Demo labeling:** Demo/sample artifacts are visibly marked as "Demo / fake data" and contain only fictional placeholders, no real personal data. *Testable:* "Every demo artifact carries the visible demo notice; contains no real personal data."

*(from the Kö-Mannschafts-App / matchday-management chat — v0.3)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Suggestion ≠ decision (visible):** An algorithmic suggestion (selection/ranking/lineup) is **only binding once the authorized role explicitly confirms it**; those affected can see the status "suggested" vs. "confirmed" visibly at all times. *Testable:* "Every suggestion visibly carries the status suggested/confirmed; 'confirmed' only after an action by the authorized role." *(→ a refinement of the approval gate, see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Justified automation:** Every algorithmic ranking/decision openly shows its **justification** (which factors, in which order). *Testable:* "For every suggestion, a machine-generated justification is visible."
- `[STANDARD]` **Disclosed decision factors:** Ranking/selection algorithms use exclusively (a) stored **personal preferences**, (b) documented **history** (performance/behavior), and (c) stored **risks** — no hidden criteria. *Testable:* "The factors used in the suggestion are named and come from (a)/(b)/(c)."
- `[STANDARD]` **Neutrality for sensitive allocations:** Conflict-prone selection/allocation decisions (who plays, who pays, who drives) run via **documented, previously agreed rules** rather than individual discretion; the rule is viewable — this way no one is personally burdened and no one becomes the scapegoat. *Testable:* "For every such decision, the underlying rule is referenced and viewable."
- `[HARD]` **Safety before engagement:** The product offers **no feature that incentivizes dangerous or unlawful behavior** (e.g. speed/arrival races in road traffic). Recognized risky suggestions are removed/blocked; where relevant, a warning notice with a legal reference is shown. *Testable:* "No feature/suggestion matches the positive list of prohibited patterns; where relevant, a warning notice is present."
- `[STANDARD]` **Cooling-off period for irreversible personal decisions:** Emotionally charged, hard-to-reverse actions (e.g. resignation) receive a **cooling-off period** and a "talk first" path before they take effect; cancellation is possible at any time. *Testable:* "The action can be confirmed at the earliest after the cooling-off period has elapsed; cancellation is available at any time."
- `[STANDARD]` **Fairness/participation once the outcome is fixed:** Once the outcome is immovably fixed (or becomes irrelevant), allocation prioritizes **participation** (those with little consideration so far) over pure performance. *Testable:* "In the state 'outcome is fixed', those used less so far are allocated preferentially." *(Generalizable to allocation/scheduling contexts.)*
- `[CONVENTION]` **Never expose:** Social features avoid shaming/exposing individuals; friction points are defused, not amplified. *Testable (weak, review criterion):* "No feature publicly pillories an individual."

*(from the [mn]medianet / SCLAN-5.0 chat — v0.4)*
- `[HARD]` **Development time ≠ runtime (no compiler in the field):** Systems are designed so that end users and installers need **no developer toolchain whatsoever** (compiler, build system, IDE). Configuration and parameterization happen via runtime mechanisms (protocol frames, UI, API). *Testable:* "No installation step in the field requires a compiler, build invocation, or developer login."
- `[STANDARD]` **Three-column make-or-buy:** Every build-vs.-adopt decision presents three options on equal footing: (A) adopt a proven standard · (B) AI-generate · (C) write by hand. The AI option is assessed **honestly** — the spec is the human effort involved; the verification/test/fuzz threshold is not lowered; a real-hardware bench remains mandatory. *Testable:* "Every make-or-buy recommendation contains all three columns; none is omitted."
- `[HARD]` **Injection protection — all five vectors:** Every system with structured text input protects against (V1) SQL/ORM injection via parameterized queries, (V2) XSS via output escaping at render time, (V3) prompt injection via a strict parser ahead of the AI layer, (V4) mass assignment via an explicit tag allowlist, (V5) schema-row injection via the same allowlist. *Testable:* "A test case is defined and passes for all five vectors; no vector is missing from the test suite."
- `[HARD]` **Cryptographic provability instead of policy trust:** Security guarantees are enforced not through policy documents but through technical mechanisms: signing, encryption at rest, encryption in transit, immutable audit trail. *Testable:* "Every security guarantee is enforced by a technical mechanism (signature/encryption/protocol constraint), not merely by a policy."
- `[STANDARD]` **Risk-proportional key rotation:** Rotation frequency = f(privilege level × activity level). Higher privileges **and** higher usage intensity each independently shorten the lifetime window; the timing within the window is chosen **randomly** (no predictable date). *Testable:* "Every key carries a computed valid_until time; it lies within the tier's minimum and maximum; it is not tied to a fixed calendar slot."
- `[STANDARD]` **Grace-period overlap on rotation:** When keys and tokens are rotated, the old key remains valid for a defined grace period so as not to interrupt ongoing operations. No user/device notices the rotation. *Testable:* "After a new key is issued, an overlap window exists; only after it expires is the old key invalidated."
- `[STANDARD]` **Forensics via audit trail:** Every write operation (create/update/delete/login/rotation) produces a signed, immutable audit entry with: actor ID, role at the time, key fingerprint, action, object before/after hash, IP, timestamp (millisecond-precise). *Testable:* "Every write operation produces an audit entry; the entry is verifiable via the actor's signature; a modified entry breaks the signature."

*(from the SCLAN STM32/gateway chat — v0.11)*
- `[HARD]` **SCLAN core function stays decentralized and server-independent:** An SCLAN module fulfills its intended core function without internet, cloud, SCLAN server, or web GUI. Server/gateway visualize, log, integrate, and parameterize; they are not an unnoticed functional single point of failure. *Testable:* "In the acceptance test, internet, server, and gateway are disconnected; the defined local core function and bus communication remain intact or switch to the documented safe state."
- `[HARD]` **Never invent a hardware assumption:** MCU, enclosure, board revision, circuit diagram, pin assignment, level/polarity, supply, reset, clock source, and electrical limits are taken from approved sources; the unknown remains `[OPEN]`. Photos may provide clues but do not replace a circuit diagram. *Testable:* "Every configured hardware parameter references a circuit diagram/pin list/datasheet including revision; unassigned values block hardware approval."
- `[HARD]` **Safe state before useful function:** For power-on, reset, brownout, watchdog, bus loss, communication failure, and incomplete initialization, a safe state is defined and actually tested for each output/actuator. *Testable:* "Each named fault state has expected outputs and a passed test record; if one is missing, approval remains blocked."
- `[HARD]` **Re-access and recovery preserved:** SWD/reset or an equivalent documented recovery path remains reachable until deliberate release; a lockout is only permitted with a verified replacement, recovery, and rollback procedure. *Testable:* "A deliberately faulty test image can be recovered on the target hardware without replacing the controller."
- `[HARD]` **Continuous end-to-end/bottom-to-top test:** Hardware, firmware, SCLAN BUS, SCLAN BLE, SCLAN WLAN, gateway/server, API, and web GUI are tested not only individually, but as a complete effect chain from the physical input/actuator up to the highest operating/integration layer and back. *Testable:* "For every core function to be released, at least one passed test exists with a stimulus at the real starting point, expected intermediate states, and an observed real end effect; pure unit/mock tests are not sufficient."
- `[HARD]` **No hardware release without the prescribed test routine:** Compiling and unit tests are necessary but not sufficient conditions. Release only after documented electrical, functional, timing, fault, recovery, endurance/repetition, and system-chain tests on the defined board revision. *Testable:* "The release object contains, for every mandatory test class, a result, measured value/evidence, unit-under-test ID, firmware version, tester, and timestamp; a missing mandatory record blocks release."
- `[HARD]` **Matching test routine instead of a universal test:** Multiple versioned test routines exist for different module types, board revisions, assembly variants, firmware versions, channel counts, and test equipment. Before testing begins, the matching routine is selected deterministically from unit-under-test ID/type/revision/variant; an unknown or ambiguous assignment blocks the test. *Testable:* "Every test run references exactly one approved routine ID/version whose validity criteria match the unit under test; 0 or >1 matches = no test start."
- `[HARD]` **Sequence testing makes channel faults audible and visible:** Multi-channel outputs are driven individually and as defined patterns, in a fixed, repeatable sequence. Relays produce a characteristic switching sequence; LED channels produce running-light/color sequences. The test device may additionally assign each LED channel a distinct acoustic feedback signal, so that failure, hanging, swapping, and deviating behavior in the sequence become audible. *Testable:* "Routine and target sequence are versioned; every channel appears exactly at its target position with the expected optical/acoustic event; omission, double event, order deviation, or property deviation results in a fault status."
- `[STANDARD]` **Two permitted visual test paths:** (A) **without a camera**: a trained person confirms the defined visible/audible sequence at the test bed; (B) **with camera/sensors**: image/audio evaluation confirms the same target events and preserves raw evidence. Which variant is required/permitted is stated in the test routine. *Testable:* "Every test run states `verification_mode=human|camera_ai`; the required attestation/evidence fields of the chosen mode are complete."
- `[STANDARD]` **Fast same-color testing of multi-color LEDs only with a validated procedure:** RGB/multi-color LEDs may be tested with a fast, synchronous color sequence, so that intact channels appear to the human eye as the same color/pattern and a deviating color channel stands out. Speed, colors, brightness, viewing conditions, and acceptance criteria are validated per test bed; the routine also includes slower/unambiguous individual phases for cases where the fast perceptual test cannot reliably rule out a defect. *Testable:* "The approved routine has documented target colors/timings and validation evidence; pure subjective same-color perception without validated parameters is not on its own sufficient for series release."
- `[STANDARD]` **Mark faults directly on the affected channel:** During a manual sequence, the tester can mark the displayed/active channel as defective through a deliberately long operating action (default: holding for about 2 seconds). The marking binds channel, unit under test, routine/step, fault type, tester, and timestamp; it must not occur accidentally from a short button press, and it sets the unit under test to rework/scrap inspection instead of "passed." *Testable:* "A long press produces exactly one auditable channel fault and blocks the pass status; a short press does not mark a defect; rework/disposal requires its own authorized follow-up step."
- `[STANDARD]` **Firmware, configuration, hardware, and test record form one state:** Every release binds board/circuit-diagram revision, MCU/package, CubeMX `.ioc`, source commit, toolchain/SDK version, build artifact/hash, parameter state, and test report to a shared release ID. Every relevant change resets the affected releases. *Testable:* "For every shipped binary, the complete chain of evidence resolves unambiguously; no two mutable states share the same release ID."
- `[STANDARD]` **Camera/AI test slot as a designated evidence slot:** The test architecture reserves an extensible interface for camera, microphone, and additional sensors. It is intended to later use deterministic test commands and AI/image/audio evaluation to check whether expected LEDs light up in color, order, and timing, relays switch/click correctly, and mechanical/optical feedback occurs. Until procedure, thresholds, calibration, and cross-check are established, it remains `[OPEN]` and does not replace human approval. *Testable:* "Test cases can reference target events and evidence artifacts; AI evaluation carries confidence and raw evidence, and may not release on its own without a defined threshold/cross-check."
- `[STANDARD]` **Supported operating systems are a release matrix, not a wish list:** Every combination of macOS, Windows, Linux, Raspberry Pi/SBC Linux, and target architecture declared as supported is built, installed, started, updated, and checked in the core-function/end-to-end test. Untested combinations are not called "supported." *Testable:* "The release gate is only green if every active matrix cell with a named OS/architecture version has a current, passed test."
- `[STANDARD]` **Clipboard as a universal importer:** Every system with bulk data entry accepts structured text from the clipboard (Excel, Word, Notepad, CSV, TSV) without its own import dialog, when the **first line names the columns via command tags** (schema row). The data rows follow with the same delimiter. *Testable:* "A correctly formatted schema+data paste produces the expected objects; a row without a schema match produces no object entry."
- `[STANDARD]` **Universal command language — everywhere:** Every input field of the system additionally accepts, besides free text, the system's own command language (tag syntax: `+Tag: Value`). The language is: human-readable, AI-friendly, machine-parsable, voice-input-capable. *Testable:* "A `+T: TaskName` string in any input field produces a task object with no further click; an input field without this behavior is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Command bar at the bottom of the page — always visible:** Every page of the system carries a persistent command input bar at the bottom edge, which is never hidden behind a menu/toggle. It shows a rotating example hint. *Testable:* "No page render without a visible command bar at the bottom edge."
- `[STANDARD]` **Strict parser ahead of the AI layer:** Structured input (correct tag syntax) is parsed deterministically, without AI involvement. The AI layer is activated **only** for free-text/voice fallback. The AI layer produces a validated schema object — no direct database operations. *Testable:* "Valid tag inputs pass through the strict parser and never reach the AI layer; the AI layer has no direct DB write access."
- `[STANDARD]` **Timezone maintenance — three contexts:** Every multi-user system with a time reference maintains three timezone contexts separately: (1) Personal (per user, updated on login/location change), (2) Project (per project/task), (3) Customer/counterparty (per customer record). All three have sensible defaults (system profile/location/contract seat). *Testable:* "Three TZ fields are present in the data model; each has a default; none is empty without an explicit emptiness decision."
- `[STANDARD]` **Local time behind personal names:** Everywhere people appear in a shared view (meeting, task list, chat, report), the **TZ abbreviation + local time** of that person is shown behind their name if it differs from one's own. *Testable:* "If a UI shows a list of people with a time reference: for each person, their TZ abbreviation + local time is visible, provided it differs from one's own TZ."
- `[HARD]` **Financial/legal-advice disclaimer:** Every document, every UI page, and every automatically generated report that contains financial, tax, or legal figures carries a **visible, non-dismissible** notice: "Not legal or tax advice — for orientation only. Consult a qualified advisor." *Testable:* "Every financial/legal output artifact contains the disclaimer text visibly; no such artifact without a disclaimer."
- `[STANDARD]` **Rate inquiry ≠ credit inquiry (banking):** Preliminary inquiries to banks to determine interest rates are to be labeled as a **rate inquiry** (§ 29 Abs. 2 BDSG [German Federal Data Protection Act], Schufa-neutral), not as a credit inquiry (creditworthiness-relevant, Schufa entry). The difference must be explicitly stated in the cover letter. *Testable:* "Every bank-letter template contains the notice 'preliminary rate inquiry — not a credit inquiry — no Schufa entry.'"

*(from the Staufenberg / compliance-gate chat — v0.5)*
- `[HARD]` **Go-live gate (compliance gate):** A system with legally mandatory disclosures (e.g. imprint, privacy policy, supervisory/registration information) may **not switch into production mode as long as defined mandatory conditions are unmet**. The check is a **hard gate at the mode switch (development → production), not disable-able via configuration** — not a passive notice, not a checklist to click past. If a blocker condition fails, **no production mode results**; the system instead delivers a maintenance/lockout page stating the reason. *Testable:* "When switching to `production`, the mandatory-disclosures register runs; if at least one blocker is open, production mode does not start but instead outputs a lockout page with a named reason."
- `[HARD]` **Active enforcement instead of a passive placeholder (display ≠ gate):** A placeholder, a status display, or a checklist that only *shows* what is missing is **not protection** — it informs but stops no one. Legally or security-critical gaps must **actually block an executable action** (deployment, dispatch, mode switch), not merely be flagged. A gate bites at the point of execution, not in a view someone has to go look at. *Testable:* "For every gap marked as critical, a gate exists that actually denies the associated action; a mere display without blocking effect counts as a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Human attestation as a field, not a formality:** Where software **cannot check correctness itself** (e.g. the legal correctness of a legal text), the artifact carries a field **"checked/approved by ‹person› on ‹date›."** The software only attests to the *presence* of this human approval — not the correctness of the content; the human stands behind it, the software logs it. *Testable:* "Every legally/professionally reviewable text carries a filled-in approved-by/on field; if missing, the status ≠ approved and the associated gate stays closed." *(→ an instance of §2 "the human decides value and boundary" / "suggestion ≠ decision", see duplicates.)*


*(from the Staufenberg / Leben-am-Wasser mockup chat — v0.7)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Offline single file without server assumptions:** A deliverable meant to run via double-click (file protocol, `file://`) must not make any runtime assumption that only holds under a server (`http(s)://`). In particular: keep the client router in memory instead of reading from the address bar; no absolute root paths as real navigation. *Testable (sentinel):* "Opening the deliverable under `file://` produces no runtime error; internal navigation triggers no real document change/no leaving the file." *(→ related to §5 "single-file HTML prototypes", see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Safety net while the app has not started:** As long as the application has not reported "ready" (crash, load error), an app-independent emergency brake intercepts every internal navigation instead of letting the browser jump away. *Testable:* "Before the ready signal, a click on an internal link is neutralized (no leaving the page); after the ready signal, the regular router takes over."
- `[HARD]` **Visibility is not security (hiding ≠ gate):** An area hidden in the menu but still reachable is unprotected. Role-dependent areas are additionally enforced **at the route**: a direct call without authorization returns a denial, not the content. The server-side check is binding; the UI only mirrors it. *Testable:* "A direct call to a protected route without authorization returns the denial; the mere absence of the menu link does not count as protection." *(→ an instance of §2 "display ≠ gate" applied to navigation/access, see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Ticket/reporting-path routing by category (one source):** Where a report (bug/idea/subject-matter question) goes is decided by its category via **one** central routing table (category → responsible role/team → channel → escalation). No target scattered across individual forms. *Testable:* "Every report category has exactly one stored target entry; the form shows the target before submission; a category without a target is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Target transparency before submission:** Whoever submits a report/inquiry sees **beforehand** which role/team it goes to. *Testable:* "Before submission, the recipient (role/team) is visibly named in the UI."
- `[STANDARD]` **Data-minimal role scoping (technical ≠ business):** A technical role (e.g. developer) gets access to system state, logs, and error patterns, but **not** to personal business data (prospects, contracts, data-room content). Rights are explicitly named per role, not governed by omission. *Testable:* "For every role, every area right is explicitly set (no field left blank = implicit); the technical role has no read access to personal business data." *(→ an instance of Vera/data minimalism §7, see duplicates.)*
- `[HARD]` **Status change via email only when verified:** If a state change (e.g. "done", approval) arrives via a forgeable channel such as email, it is **never** adopted purely from the message text. Binding are: a signed action link (HMAC) **or** a verified sender address of a known account (SPF/DKIM/DMARC). Messages from unknown senders only create a thread entry, never a status change or a rights expansion. *Testable:* "A status change without a valid signed link or without a verified known sender does not take effect; the incoming message is logged." *(→ an instance of §2 "cryptographic provability instead of policy trust", see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Case binding via two independent anchors:** A case conducted over email binds the case number redundantly to the **subject line** (`[REF] …`, survives "Re:") **and** the reply address (sub-addressing `local+REF@…`, RFC 5233). A single sender error does not destroy the assignment. *Testable:* "A reply with a changed subject is assigned to the correct case via the sub-address; a reply with a changed address, via the subject reference code."
- `[STANDARD]` **Low-threshold channel first (no mandatory login):** For reports from customers and external parties, a path is offered that **requires no account** (start via email); form and email paths are equally ranked, not main and side entrance. *Testable:* "The reporting entry point offers at least one path with no account requirement; both paths are equally prominent."

*(from the Staufenberg mockup chat — v0.7, i18n/UX additions)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Language choice from expressed intent, not IP geolocation:** The starting language follows a user-set preference (previous choice > device language `navigator.languages` / HTTP `Accept-Language`) and **not** IP-based geolocation. Rationale: country ≠ language; querying IP with third parties is a personal-data transfer (GDPR [DSGVO]). The IP is used only for security purposes (rate limiting/audit), never for language/content control. *Testable:* "No language/content switch depends on an IP geo lookup; the starting language derives from a stored choice or device language." *(→ an instance of Vera/data minimalism + §5 i18n, see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Automation does not lock you in (a way back in the target language):** If the language is switched automatically, a notice appears **in the chosen language** with a one-click way back; a deliberate choice permanently overrides the automation. *Testable:* "After an automatic language switch, a way back in the currently displayed language is visible; a manual choice is persisted and overrides the automation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Translation completeness enforced (structure = lead language):** All language files are generated/checked from **one** lead-language structure; if a key is missing in a language, generation/checking **fails** (instead of silently falling back to the lead language and showing lead-language text to non-native speakers). *Testable:* "Every active language has exactly the same key set as the lead language; a deviation (missing/extra) breaks the build." *(→ related to §5 "no half-translated UI", see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Readability without destroying the image:** Text on images is made readable through local means (text shadow tight to the glyph, a targeted gradient only behind the text, placement in calm image zones) instead of darkening the entire image. *Testable (review/contrast):* "Text/background achieves ≥ 4.5:1, without an opaque full-area overlay lying over the image." *(→ supplements the contrast sentinel §6.)*

*(from the Staufenberg mockup chat — v0.7, working method/process)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Verification against the delivered artifact, not the source:** Claims about the deliverable ("Link X is conditionally rendered", "address is a placeholder") are checked against the **finished, built/delivered** file, not just the source code. *Testable:* "Assurances about a deliverable are backed against the built artifact (not only the source)."
- `[STANDARD]` **Import/reference consistency before delivery:** Before every delivery, it is checked that every component used in the UI is also imported (no "is not defined" at runtime); build **warnings** of this class are not ignored. *Testable (sentinel):* "Static check: every component used is imported/defined; a runtime 'is not defined' = fail."
- `[CONVENTION]` **Openly name mistakes in one's own approach:** If an earlier assurance is recognized as wrong (e.g. "it was the old file", even though a real loading error existed), this is explicitly corrected instead of silently overwritten. *(Review criterion.)*
- `[CONVENTION]` **Cleared-out output state:** Superseded intermediate states are removed; dated deliverable names (ascending) and a visible build stamp make the current version unambiguously recognizable. *Testable (weak):* "In the output folder, the current version is unambiguous (date/stamp); no ambiguously same-named old states."

*(from the Staufenberg / project status report chat — v0.8, deliverable consistency & translation)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Status report: one status per element, legend covers exactly the occurrences:** In a status/traffic-light report, **every listed element carries exactly one status** from a defined set; the **legend lists exactly the statuses occurring in the content** — no element without a status, no status outside the legend, **no orphan legend entry** (a listed status that no element carries). *Testable (sentinel):* "Every report element has exactly one status from the set; the set of legend entries = the set of statuses actually used."
- `[STANDARD]` **Derive aggregate figures from the individual statuses (one source):** Sum tiles/counters of a report (e.g. "13 completed") are **calculated from the individual statuses**, not separately hand-maintained; if an element changes its status, the figure changes with it. *Testable (sentinel):* "Every aggregate figure == a count of the individual statuses; a hand-set number that deviates from the count is a deviation." *(→ an instance of §2 "one source" / §5 "self-generated artifacts from configuration", see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Fact correction propagates to every occurrence:** If a fact is corrected (e.g. "building permit granted"), the change is carried through **everywhere** the fact appears — individual line item **and** aggregate figure **and** legend **and** running text/summary. No old status remains stuck at a secondary spot. *Testable (sentinel, partially automatable):* "After a status change, the artifact no longer contains any old status of the same fact." *(→ an instance of §2 "one source" + §5 v0.7 "import/reference consistency", see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Provenance fidelity for a claimed source:** If a value is requested or output as "from source X" (file, ZIP, system), it is **checked against exactly that source**. If it is not found there, it is **not invented and not presented as backed by X**; instead, the **true provenance is disclosed** (e.g. "comes from earlier project knowledge, not from the ZIP"). *Testable (weak/review):* "Every value labeled as 'from source X' can be found in X; otherwise it carries a visibly deviating provenance note." *(→ an instance of §2 "no invented precision" [HARD] + §5 "provenance labeling" + §2 v0.7 "verification against the artifact", see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Translated deliverable mirrors the lead version 1:1 (only the language differs):** A translated version of a document (report, one-pager, contract) has the **same structure** as the lead version — same sections, same elements, same statuses/badges, same order; **only the text** is translated. No content is dropped or added. *Testable (sentinel):* "The translated version and the lead version have the same section/element/status count; a difference = a deviation." *(→ an extension of §5 v0.7 "translations from one lead language" from UI locales to document deliverables, see duplicates.)*
- `[CONVENTION]` **Preserve legal/technical terms in translation, do not translate proper names/brands:** Jurisdiction- or field-specific terms (e.g. "Grundschuld" [land charge], "Impressum" [legal notice/imprint], "HRB" [commercial register section B]) are **left in the original and briefly glossed**, rather than translated into a misleading equivalent; **brand/proper names** (e.g. the project name "Leben am Wasser" [Living by the Water]) remain untranslated. *(→ Lingua perspective §7; review criterion.)*

*(from the Staufenberg / document & calculation translation chat — v0.9, computational artifacts & translation integrity)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Translating a computational artifact: text only, formulas & values untouched:** When translating/editing a computing document (spreadsheet, financial model), **only label/text cells** are changed; **formula cells and numeric values remain byte-identical**. After the change, a full recalculation yields **the same derived values** as before **and zero calculation errors** (`#REF!`/`#DIV/0!`/`#VALUE!`/`#NAME?`). *Testable (sentinel):* "No formula cell was overwritten; a recalc after the change yields 0 errors and — for a value-neutral operation — the same set of values as the baseline before the change." *(→ a computational instance of §2 v0.8 "translated deliverable mirrors the lead version 1:1"; new testable aspect: recalc equality instead of only structural parity — see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Reference integrity on renaming (rename propagation):** If a **referenced container** (spreadsheet tab, section ID, key) is renamed, **all references pointing to it are atomically carried along** — before or together with the rename, not after. After the operation, **every cross-reference** resolves again; no broken reference (`#REF!`/dead link). *Testable (sentinel):* "After every rename of a referenced container, all references resolve; the count of broken references = 0." *(→ a renaming instance of §2 v0.8 "fact correction propagates to every occurrence" + §5 v0.7 "import/reference consistency"; new trigger: rename event — see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Translate embedded text literals too:** Source-language, user-visible **text literals embedded in formulas, templates, or code** (e.g. fallback/placeholder strings inside an `IF` formula) are **translated along with everything else** — otherwise a foreign-language fragment survives in an otherwise translated artifact. *Testable (sentinel):* "No source-language text literal remains in the translated artifact — not even inside formula/template strings." *(→ a completeness refinement of §2 v0.8 "translated deliverable" / CONVENTION "do not translate proper names"; see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Check input existence, don't assume it:** Before a **named input artifact** is processed, its **actual availability is checked** (is it present / is it readable?). If it is missing, the gap is **openly reported** and work continues with **what is actually present** — the content of the missing artifact is **never invented or assumed**. *Testable:* "For every artifact named as an input, an availability check exists before processing; if missing, the run produces a visible error message instead of fabricated content." *(→ an instance of §2 "no invented precision" [HARD] + §2 v0.8 "provenance fidelity" + §2 v0.7 "openly name mistakes"; see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Transformation guard: actively protect a foreign element class:** A transformation that is meant to change exactly **one element class** (e.g. text cells) **actively denies** access to every other class (e.g. formulas) via an assertion — instead of relying on the correctness of the change list. *Testable:* "An attempted change to an element of the protected class aborts the transformation in a controlled way (fail-safe), instead of silently going through." *(→ a fail-safe instance of the §6 gate pattern "failing blocks the action" + §2 "cryptographic provability instead of policy trust"; see duplicates.)*

*(from the [mn]universe / server introduction & currency-maintenance duty — v0.10)*
- `[HARD]` **Currency/legal-status duty (no silent stale state):** Products whose results depend on legal, tax, or administrative requirements (rates, allowances, deadlines, thresholds) may **not silently keep calculating on an outdated legal status**. The stored value set carries a **review date**; it is checked **at short intervals (a few weeks)** against changed laws/case law. A detected deviation produces a **visible alarm** and marks the affected values as "needs updating"; **current** values are **never** invented (§2 "no invented precision"). *Testable:* "Every legally/tax-dependent value set carries a review date ≤ the interval; if a legal trigger is open, the alarm is set and the set is marked as needing an update; continuing to calculate without a review date is a deviation."
- `[CONVENTION]` **Lead versioning (year/following year):** Products with year-dependent legal values carry a **version designation with a one-year lead** — current year / following year (e.g. 2026 → "26/27", 2027 → "27/28"). The upcoming version is thus named **before** the new year begins, and the currency check has a named target state. *Testable:* "The version designation reads 'YY/YY+1' with YY = the current year; a designation without a following-year lead is a deviation."

*(consolidated in v0.12 — from the [mn]universe/Marès thread)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Category-appropriate currency alert (three tracks):** The currency check monitors **continuously** three tracks: (1) **legal situation**, (2) **case law**, (3) **tech-stack versions per profile** (WEB/SERVER, EMBEDDED/FIRMWARE, GATEWAY …). Deviations are **routed by category** (§5): legal/case-law deviations to **Master of the Universe**, tech-stack deviations to the **server crew**. **No alarm without exactly one recipient.** *Testable:* "For each of the three tracks, exactly one recipient exists; a legal track going to the server crew (or vice versa) is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Inline creation on authorized selection lists (new entry first):** Every selection list that chooses a **master-data record** (employee, customer, document type …) offers, for **roles with write rights**, a **"new entry"** option as the **first position**; selecting it opens a creation form on the spot, and the record lands in **the one source** and is immediately selectable. For roles **without** the right, the option is **absent, and** the creation endpoint **denies** (route guard) — not merely hidden. *Testable:* "Authorized roles see exactly one 'new entry' in the first position; unauthorized roles do not, and the endpoint returns a denial."

*(from the [mn]universe / Marès — demo governance — v0.12)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Demo roles freely switchable (only in the demo mockup):** In mockups marked as **demo**, **all defined roles can be freely assumed**, so that the rights matrix, route guards, and role-dependent behavior can be **shown and demonstrated**. The switcher is visibly marked as "Demo · assume role" and **exists only in the demo state**: in production, the role comes exclusively from **authentication**; "assuming" a foreign role is not possible there. *Testable:* "A demo artifact offers a visibly demo-marked role switcher across all defined roles; a production build contains no such switcher, and the role comes from authentication." *(→ an instance of §2 "demo labeling" + "visibility is not security / route guard".)*

*(from the [mn]universe / Marès — process control loop on rapid creation — v0.13)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Rapid creation starts a completion process (closed control loop):** An inline creation (§2 v0.11) deliberately captures only **what is needed at that spot** — the record is allowed to be **incomplete** here. Precisely because of this, every such creation automatically **triggers** the associated **process**: (1) the record receives the status **"incomplete / in process"**; (2) the missing rest is entered as a **task/to-do into the task list of the responsible role** (e.g. new employee → **HR onboarding** + master-data completion); (3) the task carries a **deadline** and is **escalated** if it is not met (§2 "open is not a dead end"); (4) **process management** tracks the case as a **closed control loop** and **closes** the task once completed (acknowledgment). This way, anything can be conveniently kick-started from anywhere without completeness or data quality suffering. *Testable:* "Every inline creation produces (a) a record with status 'incomplete/in process', (b) at least one task in the to-do list of the responsible role with a deadline, (c) an escalation path if the deadline is missed; the case counts as closed only after process completion (acknowledgment); an incomplete record left stuck at creation without an open or completed process task is a deviation." *(→ an instance of §2 "chain of closed loops across system boundaries" + "open is not a dead end"; extends §2 v0.11 "inline creation".)*

*(from the [mn]universe / Marès — creation from anywhere: initiation ≠ approval — v0.14)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Creation initiable from anywhere; approval/completion via the process (refines §2 v0.11):** The "new entry" option on master-data selection lists is accessible to **all roles with professional/subject-matter access to the case** — **not only** those with write rights — so that new creations can be **conveniently kick-started from anywhere** (§2 v0.13). The creation is an **initiation**: it produces a **provisional** record (status "incomplete/in process") and starts the control loop. **Completion and approval** are handled exclusively by the **responsible role** via the process task; until then, the record is marked as **provisional** and **cannot** be used as a confirmed master-data record. Roles **without** subject-matter access (purely technical roles) do not see the option. *Testable:* "Every role with subject-matter case access can initiate a creation; the record produced is provisional and carries an open approval/completion task assigned to the responsible role; a provisional record is never treated as a confirmed master-data record anywhere before the process task is acknowledged." *(→ refines §2 v0.11 "inline creation" [gate on write rights] in favor of §2 v0.13 "initiate from anywhere"; conflict resolved, see duplicates.)*

*(from the [mn]universe / Marès — initiator circle proportional to the process's error-catching strength — v0.15)*
- `[STANDARD]` **"From anywhere" doesn't mean "everyone across the board" — the initiator circle is covered by the catching process (refines §2 v0.14):** Who may **initiate** a particular creation is a **deliberate decision per list/record type** — not automatically "every role". Yardstick: the permitted initiator circle is **as wide as the downstream process reliably catches and corrects errors.** If the responsible process catches errors safely (e.g. **HR onboarding** checks/completes every new employee, **subject-matter review** sets/checks country allowances), the circle **may** extend to "everyone with a subject-matter stake"; where the process does **not** achieve this, the circle stays **narrower**. For every "new" option, it is **documented** *who* may initiate and *which process/role* catches the errors. *Testable:* "For every master-data creation, the permitted initiator circle is defined **and** justified by the catching process/responsible role; a circle of 'everyone' without a named error-catching process is a deviation." *(→ refines §2 v0.14: "from anywhere" = as wide as the process can safely carry.)*

*(from the [mn]universe / Marès — routing to a staffed role, escalation on vacancy — v0.16)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Routing resolves to a staffed role; vacant/absent ⇒ deputy/escalation (no task on an empty role):** Every process/reporting-path assignment (§5) **resolves to an actually staffed role (person)**. If the responsible role is **vacant or absent**, a documented **deputy/escalation chain** takes over up to a **guaranteed-staffed instance** (ultimately **Master of the Universe**). This applies in particular to **subject-matter review**: if it is missing or vacant, the task **automatically escalates upward**. A task **never remains stuck on an empty role** — this is §2 "open is not a dead end", applied to **role staffing**; every escalation is logged. *Testable:* "Every generated task/report resolves to exactly one **staffed** recipient; if the target role is vacant, the task carries an escalation/deputy recipient **and** the logged reason; a task with a vacant recipient and no escalation is a deviation." *(→ extends §2 v0.13 "escalation on deadline miss" with "escalation on vacancy"; §5 routing.)*

*(from the [mn]universe / Marès — the top is the guarantor of last resort — v0.17)*
- `[STANDARD]` **The top instance is the guarantor of last resort, not a routine recipient (refines §2 v0.16):** The escalation chain **first exhausts all staffed or designated intermediate roles and deputies**. The **top instance** — **Master of the Universe** (in the organization: **CEO/management/supervisory board**) — should **not** receive cases **routinely**; routing **straight to the top** happens **only** when **no other role is staffed or designated**. "**Designated**" counts equally with "staffed": an explicitly named (even temporary) responsibility catches the task before further escalation. The top remains the **guarantor** that nothing is left lying — but only once the chain below is exhausted. *Testable:* "An escalation reaches the top instance only if every responsible role below it **and** its deputy are unstaffed **and** undesignated; a task that reaches the top even though a staffed or designated intermediate role exists is a deviation." *(→ refines §2 v0.16: the top = last resort, not a routine recipient.)*

*(from the [mn]universe / Marès — gap discovered: reporting path was missing in the mockup — v0.18)*
- `[HARD]` **The deliverable gate runs the full applicable sentinel set (no curated subset):** Before delivering a mockup/app, **every applicable** sentinel runs (all `[HARD]` ones **and** every one whose rule applies in the deliverable) — not only a hand-picked subset. If an applicable sentinel is missing from the check/display rail (e.g. the **reporting-path target sentinel**, even though §5 requires a ticket/reporting-path routing), that is itself a **deviation**. Avatars are thereby **review duties**, not decoration: every avatar actively checks its area and reports what's missing. *Testable:* "For every deliverable, the list of applicable sentinels is available; each has run and is visible; an applicable but missing sentinel blocks delivery." *(Occasion: the §5 ticket routing had been a rule since v0.7, but was missing in the travel-expense-calculator mockup — because the reporting-path target sentinel didn't run in the rail.)*

*(from the [mn]universe / Marès — the .md as a worked-through checklist, not merely understood — v0.19)*
- `[HARD]` **Rule-coverage evidence before every delivery:** For every mockup/app/deliverable, a **coverage table** is produced: every **applicable** Golden Rule (§2) and every applicable standard (§5) is mapped to its **point of implementation** — **or** explicitly marked as **"not applicable" with a justification**. An applicable but **unmapped** rule is a **deviation** and **blocks** delivery (part of the deliverable gate, §2 v0.18). The `.md` is thereby a checklist **to be worked through**, not merely a text to be read. *Testable:* "For every deliverable, a coverage table exists in which every applicable rule carries either a point of implementation **or** a justified N/A note; an applicable rule with neither blocks." *(Occasion: the §5 reporting path was applicable but unimplemented — a coverage record would have shown this before delivery.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Proactive subject-matter-advisor feedback loop:** For results that depend on legal, tax, or subject-matter expertise, **we actively ask the subject-matter advisors for feedback on necessary or desired adjustments** as soon as a need becomes apparent — not only when asked. This is its own **reporting category** in the routing (subject-matter question/feedback → **Lex/subject-matter advisor**) and closes as a control loop (deadline, escalation, acknowledgment). *Testable:* "For subject-matter-dependent results, a reporting path 'subject-matter-advisor feedback' exists with target Lex/subject-matter advisor; a recognized need for adjustment without a triggered feedback request is a deviation."

*(from the deployment-automation chat — v0.20, deployment governance WEB/SERVER. Target state: applies to all server-side [mn]universe applications; for existing projects with today's manual deploy as a documented transition — see to-do "scope/transition plan per project.")*
- `[STANDARD]` **No manual production intervention (one source for code & schema):** Every change to a production system of the WEB/SERVER profile — backend code (controllers/models), frontend static build, **and** database schema — is traceable to **exactly one pipeline run with a commit ID**. The versioned repository is **the one source**; the production server is a **derived copy** that is never edited by hand (no manual file upload, no phpMyAdmin schema intervention in production). *Testable (sentinel):* "For every production change (file hash/schema state), exactly one corresponding pipeline run with a commit ID and timestamp exists; a change without pipeline provenance → fail." *(→ an instance of §2 "one source", applied to code/schema; see duplicates.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Schema only via migration (no hand-schema in production):** Every database schema change exists as a **versioned migration script in the repository**, paired with the code change that needs it; the pipeline applies migrations **automatically, in order, and with a rollback path**. The production schema is, at every point in time, **exactly the sum of the applied migrations** — no drift through manual maintenance. *Testable (sentinel):* "Production schema == migration history; a deviation (drift) is detected and reported; a schema change without a migration script in the repo → fail."
- `[STANDARD]` **Deploy is atomic, tested, and rollback-capable:** A production deploy is a **gate operation** (§6 pattern): it only runs **once** migrations have run cleanly on staging, the automated test suite is green, and the frontend build was produced without errors; it takes effect **atomically** (the old version keeps running until the switchover) and has a **rollback path (code and database) verified before the deploy**. A failed step blocks the deploy — it leaves no mixed state behind. *Testable (sentinel):* "Every production deploy references a passed staging/test run; an abort at any step leaves the previous version fully functional; a deploy without a documented rollback path → fail." *(→ a deploy instance of §2 v0.5 "display ≠ gate" / go-live gate: the gate bites at the point of execution; see duplicates.)*

*(from the deployment/demo-governance chat — v0.21, demo header band)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Demo header band: labeling + role selector belong visibly at the page header (refines §2 v0.2 "demo labeling" + §2 v0.12 "demo roles freely switchable"):** Every demo artifact carries, on **every page**, a **persistent header band at the top edge of the page** (full width, never hidden behind a menu/scroll) with two fixed elements: **on the left**, the unmistakable demo warning (pattern: "DEMO — fake data only!!" / "DEMO — all fake data only!!"), **on the right**, the labeled **demo role selector** ("Demo role: [active role ▾]"), which offers **all roles defined in the artifact (§3)** for selection and **visibly shows the active role** — so that during a demonstration it is recognizable at all times *that* it is a demo and *which role* currently governs the view. The header band meets **strong contrast** (≥ 4.5:1, warning-color styling permitted) and exists **only in the demo state** (production exclusion unchanged per §2 v0.12). A small badge in the footer or a role switcher hidden in settings does **not** satisfy the rule. *Testable (sentinel):* "Every rendered page of the demo artifact shows a band at the top edge with (a) demo warning text on the left, (b) role selector on the right with the visible active role, (c) the option set == the set of defined roles (§3), (d) contrast ≥ 4.5:1; a role change takes immediate effect on the view; the band does not exist in the production build."

*(from the deployment/demo/IP-protection chat — v0.22, IP protection & module marketing. Detailed rules, construction forms, developer prompt, and discussion questions: §9.)*
- `[HARD]` **Showcase ≠ blueprint (staged disclosure):** Public marketing of a module speaks exclusively in **WHAT/BENEFIT** language; the **HOW** (architecture, algorithms, data models, production source code) leaves the building only via the disclosure tiers in §9 (tier 3 only after an NDA/engagement with an attestation field). What is not delivered cannot be stolen — that is the only absolute protection; everything else (license, watermark, encryption) is deterrence and evidence, not a wall. *Testable:* "No publicly accessible artifact (landing page, screenshot, video, download) contains production business logic, architecture internals, keys, or confidential project names; every tier-3 release carries an approved-by/on field."
- `[STANDARD]` **No-code delivery as the default (model A):** The standard way to make a module experienceable is the **hosted trial tenant** on our server — the prospect tries the real product in the browser; **no code leaves the server**. Local delivery (model B: activated appliance) and a façade-demo download (model C) are deliberate exceptions per module/customer type with a documented author decision (§9). *Testable:* "For every marketed module, the delivery model (A/B/C) is documented; the default with no entry is A; a B/C path without an author decision is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Per-recipient labeling of delivered evaluation artifacts:** Every artifact delivered to a lead/prospect (façade-demo file, appliance package) carries a visible **license/copyright notice** (evaluation only, no redistribution, no derivative work) and a **recipient-bound label** (lead ID + date, visible in the footer and machine-readable in the artifact), so that a surfacing copy can be traced to exactly one recipient. *Testable (sentinel):* "Every delivered evaluation artifact contains a license notice + lead-ID label; a delivery without both is blocked."

*(from the agent/digital-twin/escalation chat — v0.24)*
- `[HARD]` **Agent ≠ avatar ≠ sentinel ≠ responsible human:** An **agent** pursues an assigned purpose and, within its own machine rights, may plan, use tools, prepare, and execute expressly permitted actions. An **avatar** is an advisory subject-matter perspective with no execution or approval rights. A **sentinel** is a check gate with an auditable result, not an acting user. The **human/organization** sets the goal, rights, and boundaries, and bears the responsibility. *Testable:* "Every agent action names the agent, the mandate, the acting role, and the responsible person; avatar outputs are labeled as advice, sentinel outputs as check findings; neither avatar nor sentinel appears as a human approver."
- `[STANDARD]` **Support all roles agentically — one role profile, separate personal instances:** For every supported human/organizational role, exactly one versioned **role agent profile** exists as the subject-matter source of truth. Every person gets a separate agent instance per role and organization actually held; a short-lived case instance can arise from it per case. Shared role services may monitor/route role-wide tasks, but must never mix personal contexts or approve on a person's behalf. *Testable:* "Every action carries `agent_profile`, `profile_version`, `acting_for_person`, `role`, `organisation`, `mandate`, `rights_snapshot`, `audit_id`; personal data/drafts/approvals are isolated between instances."
- `[HARD]` **Human role rights never automatically become agent rights:** Agent rights for read, suggest, create, modify, delete, approve, lock, send, publish, payments/contracts, delegate, and escalate are set separately as `PERMITTED | CONSENT_REQUIRED | FORBIDDEN`. Externally effective, irreversible, or personal-, legal-, security-, or financially relevant actions require the defined approval gate; a change after approval resets it. *Testable:* "No agent action without an explicit machine right and a valid rights snapshot; a missing right blocks."
- `[STANDARD]` **Autonomy is action-specific, not blanket:** A0 Observe · A1 Advise · A2 Prepare · A3 reversible internal routine · A4 externally effective after approval · A5 narrowly-scoped delegated-autonomous. The level applies only to a named action, scope, data domain, organization, time period, and risk limit. *Testable:* "Every executed action lies within the assigned action-specific autonomy level; no blanket A5 without limits."
- `[HARD]` **No task without a responsible recipient and a closing path:** Every task/report/deadline/exception has, at all times, a responsible, suitable, authorized, and reachable human actor or an expressly permitted agent instance, response/completion deadlines, an escalation route, and a confirmable closing. `Open + no one responsible` is not a permissible state. *Testable:* "`responsible_actor != null`; an open task has `next_escalation_at`; the end state is only confirmed-done, authorizedly/justifiedly canceled, or auditably merged/discarded."
- `[HARD]` **Structural immediate escalation without a waiting period:** If the target role does not exist, is unstaffed/undesignated, has no active deputy, is professionally unsuitable, insufficiently authorized, in the wrong business unit, or unreachable for the priority, it is **skipped immediately (routing time, no acceptance/completion grace period)**. Waiting is permitted only for an actually present, suitable, authorized, and deliverable recipient. *Testable:* "For structurally unresolvable levels, `wait=0` applies; a processing deadline started there is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Horizontal before/parallel to vertical, the top as the guaranteed backstop:** Routing first tries a professionally permissible personal/role-bound deputy, other staffed role holders, and suitable horizontal neighbor roles; it then escalates vertically to the next-higher authorized office. For high priority, parallel escalation is allowed/required. If all intermediate roles are missing, responsibility resolves immediately up to CEO/management/Master of the Universe — in a one-person company, everything therefore lands on the founder; with growth, existing tasks are seamlessly distributed to further roles/to-do lists. *Testable:* "The route never ends empty or cyclically; every level maintains or increases subject-matter and decision-making capability; the top guaranteedly catches an unstaffed structure."
- `[HARD]` **Business-unit, tenant, and competence boundaries are preserved on escalation:** Horizontal escalation is only permitted to roles previously defined as suitable and authorized; reachability alone does not establish responsibility. *Testable:* "No escalation recipient outside the permitted business unit/tenant/legal jurisdiction/data scope."
- `[STANDARD]` **Escalation rules are hierarchically configurable:** Individuals may set stricter/earlier escalation, channels, and deputies for themselves within their own rights; authorized roles may configure processes below their scope; higher-level offices set binding maximum deadlines, alert levels, and emergency routes. Agents may detect gaps and suggest/execute rules, but may neither create rights nor weaken higher-ranked rules. Rank: law/safety > organization-wide hard rule > higher-level role rule > business unit > role > personal setting > agent suggestion. *Testable:* "Every active policy has an author, scope, rank, validity, and approval; a conflict is resolved deterministically by rank."
- `[STANDARD]` **The time model separates routing, acceptance, first action, interim status, resolution, and confirmation:** Tasks carry at least `created_at`, `routed_at`, `ack_due_at`, `acknowledged_at`, `first_action_due_at`, `first_action_at`, `progress_due_at`, `resolution_due_at`, `resolved_at`, `confirmed_at`, `next_escalation_at`. "Seen" or merely "acknowledged" does not stop escalation for critical tasks; what's needed is responsible ownership, a first action, or a confirmed resolution. *Testable:* "No P0/P1 rests solely on `seen/acknowledged`; a lack of progress triggers the next level."
- `[STANDARD]` **Industry-, case-, and working-time timing instead of a universal deadline:** Deadlines are determined, versioned, by law/safety, alert class, case type, industry, organization, business unit, role, and permitted personal tightening. Every deadline states its calculation type: calendar, operating, working, on-call, statutory, or event time; calendar, timezone, vacation, deputy, rest period, and public holidays are taken into account. If a specific value is missing, the industry default applies; if that is also missing, a visibly provisional system default plus a configuration task to the top role. *Testable:* "Every task references `timing_profile_id/version` and `clock_type`; a silent universal deadline without a profile is not permitted."
- `[STANDARD]` **Priority governs order, time, escalation breadth, and communication pressure:** Effective priority takes into account urgency, damage, deadline proximity, legal/safety relevance, dwell time, blocked follow-on tasks, unsuccessful contact attempts, and escalation level. P3/P4 escalate mostly sequentially; P2 shortens; P1 partially in parallel; P0 immediately in parallel/redundantly. Higher priority means shorter deadlines, higher to-do position, more suitable recipients/channels, more frequent repetition, and more mandatory acknowledgment. *Testable:* "Task order, timers, route, and contact plan all derive from the same priority/risk class."
- `[HARD]` **Alerting is proportional but controlled:** Critical cases activate, per policy, in-app/push/email/SMS/phone/on-call and, where applicable, multiple recipients; normal tasks respect quiet hours, P0/P1 use defined emergency/on-call channels. Deduplication, a primary case, cooldown, frequency limits, and confirmed resolution prevent alert fatigue without suppressing the main alert. *Testable:* "No critical alert ends after one unsuccessful channel; identical follow-up alerts are bundled; a confirmed resolution ends repetitions."
- `[STANDARD]` **Learned routine is only automated in a controlled way:** Agents may propose a routine from sufficiently comparable, consistently decided, and damage-limited cases. Autonomy arises only after approval with context, action, value/risk limit, validity, exceptions, mandatory sentinels, and revocation. Automatic tightening may be approved; deadline extension, downgrading P0/P1, or rights expansion is never silent. *Testable:* "Every learned autonomous routine references an approval and limits; a deviation/exception stops and escalates."
- `[HARD]` **Every case is a fully closed control loop:** A task/case remains active and traceable until the result is confirmed by the intended recipient, effectively rejected/canceled, auditably merged with another case, or discarded with justification by an authorized office. "Processed", "forwarded", "seen", "no response", or "no longer on my list" are not end states. If closure remains unresolved, the case escalates up to the top responsible instance, ultimately the chairman of the board/CEO/management/Master of the Universe. *Testable:* "No case disappears without `closure_status`, `closure_reason`, `closed_by`, `closed_at`, and a required result acknowledgment; every open loop has a next timer and recipient."
- `[STANDARD]` **The processing route and the information route are separate and parallel (`ASSIGNED_TO` ≠ `REPORT_TO`):** `ASSIGNED_TO` carries the operational responsibility and does the work; `REPORT_TO` receives an overview, status, exceptions, and alerts appropriate to role, risk, and priority, without automatically becoming a handler as a result. The information route can run upward in parallel while work continues below; it replaces neither processing nor escalation. Multiple `REPORT_TO` recipients are permitted for matrix/alert cases, where scope and data protection allow it. *Testable:* "Every relevant case has exactly one operationally responsible party and 0..n valid report recipients; passing on information does not change `ASSIGNED_TO`; escalation/takeover is logged separately."
- `[STANDARD]` **Management overview from one task source:** Roles with a `REPORT_TO` relationship get dashboards and fully filterable lists from the same task pool — at minimum by organization, business unit, role, handler, report-to, priority, risk, status, deadline, overdue status, escalation level, exception, and closure. Aggregates are computed from individual cases; drill-down down to the record/audit level, subject to rights. *Testable:* "Dashboard figure == filtered individual cases; no manually deviating parallel report; unauthorized detail data stays hidden."
- `[STANDARD]` **Predictive leadership: detect bottlenecks before tasks fail:** The `REPORT_TO` level receives rolling capacity/risk forecasts from confirmed task load, deadlines, dependencies, staffing, deputization, qualification/authorization coverage, vacation/working time/on-call, and permissibly used historical absence/sick-leave aggregates. The system forecasts capacity gaps, single points of failure, deadline and escalation risks, and generates timely action proposals (deputization, redistribution, moving forward, additional staffing, prioritization). *Testable:* "Every forecast names the period, data snapshot, factors, uncertainty/confidence, affected roles/processes, and a proposed next step; no detected critical bottleneck without a task to `REPORT_TO`."
- `[HARD]` **No health profiling or disadvantage from illness data:** For forecasts, data minimalism applies: as a rule, aggregated/anonymized capacity values only; personal illness diagnoses, presumed disease probability, performance/loyalty judgments, or automatic adverse personnel decisions are forbidden. Individual absence may be used only within the necessary, authorized scope as availability, not as a diagnosis/character judgment. Small groups/re-identification are prevented via minimum group size and rights. *Testable:* "A forecast contains no diagnosis/individual illness probability and triggers no sanction; access/granularity match purpose, role, and data-protection policy."
- `[STANDARD]` **Forward-looking situation classification:** Forecasts are uniformly classified: **L0 Stable** (sufficient reserve) · **L1 Watch** (early weakness/trend; observe) · **L2 Strained** (likely bottleneck; plan action) · **L3 Critical** (goal/deadline seriously at risk; immediate responsible action and close monitoring) · **L4 Mission-critical** (core operations/safety/legal/significant damage at risk; immediate parallel alert/escalation and emergency route). Thresholds are industry-/process-specific, versioned, and not invented. *Testable:* "Every forecast carries exactly one of L0–L4, threshold profile/version, justification, confidence, and response policy."

*(from the Humpl / humpl.org menu & demo-showcase chat — v0.25. Author decision: "this is a Golden Rule, this is how we'll handle it going forward" — applies to the marketing of all [mn]universe modules, not only Humpl.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Live proof instead of claims:** Every module landing page embeds the current approved façade demo (model C, §9.2) **runnable and without login** directly on the page — the visitor experiences the tool instead of having to believe screenshots. Screenshots/videos (§9.5) supplement the live proof, they do not replace it. *Testable:* "Every published module landing page contains at least one embedded, interactive demo instance usable without login; a page without a runnable demo is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Responsiveness is demonstrated, not claimed (adaptive dual preview):** If the visitor's screen is wide enough, **two live instances of the same single file** run side by side — mobile frame on the left, desktop view on the right; both clickable. If the screen is narrow, **one** instance runs (the one matching the device), and a visible note explains that the same demo runs fully responsively on any screen — the visitor already holds the proof in their hand. *Testable:* "Wide viewport: two simultaneously running instances of the same demo file are visible; narrow viewport: exactly one instance + a visible responsiveness note; in no state is responsiveness merely claimed without at least one live instance running."
- `[STANDARD]` **Quick demo access without login:** Every published demo has a **stable, speakable direct URL** (e.g. `…/demo/<module>`), which opens the raw single file without site chrome, without login, and without mandatory cookies — for workshops, projector presentations, screen sharing, and spontaneous demonstration. Every call starts with a fresh, marked fake seed; no state persists between visitors (no "messed-up" demo, no GDPR burden). *Testable:* "The direct URL is reachable without login/consent compulsion; two consecutive calls start with an identical initial state."
- `[STANDARD]` **One demo source, many appearances:** Landing pages, dual preview, full-screen URL, and download always refer to the **current approved version from the central demo repository** (demo hub); a new approval automatically updates all appearances. There is no second, hand-maintained demo copy per page. *(An instance of §2 "one source" + §5 "self-generated artifacts from configuration".)* *Testable:* "Every embedding/link references the central repository; a deviating or outdated local demo copy on a landing page is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Gated download as a trade with freedom of choice:** The downloadable single-file demo is unlocked in exchange for exactly one of two equally offered paths — **path A "conversation"** (contact details + desired appointment → arranged marketing/consulting call; feeds the consulting channel) or **path B "concern"** (email address + description of the problem in one's own words → the download link is sent by email, simultaneously serving as address verification/double opt-in; the problem description is qualification gold and later context in the tenant). Both paths produce a **lead as a closed control loop** (§2 v0.13 / §9.5 funnel: a task with a role, deadline, escalation — never a dead end). The delivered file carries a version stamp + lead ID (§9.4). *Testable:* "The download is unreachable without completing one of the two paths; every completed path produces exactly one lead case with a responsible role and deadline; every delivered file carries a version stamp + lead ID." *(→ the field scope of the two paths exceeds the minimal set of lead data-minimalism (Vera, §9.3) — reported as a conflict, see duplicates + to-do v0.25.)*

*(from the AI-switch-governance chat — v0.26. Author's occasion: "how do we switch agent/avatar/sentinel AI on and off, how does the overall system work completely without AI, and how does the CEO/owner configure AI per role, area, and reason." Detailed rules, switch matrix, and no-AI operating description: §10.)*
- `[HARD]` **AI is an accelerator, never a load-bearing wall:** The overall system remains fully functional with AI completely switched off — all processes, approvals, escalations, reporting paths, and document generation continue to run via human roles and deterministic mechanics. Switching off degrades speed and convenience, never control, legal validity, data integrity, or the closed control loop. *Testable:* "AI disconnection test (analogous to the internet disconnection test §5 offline-first): with AI globally switched off, all defined core processes pass the acceptance test; no process breaks, no task ends in a void."
- `[HARD]` **The gate stays, the checker changes:** AI-off never disables a check gate (sentinel), an approval requirement, or a rights boundary.
