Deterministic sentinels are not AI and remain always active; AI-assisted sentinels fall back to their defined deterministic substitute check or human attestation (`verification_mode=human`, pattern from §5 test routines); humanly attested gates are unaffected. *Testable:* "For every AI-assisted sentinel, a fallback (deterministic or human) is stored; in the AI-off state, no mandatory gate is skipped — each instead runs with a switched checker."
- `[HARD]` **The AI switch is a rights object with a kill switch at the top:** The global AI-off/on switch lies exclusively with the top instance (CEO/management/Master of the Universe); global-off is a **kill switch** — immediately effective, no confirmation, no grace period. Area, role, and action switches are delegable along the hierarchy, but only **more restrictively** or within the limit approved from above. *Testable:* "A switching attempt outside one's own scope or beyond the limit set from above is not executable; global-off takes effect system-wide within one evaluation cycle."
- `[STANDARD]` **Effective AI state = the most restrictive level of the chain:** The effective AI state of an action results from the chain global → organization/tenant → business unit → role → action as a **minimum** (the most restrictive setting wins); a subordinate level can tighten, never open, what is closed above. The switch is not merely binary: it sets the **maximum autonomy level** per scope along the delegation ladder (off → comments → suggests/human approves → decides and informs ≙ A0–A5, §4.1). *Testable:* "effective_ai_level(action) == min(chain); no executed AI step exceeds the effective level."
- `[STANDARD]` **AI-off takes effect immediately and hands over in an orderly way:** When switching off, running agent cases stop immediately and are routed as a **handover task with a short briefing** (state, steps so far, next due step, deadline) to the responsible human role — no case ends in a void (an instance of §2 v0.24 closed control loop). *Testable:* "At the moment of switch-off, open agent cases each subsequently have exactly one handover task with a human recipient, a short briefing, and a deadline; no case without a `next_actor`."
- `[STANDARD]` **Switching off deletes nothing (hide-not-delete):** AI-off deactivates; it deletes no agent profiles, learned states, configurations, or history. Everything remains preserved and auditable, but ineffective, until authorizedly switched on again. *(An instance of §5 "module switch hide-not-delete".)* *Testable:* "After off + on again, profiles/configuration/learned states are unchanged and present; while off, none of it is executed or advanced."
- `[STANDARD]` **Switching back on is an approval act and starts conservatively (safety net):** Switching on requires the authorized role (suggestion ≠ decision); after switching back on, every affected action initially operates **one level more conservatively** (back to suggestion → human approval) until the control regime (routine sampling) has again established trust. Learned autonomous routines (A3–A5) require a renewed approval check after a longer off-duration or a context change (threshold `[OPEN]`). *Testable:* "The first N actions after switching back on run in suggestion mode; a learned routine above the threshold references a renewed approval."
- `[STANDARD]` **Every switching action carries a reason and an audit:** No AI switch without mandatory fields who/when/scope/function class/state/**reason** (categorized + free text) and, optionally, valid-until (a time-limited switch with a follow-up date). Every action produces a signed audit entry (§2 audit trail). *Testable:* "Every switch audit entry has all mandatory fields; a switching action without a reason is not executable."
- `[STANDARD]` **Visible AI status (transparency toward those affected):** The effective AI state per scope is visible to the affected roles (e.g. a status element "AI: off / comments / suggests / decides — reason, since, by role"); no one works unknowingly with or without AI involvement. *Testable:* "In every workspace, the effective AI state including reason is retrievably visible; a state change is displayed to the affected roles."

*(from the AI-Provider-Abstraction chat — v0.27. Author's occasion: "Organizations may not be allowed to use AI at all, only partially, or only certain providers [localization/jurisdiction requirements] — how do we build applications so that the AI provider is selectable, switchable, or is the organization's own AI." Detailed architecture: §10.8–§10.11.)*
- `[HARD]` **AI behind an adapter, never in business logic:** Business logic never calls a specific AI provider directly; every AI access goes through a **versioned internal AI contract** (capability classes with defined input/output schemas), behind which providers sit as interchangeable **adapters**. *(Design pattern of the SCLAN portability architecture, §5: domain core ⟷ transport adapter — here: business logic ⟷ AI-provider adapter.)* *Testable:* "An automated scan finds no direct provider call (SDK/endpoint) in the business logic; swapping a provider adapter — including the null provider (P0) — requires no change to the business logic; the business logic is testable with a test/mock adapter, without a real provider."
- `[HARD]` **Providers only from the approval register (allowlist, default deny):** An AI provider is usable only once it is listed as approved in the **provider register** for the respective jurisdiction and tenant — with hosting location/data residency, data-processing agreement (Art. 28 GDPR), certifications/regulatory role, no training on tenant data, and supported capability classes. Not registered or not approved = not callable. Approval/blocking only by the authorized role (top instance or dedicated Legal-Admin), never sales/operator. *Testable:* "Every executed AI call references a register entry with status 'approved' for tenant + jurisdiction + capability class; a call without a valid approval is not executable."
- `[HARD]` **Data-classification gate before every external AI call:** Every payload is classified before being sent (e.g. public · internal · personal · health/special categories · crown-jewels logic); it is defined per provider and jurisdiction which classes may reach it. **Crown jewels and forbidden classes never leave the organization** (only P3/local or not at all); personal content passes through a redaction/pseudonymization gate wherever mandated; **no training on tenant data without explicit, documented approval from the tenant.** *Testable:* "Every external AI call carries a payload classification; a class inadmissible for the target provider blocks the call; crown-jewel markers in an outgoing payload = failure."
- `[STANDARD]` **Provider profile per tenant (P0–P3) with resolution per scope × capability class:** Every tenant runs its AI-sourcing model — **P0** no AI (≙ §10 off-state) · **P1** shared platform provider from the register (default; per-tenant key per KMS §5) · **P2** customer's own cloud provider (customer's own contract/key, "Bring Your Own AI") · **P3** customer-owned/local AI behind the same adapter (data never leaves the organization). The effective choice is resolved, like the AI state (§2 v0.26), per **scope × capability class**, with a defined **fallback chain** (e.g. P3 → P0 rather than silently switching to P1). *Testable:* "Every tenant has exactly one active provider profile; every call deterministically resolves to a provider + fallback; a fallback never switches to a provider inadmissible for the data class/jurisdiction."
- `[STANDARD]` **Provider switching is an audited procedure with regression proof and a conservative restart:** Switching a provider (including P1→P2/P3) is done by the authorized role, passes the **golden-fixture regression** for each affected capability class (versioned test cases, analogous to bridge contracts §5), and starts conservatively (§2 v0.26 safety net: first N actions in proposal mode). No silent change in quality/behavior. *Testable:* "Every switch references the approver, the reason, passed fixture runs per capability class, and the activated restart mode."
- `[STANDARD]` **AI provenance record per call:** Every AI call logs provider, model/version, adapter version, capability class, scope, payload data class, response status, and cost unit — auditable and evaluable per tenant (cost/compliance proof). *(Counterpart to the audit record §4.1 at the call level.)* *Testable:* "No AI call without a complete provenance record; the per-tenant evaluation is derivable from the individual calls (aggregate from individual status)."

*(from the Intelligence-Tiers chat — v0.28. Author's fixed statement: guiding principle §10 verbatim "AI is an accelerator, never a load-bearing wall"; Machine Learning is **intelligent code, not intelligent agents and not artificial intelligence** — its own, configurable tier below AI, deployed as widely as possible to close control loops, learn, and code more dynamically — strong AI-like functions at a drastically smaller compliance attack surface. Detailed architecture: §10.12–§10.13.)*
- `[HARD]` **Intelligence-tier ladder I0–I3 (label follows capability, not marketing):** Every intelligent system function carries exactly one tier — **I0** deterministic rule/code (templates, arithmetic, routing tables) · **I1** machine learning (statistical models per the I1 criteria catalog) · **I2** generative AI/language models (provider abstraction §10.8–§10.11) · **I3** agentic AI (§4.1). Classification follows actual technical capability; a component with generative capability may never be declared I1 in order to bypass switches, registers, or data-classification gates. *Testable:* "Every intelligent component carries exactly one I-tier; an automated capability scan that finds generative/external AI capability in a component declared I0/I1 is a failure."
- `[HARD]` **I1 criteria catalog (what may be called 'Machine Learning' with us):** A component is only I1 if **all** criteria are met: (1) trained exclusively on the organization's/platform's own data with purpose limitation; (2) runs entirely within the platform or at the tenant — **no external data flow, no provider** (data residency trivially satisfied); (3) versioned model artifact: same model + same input = same output (reproducible); (4) **explainable** — the factors used are named and disclosed (instance of §2 v0.3 "Reasoned automation" + "Disclosed decision factors"); (5) outputs are **scores/suggestions/predictions, never autonomous actions** — effect arises only via the delegation ladder (§10.2); (6) no free-text generation, no external world knowledge, no agent planning. *Testable:* "Every I1 component demonstrates all six criteria (model governance object); a missing criterion escalates the component to I2/I3 — with all obligations that entails."
- `[HARD]` **The accelerator principle applies per tier (I0 disconnection test):** "Never a load-bearing wall" applies not only to AI but to every tier above I0: the overall system remains fully functional under pure **I0 operation** (ML and AI off) — control loops are then closed by humans and deterministic mechanics. I1 predictions/scores are a convenience, never a prerequisite. *Testable:* "I0 disconnection test: with a global off-switch set for I1–I3, all core processes pass the acceptance test (generalization of the AI disconnection test v0.26)."
- `[STANDARD]` **ML closes control loops (target expansion stage — refined by author correction v0.29: "I0 first, rule before model"):** I1 is the preferred expansion stage for making existing — already deterministically closed — control loops capable of learning and improving — forecasting (capacity/deadline risk), prioritization and routing suggestions, anomaly/duplicate detection, classification suggestions — always as a suggestion within the existing gates (suggestion ≠ decision), always with disclosed factors, always switchable off per scope. Where I1 suffices, I2/I3 is not deployed (smallest sufficient tier). *Testable:* "Every deployed intelligent function documents why its tier is the smallest sufficient one; an I2 deployment for a task solvable by I1 requires justification."
- `[STANDARD]` **Tier switch in the existing matrix:** The switch matrix (§10.2) gains the axis **maximum I-tier per scope × function class** (e.g. "Organization X: max I1 — ML yes, AI no" · "Area Y: max I0" · "Role Z: I2 only for translation"). Resolution as before: minimum of the chain; I1 can remain active when I2/I3 are off — that is exactly the tier's purpose. I1 too is switchable (e.g. co-determination for employee-data models). *Testable:* "effective_i_level(action) == min(chain); no executed function exceeds the effective tier of its scope."
- `[STANDARD]` **Tier classification does not replace legal review (Lex):** The I-tiers are **architecture and control concepts, not legal concepts**. Regulatory AI definitions (e.g. the EU AI Act) may also cover I1 systems; GDPR obligations (including automated decisions) apply regardless of the technology. The drastically smaller compliance attack surface of I1 (no provider, no data outflow, explainable) is an architectural advantage — the legal classification per jurisdiction/use case is performed by a real lawyer/data-protection officer and is attested. *Testable:* "Every productive I1/I2/I3 deployment on personal data references an attested legal classification (reviewed-by/on) per jurisdiction; 'I1 = automatically compliant' appears as a statement in no document."

*(from the author correction "Rule before Model" — v0.29. Author verbatim: "Is ML the only thing that closes control loops? Deterministic code can do that too — and I would actually prefer that, especially at the start, as long as there isn't yet enough evaluation and training data available." Corrects the overly hasty v0.28 wording "deployment default".)*
- `[HARD]` **I0 closes every control loop first (rule before model):** Every control loop is closed from day 1 by deterministic code and human roles — routing tables, thresholds, timers, approval gates. An I1 model is always an **expansion stage of a functioning I0 loop**, never its first closure; no control loop waits for a model. *Testable:* "Every defined control loop possesses a complete, model-independent I0 closure (rule set + responsible role + deadline + escalation); a control loop whose functioning presupposes an I1 model is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Data-maturity gate before I1 live operation:** An I1 model may only supplement or replace an I0 suggestion once data maturity has been demonstrated: sufficient evaluation/training data (minimum quantities per deployment pattern `[OPEN]`), a passed **shadow mode** over a defined period (model runs in parallel, has no effect, is measured against the actual human/I0 results), and quality at least at the I0 baseline — attested approval in the model governance object (§10.12). *Testable:* "Every effective I1 model references proof of data maturity, a shadow-mode report (period, metrics, comparison baseline), and an approval attestation; live operation without these three proofs cannot be activated."
- `[STANDARD]` **I0 remains the guardrail and fallback:** After an I1 model is activated, the I0 rule is not removed — it remains as a plausibility corridor (an I1 value outside the I0-plausible range is flagged and falls back to the I0 suggestion) and as an immediate fallback upon model deactivation, drift, or tier switch. *(Instance of the ladder rule §10.12 "every tier falls back in a controlled manner to the one below".)* *Testable:* "For every effective I1 model, the active I0 rule exists together with a defined plausibility corridor; deactivating the model lets the control loop continue on I0 without a transition gap."
- `[STANDARD]` **I0 operation generates the training data:** The human decisions made within the deterministically closed control loop are captured — purpose-bound and in accordance with data-protection limits (§2 v0.24) — as future evaluation/training data. "Deterministic first" is not a detour but the data-collection phase of the maturity path; the quality of later models arises from today's rule-based operation. *Testable:* "For each control loop it is defined which decision data is captured, under which purpose limitation, for later I1 maturity; capture without purpose limitation is a deviation."

*(from the Control-Loop-Lifecycle chat — v0.30. Author's fixed statements: ML needs deliberate trust extension just like AI; role changes need an AI/ML handover protocol; it must always be understood and explained what happens when control loops are opened, how they proceed, and how they are closed; Golden Rule "status change of control loops" — automations must always remain controllable; every control loop, however small, has a caretaker role; the status changes form a management layer that answers the question: "Are my processes running — how successfully, where not, where well?" Detailed architecture: §11.)*
- `[HARD]` **Trust is handed over deliberately — for ML as for AI:** No I1/I2/I3 function acts beyond proposal mode without explicit, attested **trust extension** by the responsible role for exactly this scope. Data maturity (§2 v0.29) is the necessary technical condition — the trust extension is the second, independent human condition; trust is granted, never assumed, and is revocable at any time (revocation = fallback to proposal mode, safety net §2 v0.26). *Testable:* "Every function acting beyond proposal mode references a valid trust extension (role, scope, date, attestation); revocation takes effect within one evaluation cycle."
- `[STANDARD]` **AI/ML handover protocol on role change:** Every role-assignment change automatically generates (self-generated artifact from configuration, §5) a **handover protocol** for the person taking over: which intelligent functions run within the role's scope (I-tier, models, autonomy/delegation levels, trust extensions), how they work (factors, corridors, control-loop linkage), **which parameters the role is permitted to adjust/regulate** (rights-precise) — and **do-NOT notices**, what is better left unchanged so as not to open control loops and leave them unattended. Taking over is acknowledged (attestation); **until acknowledgment, autonomous functions within the role scope run one tier more conservatively** (proposal mode — instance of the safety net). *Testable:* "Every role change references a generated protocol + acknowledgment; between the change and the acknowledgment, no function within the scope runs beyond proposal mode."
- `[HARD]` **Control loops are explained (comprehensibility obligation):** For every control loop, it is documented and explainable in a way understandable to the roles involved: **what opens it, how it proceeds** (stages, roles, deadlines, escalation), **how it is closed — and what happens if it is opened and left open.** No invisible loop, no unexplained automation. *Testable:* "Every defined control loop possesses a complete process description (opens/proceeds/closes/consequences-if-left-open); an automation without a referenced description is a deviation."
- `[HARD]` **Golden Rule "status change of control loops":** Every control-loop instance — human-, deterministic-, ML-, or AI-driven — is **at all times** controllable by the responsible role: pause, abort, delete/archive, or **declare closed by decision** (even if factually unfinished). Every status change immediately stops that loop's automations, **informs everyone involved**, carries a reason + audit (AI-switch pattern §10.4), and preserves information and learning value (hide-not-delete; deletion only per legal requirement). This keeps automations always controllable — they never run wild and never escalate pointlessly; even lower roles can stop and change status within their scope. **Paused without a follow-up date is forbidden:** every paused loop carries a follow-up date or a deliberate, attested indefinite decision. *Testable:* "Every instance carries exactly one lifecycle status; a status change stops automations within one cycle and generates stakeholder information + an audit entry with a reason; no automation acts on paused/aborted/closed loops; no paused loop without a follow-up date or an indefinite attestation."
- `[HARD]` **Every control loop has a caretaker role:** Every control loop, however small — in SCLAN, Humpl, every product and every organization — has exactly one responsible role that **nurtures, maintains, and manages** it (definition, parameters, health, status decisions). The system **prompts this role with what matters — at the right place** (in the work context, not in a distant report). There are no orphaned loops. *(Instance of "responsible role instead of person" + staffing/deputizing rules.)* *Testable:* "Every defined control loop references exactly one staffed caretaker role; a loop without a staffed caretaker role is a deviation with immediate escalation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Control-loop management layer (dampened escalation, information preserved):** The status changes of all loops feed a maintained, appealing management view for higher-level roles (`REPORT_TO`): from this it is recognizable **whether something is running smoothly or needs support** — a real management tool with immediate alerting that is itself a control loop (caretaker role, deadlines, escalation). Escalation is dampened, but information is never lost, the learning value is preserved, centrally important information is automatically passed upward (aggregate from individual status, §2 v0.24). It answers, for every loop however small — not just marketing success tracking — the question: **"Are my processes running? How successfully, where not, where well?"** *Testable:* "Every status change appears in the responsible `REPORT_TO` view; defined alert thresholds trigger immediate tasks; no status event without an aggregation path upward."

*(from the Governance-Layers chat — v0.31. Author's mandate: "FIRST 1, 2, and 5" — data governance, dependency governance, emergency governance. All three built on the same seven-part pattern that shaped §10/§11: lifecycle · caretaker role · switch/tiers · register · disconnection test · handover/trust · management view. Detailed architecture: §12–§14.)*

**Data Governance (§12):**
- `[HARD]` **No ownerless data:** Every data object carries a data class (§2 v0.27), exactly one staffed **data caretaker role** (data steward), and a lifecycle status (collected → active/confirmed → archived → deleted). A data object without a class, steward, or status is a deviation. *Testable:* "Every data-object type references a class + staffed steward role + status model; orphaned types escalate immediately."
- `[HARD]` **Deletion is a control loop, not a hope:** Retention and deletion rules are set per data class (legal basis via Lex, nothing invented); expiry automatically generates a deletion/archiving task for the steward. **Data-subject rights** (access, rectification, erasure, portability) are standard control loops with statutory deadlines as timers — no case-by-case panic. *Testable:* "Every data class references retention/deletion + legal basis; every data-subject request opens a deadline control loop; no expiry without a generated task."
- `[STANDARD]` **Data freshness instead of silent obsolescence:** Master-data records carry a confirmation date; if unconfirmed beyond the freshness threshold (`[OPEN]` per type), they are visibly flagged as "possibly outdated" and generate a review task for the steward — data without a freshness rule is the date-counterpart to "paused without a follow-up date". *Testable:* "Every master-data type has a freshness threshold; exceeding it generates a flag + task."
- `[STANDARD]` **Purpose limitation on the object:** The purpose of collection is recorded on the data object; use outside the purpose is blocked or subject to approval (generalizes the training-data purpose limitation §2 v0.29 and the data-classification gate §2 v0.27 to all uses). *Testable:* "Every type of use of an object is checked against the recorded purpose; use outside the purpose without approval is not executable."

**Dependency Governance (§13):**
- `[HARD]` **One dependency register for everything external:** Every external dependency — hosting, payment, DATEV, certificates, external libraries/services, AI providers — resides in **one** register (design pattern §10.9; the AI-provider register is henceforth a special-case view of it, not a second register). Unregistered = not usable in production (default deny). Every dependency has a caretaker role, a criticality tier, and a review date. *Testable:* "Every dependency used in production references exactly one register entry with a caretaker role, criticality, and review; an automated inventory scan finds no unregistered use."
- `[HARD]` **Vendor disconnection test for every critical dependency:** For every dependency of criticality K2 or above, the question "What happens if X fails?" is answered and proven with a **defined degradation** — the third instance of the disconnection-test pattern (internet §5, AI §10, vendor §13). *Testable:* "Every K2/K3 dependency references a documented, tested degradation path; K3 additionally an exit/switch path."
- `[STANDARD]` **Expirations are timers, not surprises:** Contract endings, certificate, and key expirations automatically generate follow-up tasks for the caretaker role, in good time before expiry (lead time per criticality `[OPEN]`). *Testable:* "No expiry date in the register without a generated lead-time task; an expired entry without a prior task is a sentinel failure."

**Emergency Governance (§14):**
- `[HARD]` **The rehearsed emergency:** Disconnection tests are not just acceptance checks but **recurring, real exercises** (AI off, internet off, vendor simulated as failed) with a measured result; every exercise generates finding tasks; an overdue exercise escalates like a missed follow-up date. *Testable:* "Every critical degradation has an exercise calendar; every exercise leaves behind a measurement result + finding tasks; overdue exercises generate escalation."
- `[HARD]` **Incident lifecycle with a learning obligation:** Every incident passes through reported → contained → resolved → **learned** (role, deadline, escalation per stage — status model analogous to §11.1); **no incident is closed without a learning entry** (what, why, which rule/sentinel/model is being adjusted). *Testable:* "Every incident instance carries the status model; closure without a referenced learning entry is not executable."
- `[STANDARD]` **The emergency plan works without the system:** Emergency roles, deputies, reachability, and first measures are named in advance and **available outside the system** (exported/printed, kept current via follow-up dates) — the plan for "system off" must not exist only inside the system (offline-first applied to the emergency itself). *Testable:* "The emergency plan exists as a current offline artifact with a date stamp; its freshness control loop runs; the emergency roles are staffed."

*(from the Identity-Governance chat — v0.32, candidate 3 of the layers list. Completes the v0.30 thread: the mover has their handover protocol — now joiners and leavers get their control loops. Detailed architecture: §15.)*
- `[HARD]` **Identity lifecycle (Joiner–Mover–Leaver):** Every identity carries exactly one status (joining → active → transferring → leaving → deactivated/archived); **rights exist only bound to an active identity with a valid role assignment.** On the departure date, all rights end automatically; accounts are deactivated, never deleted (audit/records remain — hide-not-delete). *Testable:* "No effective right without an active identity + valid assignment; no right survives the departure date; deactivated accounts are unable to log in but remain auditable."
- `[HARD]` **No deactivation with ownership (orphaning lock):** An identity can only be deactivated once all of its caretaker roles (§11), data stewardships (§12), dependency responsibilities (§13), and open control-loop instances have been **demonstrably handed over** — offboarding automatically generates the handover tasks (checklist as a self-generated artifact, §5). *Testable:* "A deactivation attempt with existing ownership is not executable; every offboarding references the complete, acknowledged handover list."
- `[HARD]` **Recertification — rights lapse if nobody confirms them:** Right assignments are periodically (interval per right criticality `[OPEN]`) confirmed by the management role; unconfirmed rights are first conservatively restricted, then withdrawn. *(Fifth instance of "nothing ages unobserved".)* *Testable:* "Every right assignment carries a confirmation date; overdue status generates restriction + a task; continued overdue status generates withdrawal + escalation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Rights diff instead of accumulation (mover hygiene):** Every role change is an explicit diff — what comes, what goes — and old rights end when the new ones begin; nobody accumulates rights across stations. Temporary rights (deputizing, project) always carry an expiry date. *(Supplements the handover protocol §11.2 with the rights side.)* *Testable:* "Every change references a diff; no right without a source in the current assignment; no temporary right without an expiry."
- `[STANDARD]` **Staged onboarding (joiner):** Rights take effect in stages upon joining — each stage against an acknowledgment (training, acknowledgment of the handover protocol, briefing by the sponsor role); full effective scope only after the onboarding control loop is completed. *(Instance of the safety net: new = conservative.)* *Testable:* "Every activation stage references its acknowledgment; no full rights without a completed onboarding loop."
- `[STANDARD]` **Emergency access (break-glass) as an audited control loop:** Predefined, time-limited emergency access exists for named scenarios; every use is fully audited, immediately informs the management role, and automatically opens an incident review (§14.2). *Testable:* "Every break-glass use generates audit + immediate information + a review instance; no emergency access without a time limit."

*(from the Layers-Completion chat — v0.33, author's mandate "the missing ones": candidates 4, 6, 7, 8. Detailed architecture: §16–§19.)*

**Budget/Cost Governance (§16):**
- `[HARD]` **Budget switch with staged degradation:** Every cost-incurring automation is bound to a budget per scope and degrades in **stages** upon exhaustion — normal → throttled → proposal-mode-only → off — rather than running unbounded or dying abruptly; costs come from the existing provenance records/individual events (§2 v0.27), not from a second set of books. *Testable:* "Every cost-incurring automation references a budget; crossing a threshold demonstrably switches to the next degradation stage; no automation without a budget binding incurs ongoing costs."
- `[STANDARD]` **Budget overrun is a control loop:** Exhaustion generates a decision task for the budget caretaker role (increase · leave throttled · switch off) with a deadline — a deliberate decision instead of silent continuation; the burn-rate indicator per scope appears in the management view. *Testable:* "Every exhaustion references exactly one decision task; no budget increase without an approval act + reason + audit."

**Reporting/Whistleblower Governance (§17):**
- `[HARD]` **The protected reporting channel is a walled-off control loop:** Reports (including anonymous ones) open a control loop with its own, walled-off **confidentiality role** as caretaker role; statutory deadlines (acknowledgment of receipt, feedback — concrete values per jurisdiction via Lex, nothing invented) run as timers with escalation confined within the wall. *Testable:* "Every report opens a loop instance with deadline timers; access exclusively for the confidentiality role; escalation never leaves the walled-off area."
- `[HARD]` **Wall architectural, not organizational:** Reporting data is its own data class (§12) accessible only to the confidentiality role; there is **no** link between a report and a person visible to management or `REPORT_TO`; the management view receives only k-anonymous aggregates (count, categories, throughput times — the anonymization pattern of benchmarking, including automatic widening of small groups). *Testable:* "No non-confidentiality access path to raw report data exists; every aggregate output passes the k-anonymity check; a re-identification test (differencing) fails."

**Knowledge Governance (§18):**
- `[HARD]` **Knowledge ages visibly:** Every knowledge artifact (guide, process/procedure description §11.3, template, training material — and the master itself) carries a caretaker role, a date stamp, and a review interval; overdue = a visible "possibly outdated" flag + a review task. *(Sixth instance of "nothing ages unobserved".)* *Testable:* "Every registered artifact has a role + date + interval; overdue status generates a flag + task; unflagged outdated knowledge is a failure."
- `[STANDARD]` **Reference instead of copy, generation instead of manual upkeep:** Knowledge is, where possible, generated from configuration (§5 self-generated artifacts) and otherwise maintained exactly once and referenced — copies age individually and contradict "one source". Knowledge gaps (from incidents §14.4, handovers §11.2, onboarding §15.2) are tasks within the control loop, not silent wishes. *Testable:* "No mandatory content exists as a separately maintained copy; identified gaps reference a task with role + deadline."

**External-Voices Governance (§19):**
- `[HARD]` **Every external voice opens a control loop:** Complaint, support ticket, feedback, review, reason for termination — every voice from outside becomes a loop instance with a caretaker role, deadline, and lifecycle status (§11); nothing falls through the cracks. *Testable:* "Every captured external voice references exactly one loop instance; orphaned or deadline-free voices are a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Every complaint is a learning datum:** Closure only with a categorized learning entry (pattern §14.2 "learned"); the anonymized learning entries feed product to-dos, the maturity path (§10.13), and the management view. **The loop also closes outward:** the sender learns what became of their voice (feedback per channel and reasonableness). *Testable:* "No closure without a learning entry; every instance documents the sender feedback or the deliberate, justified waiver thereof."

*(from the Bilingualism chat — v0.34. Occasion: an English-speaking development team needs an EN version of the master.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Bilingualism rule for the master (DE = lead version, EN = derived artifact):** The universemaster is maintained exclusively in the **German lead version** — it is the one source (§2 "One source"). The English version `universemaster_EN.md` is a **generated, derived translation artifact**: it carries in its header (a) the **precedence note** ("The German version prevails"), (b) the **source version stamp** (which DE version it was generated from), (c) the **binding terminology map** DE→EN, and (d) the **explicit statement that it is the translation of the German master document**; not-yet-translated parts are marked as declared stubs referencing the master (instance of §2 v0.8 "Translated deliverable mirrors the lead version 1:1" — deviations only as declared stubs). The EN version is **regenerated with every new DE version, never edited independently** (§18 "Reference instead of copy / generation instead of manual upkeep"); a substantive change always begins in the German master. *Testable:* "The EN version carries the precedence note, source version stamp == current DE version (otherwise visibly flagged as outdated), terminology map, and derivation statement; every untranslated section is declared as a stub; an EN edit without a corresponding DE change is a deviation."

*(from the Developer-Roles-&-Migration chat — v0.35. Author's clarification: the developer role is responsible for the application control loops; the server crew is responsible only for operating the server, not for functions of Humpl and other applications. Author's mandate: rule coverage must be reflected as documented status in the project documentation (overview open/done, CPM/Kanban tracking); first test, understand, and use the team's own existing tools for to-dos/Kanban; migrate these over time to the version updated per this master and thereby run first tests in the real lab — migration from old to new.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Function vs. operations responsibility (developer ≠ server crew):** The **developer role** is the caretaker role (§11) for the **application control loops and functions** per product, as well as for the functional dependencies (business interfaces, §13). The **server crew** is responsible exclusively for **operations** — server, infrastructure, stack, pipeline operations, infrastructure dependencies (hosting, infrastructure certificates) — and **never** for application functions. *Testable:* "No application control loop references the server crew as caretaker role; every application control loop references a developer/business caretaker role; infrastructure items reference the server crew."
- `[STANDARD]` **Rule coverage as a living project status (CPM/Kanban-capable):** The rule-coverage proof (§2 v0.19) is not only a proof per deliverable but is maintained as a **continuous status in the project documentation**: exactly **one living coverage board** per product, on which every applicable rule/implementation package sits as a card — with status (open · in progress · done · n/a with justification), the responsible developer role, a deadline, and **dependency links** (for critical-path consideration). This way, during the development period we have a concrete overview of what is open and done, and can track planning, implementation, and completion CPM/Kanban-style. Metrics are an aggregate from the card statuses (§2 v0.8), never manually maintained alongside. *Testable:* "Exactly one coverage board exists per product; every applicable rule has exactly one card with status, responsible party, and deadline; dashboard figure == card count; an applicable rule without a card is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Master acknowledgment of the developer role (onboarding stage):** Every person in the developer role acknowledges awareness of the master rules applicable to their work (EN version permissible; German governs, §2 v0.34) as a mandatory stage of onboarding (§15.2); no acknowledgment, no write/approval-rights stage. *Testable:* "Every active developer assignment references a master acknowledgment with a version stamp; a new master main version generates a re-acknowledgment task."
- `[STANDARD]` **Own tools first (dogfooding obligation):** For to-do/Kanban planning, the team's **own, already existing tools** are tested, understood, and used first — before foreign ones are procured or new ones are built. Usage is at the same time the **real lab**: identified gaps and friction flow as learning/external-voice entries (§14.4/§19) into further development. Which tools exist: `[OPEN]` — inventory them, invent nothing. *Testable:* "Before procuring/building a new planning tool, a documented test of the existing inventory exists; every use of the inventory generates learning entries."
- `[STANDARD]` **Migration old→new as a guided path (real lab instead of a hard cut):** Existing tools and applications are gradually brought up to the standard of this master: **(1) actual use as real lab** — the team's own tool is used in production and thereby checked against the master (gap list = cards on the coverage board); **(2) migration cards** with priority and dependencies plan the takeover; **(3) adoption into the updated version as soon as reasonably possible**, with first tests in real operation; **(4) the old state remains fully operational until the takeover is confirmed** (no hard cut; hide-not-delete; a way back stays open — pattern §10.6/§2 v0.29 "prove it before it takes effect"). *Testable:* "Every existing tool under migration has a gap list + migration cards on the board; the new version moves into leading use only after a confirmed real-lab test; the old state remains operational until then and the way back is documented."

*(from the Pair-Generation chat — v0.36. Author's instruction: "from now on always generate the DE and EN versions together and always check for consistency." Responsibilities by category discipline: the automated gate = EN derivation sentinel (§6), content quality = avatar Lingua (§7) with human attestation.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Pair generation DE+EN with a consistency gate:** Every new German master version generates the English version **in the same approval step** (pair generation — no DE approval without a jointly generated EN version). Before the **pair approval**, two checks run: **(a) automated** the EN derivation sentinel (§6) — precedence note, source version stamp == DE version, terminology map, derivation statement, declared stubs, **structural parity** (same number of sections/rules/status tags per translated area, §2 v0.8); **(b) content-wise** the avatar **Lingua** (§7) for the materially changed sections — fidelity of meaning and terminology, attested as "reviewed by ‹person› on ‹date›" on the EN artifact (§2 v0.5: the software only attests to the presence of the human review; Lingua is advisory, never the approver, never the gate). **Alarm on a finding (closed control loop):** a failure blocks the pair approval; the DE version remains usable, the EN version is visibly marked as **"outdated / not approved"** (no silent stale version); the finding generates **exactly one P2 task with a deadline and short finding summary** to the master's caretaker role; if unaddressed, the standard escalation chain applies (deputy → management → top as guarantor); the event appears as a health signal in the management view (§11.4/§18). *Testable:* "For every DE version there exists an EN version generated in the same step with a green derivation sentinel and Lingua attestation of the changed sections; if either is missing, the EN version is visibly marked as outdated/not approved and exactly one finding task with a deadline is open; an unmarked outdated EN version is a deviation."

*(from the Freshness-Anchor chat — v0.37. Author's template from an older project: a QR code on paper/PDF that triggers a verification link on the team's own server — "check the document's currency by scanning"; underneath, the wish for role-/template-level control (mandatory · deliberately omittable · self-addable) and the insight that scans show which documents are actively used. Classification: this is "nothing ages unobserved" applied to the analog world — the QR code is the paper's derivation sentinel.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Freshness anchor on outgoing documents (paper freshness):** Documents that leave the system (print, PDF, dispatch) carry, per template policy, a **freshness anchor**: a QR code at the start and/or end of the document with a visible caption ("Check the currency of this document by scanning"), encoding **document ID + version/date ID** (§2 v0.2 "results from a confirmed state") and leading to a **public verification endpoint**. The endpoint responds exclusively with status — "current" or "a newer version exists (version, date)" — **never with content** (§2 v0.22 showcase ≠ blueprint); access without login/mandatory cookies (low-threshold, §2 v0.7). **Honest degradation:** if the endpoint is unreachable, the response is "verification currently not possible — refer to the date on the document" — never a false "current". *Testable:* "Every anchor resolves to document ID + version; the endpoint response contains exclusively status/version/date, no document content; the unreachable case delivers the degradation response instead of a currency verdict."
- `[STANDARD]` **Template policy per document type (mandatory · optional · free), role-dependent:** Template management defines per document type: **MANDATORY** — the generation gate refuses output without the anchor (display ≠ gate); **OPTIONAL** — authorized roles may **deliberately omit** the anchor (documented decision with role + reason); **FREE** — roles may add the anchor themselves. Document types with legal/tax/deadline implications (§2 v0.10 currency obligation) are candidates for MANDATORY. *Testable:* "Every document type carries exactly one anchor policy; a MANDATORY document cannot be generated without an anchor; every deliberate omission references role + reason; policy changes only by the authorized role with audit."
- `[STANDARD]` **Scan telemetry data-minimal (anonymous by default):** A scan event by default captures only **document ID, checked version, timestamp, verification result** — **no personal reference**; the IP is used exclusively for security purposes (§2 v0.7). Personally identifiable usage signals only in an authenticated context with purpose limitation on the data object (§12); anything beyond that: Lex review, nothing assumed. The anonymous usage signals are legitimate operational knowledge: they feed the knowledge management view (§18.3 "most-used artifacts"), and **frequently checked documents receive shorter review intervals** (freshness prioritization; I1 candidate on the maturity path — phase 0 starts as a simple counting rule). *Testable:* "A default scan event contains no personal field; person-bound capture references authentication + purpose; the review-interval prioritization is traceably derived from the count values."

*(from the Interplay chat — v0.38. New chapter §20: the **human** and the five actor types (roles · agents · digital role twins · avatars · sentinels) — synergies & coexistence including human staffing. Mnemonic (v0.39): **The human is sovereign — the role is accountable, the agent acts, the twin personalizes, the avatar advises, the sentinel blocks.** The avatar ⟷ sentinel relationship is n:m — an avatar can represent several gates (Tekton: six), a gate can embody several perspectives (AI-data-flow sentinel: Vera + Lex).)*
- `[STANDARD]` **No gate without care, no perspective without a bearer (§20.5):** Every sentinel references a staffed caretaker role (application sentinels → developer role, infrastructure sentinels → server crew, §2 v0.35); every avatar references its current bearer mode (AI-assisted / human-played) and the person who attests its checks. *Testable:* "No sentinel without a caretaker-role reference; no avatar attestation without a person + date; an orphaned gate escalates like an orphaned control loop (§11)."
- `[STANDARD]` **One person, many hats, clear hat changes (§20.5):** One person may hold several roles and play several avatars — but for each action it is visible **in which capacity** they are acting (role, avatar played, attestation); self-approval of one's own work on the same matter follows the existing four-eyes/attestation rules. *Testable:* "Every approval/attestation names the person + capacity; an action without a stated capacity is a deviation."

*(from the Avatar-Intelligence/Distillation/Monetization/Communication chat — v0.40. Four related threads: where avatar intelligence lives (§20.8) · how ML learns faster through AI (§10.14) · what intelligence costs and yields (§16.4/16.5) · how outgoing communication is checked (§5 design pattern).)*

**Avatar Perspective Profile (§20.8):**
- `[STANDARD]` **Avatar intelligence lives in the versioned perspective profile, not in the model:** Exactly one versioned profile exists per avatar (embodied rules · review questions/checklist · applicability triggers · represented sentinels · known failure patterns · bearer mode + attesting person · learning inflow · version + approval). **When an avatar must be concerned is decided by the applicability trigger (an I0 rule, set by a human) or a red gate of its representation — never the model, never ad hoc.** The bearer (I2 or human) merely *speaks* the profile; when AI is off, the human checklist simply is the profile itself — the perspective loses nothing but speed. New review questions arise exclusively as a new profile version with approval (never silent learning). *Testable:* "Every avatar references exactly one profile with all mandatory fields; every avatar appearance references a trigger or a gate finding; a profile change without version + approval is a deviation."

**Teacher-Student Distillation (§10.14) — AI teaches, ML works:**
- `[STANDARD]` **Distillation is a training-time relationship, not a runtime tier:** I2 may accelerate I1's data maturity (pre-labeling existing data as **suggestions**, humanly confirmed under a sampling regime) — at runtime of a completed capability, **only I1 runs**; no intermediate tier "I1.5" (label follows capability). The data-maturity gate and shadow mode (§2 v0.29) remain untouched: distillation accelerates data collection, never the approval. *Testable:* "In the runtime path of an I1 capability marked as complete, no I2 call occurs; every distillation project references a data-maturity gate + shadow report just like every I1 model."
- `[STANDARD]` **Label provenance in the model governance object:** Every training datum carries its provenance (human · I2-suggested+confirmed · I2-unconfirmed); unconfirmed teacher labels only with a quota and a flag, never as a majority; the data-classification gate also applies to teaching (sensitive existing data never goes to external teachers — P3-local or human only). *Testable:* "`label_provenance[]` and `teacher_ref` are mandatory fields; a quota violation or a teaching call with an inadmissible data class = failure."
- `[STANDARD]` **Training budget with a zero-token completion goal:** Every distillation project has a time-limited training budget (§16) and defines from the outset the completion goal of **zero runtime tokens for this capability**; retraining upon drift is a bounded budget event, never a creeping permanent return to I2. *Testable:* "Tokens per capability demonstrably decrease over the maturity path; a completed capability with sustained runtime token consumption is a deviation."

**Decision Economics (§16.4/16.5):**
- `[CONVENTION]` **Cost mnemonic:** *Tokens are only incurred where free-form speech happens.* Everything structured (triggers, gates, classification, prediction, routing, templates) is I0/I1 and token-free; the strict parser ahead of the AI layer (v0.12) is literally the cost brake; "smallest sufficient tier" (v0.28) is at the same time the savings rule.
- `[HARD]` **Never sold is "the decision" as a legal act:** What is monetized is decision *preparation* (I1/I2 units, always a suggestion) and delegated *routine execution* within humanly approved limits (trust extension §2 v0.30) — the binding act remains with the human (Golden Rule v0.1/Humpl base rule). Which decision types may even be offered as delegated units at all is decided, per type, by an attested Lex review (HR = high risk: GDPR Art. 22, EU AI Act employment category; §2 v0.24 "no automatic adverse HR decisions" is non-sellable). *Testable:* "No sold unit type without a Lex-approval reference per jurisdiction; no delegated unit outside a valid trust extension from the customer."
- `[STANDARD]` **Billing = aggregate from provenance records (per cfg):** Sold units (suggestion/score I1 as a quota per plan/headroom · comment/review I2 measured per call · delegated routine decision I3/A3+ per execution) are billed exclusively from the existing provenance records — every invoice line item auditable down to the individual call; the customer's switch matrix is at the same time their price configurator ("you pay for what is delegated"); budget degradation (§16.2) is the built-in customer-protection cap; the **distillation dividend** (I2→I1 drives marginal cost toward zero) is shared (price drops, margin remains). *Testable:* "Every invoice line item == aggregate of referenced individual records; no unit consumption without a record; no billing beyond the configured cap."

**Communication Review Tracks (§5 design pattern):**
- `[STANDARD]` **Review profile per communication template:** Every business communication leaving the system (letter, email, quote, report) runs through its template's **review profile**: per review dimension (spelling/grammar · corporate writing style · legal · technical/domain · security/data classes · mandatory disclosures) exactly one of the states **MANDATORY** (gate — blocks dispatch), **COMMENT** (advice, never blocks), or **OFF**. "OFF for everything = free-form design, unreviewed" is a **documented role permission**, not a default; likewise, "only grammar + spelling, nothing else" is a valid configured state. *Testable:* "Every outgoing template references exactly one review profile; MANDATORY dimensions have demonstrably run before dispatch (approval gate v0.2); a full-OFF references the role permission."
- `[STANDARD]` **Locked-down review prompts as versioned approval objects:** I2 reviews run exclusively through **prompts stored in the template system, versioned and approved** (per dimension: security, legal, style, domain review) — never through free ad-hoc prompts in the review path. A locked-down prompt is the configuration of a capability class (§10.8) with golden fixtures; a change = a new version + approval. *Testable:* "Every I2 review call references a prompt ID + version with approval; a review call without a registered prompt is not executable."
- `[STANDARD]` **Tiered economics of reviews (ML handles most of it):** Spelling, grammar, and **the organization's own writing style** run as I1 — an own spelling/style database as a maintained knowledge artifact (§18: caretaker role, freshness, version), token-free, distillable (§10.14: the style is learned once by I2, then watched over by I1); only dimensions that require free understanding (law, complex domain expertise) use I2 via locked-down prompts — budgeted (§16). AI-co-written texts go through the same track; the **draft provenance** (human · AI · mixed) is recorded on the artifact. *Testable:* "Spelling/style reviews generate no token records; the style database has a caretaker role + date; every reviewed artifact carries the draft provenance."
- `[STANDARD]` **Conflict resolution by the higher authority:** If review voices contradict each other, or a review voice contradicts the author role, a **conflict case** is created for the next higher responsible authority (chain-of-command seam/escalation chain) — unless the role holds the documented free-design permission for this template. Comments never block (only MANDATORY dimensions are gates, suggestion ≠ decision); every conflict outcome becomes a learning datum (style/review sharpness learns). *Testable:* "Every unresolved contradiction at a MANDATORY gate references a conflict case with a higher authority and a deadline; dispatch past a red MANDATORY gate without an authority decision is not executable."

*(from the Universe-Mail chat — v0.41. Author's mandate: the Universe must offer its own mail software, online and offline, that observes all Universe rules. Detailed architecture: §21.)*
- `[HARD]` **Mail is a channel, not storage:** Business-relevant matters live as control-loop instances (§11/§19) with a caretaker role, deadline, and status — **never only in the mailbox**. Incoming mails are bound to their case via case-binding (§2 v0.7: `[REF]` subject + sub-addressing); unbound business-relevant mails generate an assignment task. A case that exists only in a mailbox is a deviation. *Testable:* "Every case-related mail references a loop instance; the assignment queue is a control loop with a deadline; no case is without a `next_actor` just because it arrived by mail."
- `[STANDARD]` **Universe-Mail is a full-fledged Universe application — all rules apply:** outgoing mails run through the **communication review track** (v0.40, including the recipient-language rule, draft provenance, approval gate v0.2); the **mandatory signature** (business mandatory disclosures per jurisdiction — content via Lex, nothing invented) is a MANDATORY element of the track; incoming status is **never adopted unverified** (§2 v0.7 [HARD]: signed action link or verified sender); AI/ML in mail (triage suggestion I1 · reply draft I2 via locked-down prompts) obeys the switch matrix, the data-classification gate, and the budget — **mail content reaches external AI only through the data-classification gate**; encryption at rest + in transit (v0.4) with tenant keys. *Testable:* "No dispatch without a completed review track + signature element; no unverified status flip; no external AI call on mail content without a classification record."
- `[STANDARD]` **Offline mail with an honest queue:** Universe-Mail works fully offline (reading/searching the local store, composing, review-track I0/I1 dimensions locally); outgoing items sit in a **visible outbox queue** with a status per mail (waiting · sent · acknowledged · failed→task), dispatch upon reconnection as a closed control loop (no silently lost mail); I2 review dimensions degrade offline per gate rule (MANDATORY-I2 → human attestation or dispatch waits — documented per profile). The internet disconnection test applies to Universe-Mail just as to SCLAN. *Testable:* "In the disconnection test, reading/composing/queuing are fully functional; every queued mail reaches an end state with an acknowledgment or a task; no mail disappears between states."
- `[STANDARD]` **Mail retention is a §12 control loop:** Business mails are data objects with a class, a steward, and a retention/deletion rule (jurisdiction obligations such as commercial/tax law via Lex — nothing invented); deletion/archiving runs as a control loop, never as a mailbox cleanup action. *Testable:* "Every business mail carries a data class + retention-rule reference; expiry generates the task; manual deletion outside the rule is not executable."

*(from the Ledger/Table/App-Register chat — v0.42. Author's questions: "Do we already have table/filter rules in the Golden Rules? The ledger has to reach our standard. And how do we get an overview of all the apps we've started — an app list in the master or maintained in Humpl?" Finding: task filter lists (v0.24), clipboard import (v0.12), and calculation protection (v0.9) existed; the generic table design pattern, ledger integrity, and product register were missing. Answer to the list question: **the rule goes in the master, the list goes in its own tool** — the master is a rulebook, not an aging inventory (§18 reference instead of copy; dogfooding v0.35).)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Table/list design pattern (every data table in the Universe):** Every list/table view offers at least: **column choice + order** per user · **sorting** per column · **filter per column** + full-text search · **saved, named filter views** (personal; shareable per role within the rights) · **a totals/counter row as an aggregate from individual status** (metric == filtered rows, v0.24) · **export as a clipboard round trip** (export with a schema row v0.12 — every export is re-importable) · handling of large volumes (paging/virtualization) without silent truncation · rights-precise row actions · a speaking empty state with a next step. *Testable:* "Every table view fulfills the capability catalog; counter row == row count of the active filter; an export artifact can be losslessly re-read via schema import; a silent truncation of volume without a display is a deviation."
- `[HARD]` **Ledger integrity — correction by counter-entry, never by overwriting:** Booked entries of a ledger (postings, stock levels, cash/account movements) are **immutable**; errors are corrected by a **reversal/counter-entry referencing the original entry**, never by changing or deleting. Balances and stock levels are exclusively **aggregates from individual entries** (never manually maintained parallel figures); every entry carries the audit record (v0.4) and its confirmed state (v0.2). *(Instance of hide-not-delete + audit trail + "aggregate from individual status" — applied to bookkeeping; domain-specific retention/record-keeping obligations per jurisdiction via Lex, nothing invented.)* *Testable:* "A mutation or deletion attempt on a booked entry is not executable; every correction references its original entry; balance == sum of individual entries at every point in time (recalculation = 0 deviation)."
- `[STANDARD]` **One product/app register — the rule in the master, the list in the tool:** Exactly **one** living product register exists for all started applications (Ledger, Humpl, Mares, BillingTool, tuning-mall, Universe-Mail, Demo-Bestand …) — **maintained in the team's own tool** (Humpl/Universe project world, dogfooding v0.35), **not in the master document** (the document holds the rule, never the aging inventory — §18). Per app: `status` (idea · started · real lab · master-compliant up to vX · productive · paused (with a follow-up date!) · archived) · caretaker/developer role · referenced **master state** (against which version it was last checked) · a link to its **coverage board** (v0.35) · migration phase old→new. The register permanently answers: *Which apps have we started, which do we need to finish, which are up to date?* *Testable:* "Every started app has exactly one register entry with status, role, master state, and board link; an app without an entry is a deviation; a master state older than N main versions generates a review task (`[OPEN]` N)."

*(from the Ledger Extraction — v0.43. Author's mandate: extract all table/list rules from `ledger-and-lines-v12_4_2_.html` and define them as **configurable functions switchable per role**. The extracted capability catalog: §5 "table capability-switch catalog"; it is at the same time the initial stock of the ledger gap list (v0.42 migration).)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Every table capability is a named switch per scope × role:** The capability catalog (§5 v0.43) is shipped per template/view as a **default profile** and configured per organization → area → role (on/off; hide-not-delete — switching off deletes nothing). **OFF is enforced at the point of action, not merely hidden** (§2 v0.7 "visibility is not security"): the direct invocation of a switched-off capability is refused. *Testable:* "Every capability carries exactly one switch key; every view references its effective profile (chain, minimum wins); the direct invocation of an OFF capability delivers the refusal, not the effect."
- `[HARD]` **Capability switches only narrow — they never open up:** No role/template configuration can extend beyond the rights matrix (§4) or override integrity rules: **ledger integrity (counter-entry, v0.42), the audit obligation, MANDATORY gates, and data-classification gates are unswitchable.** In particular: no switch ever permits changing/deleting booked entries; structural/format capabilities affect display and unbooked work, never the ledger itself. *Testable:* "Effective capability ⊆ rights-matrix permission; a switch object addressing a [HARD] rule cannot be created; a mutation attempt on booked entries remains non-executable regardless of any cfg."
- `[STANDARD]` **Confidence import is a suggestion (tiered governance in import):** Extraction from image/PDF/screenshot displays its **confidence** and generates suggestion rows that are humanly adopted (suggestion ≠ decision); the procedure honestly carries its I-tier (deterministic parsing I0 · own model I1 · generative extraction I2 → then provider, data-classification, and budget governance — a screenshot with personal data reaches external AI only through the gate). *Testable:* "Every import adoption references human confirmation; every extraction procedure carries its I-tier; an I2 import without a classification record is not executable."
- `[STANDARD]` **Honest work and sync status:** Storage, sync, and queue status are visible (saved · waiting · synced · failed→task); the selection/live statistics are an aggregate from the individual cells (v0.24 pattern); change highlighting shows fresh changes by others during collaboration. *Testable:* "No silent loss between states; statistics == recalculation of the selection; changes by others since the last view are flagged."

*(from the Search/Filter Thinking chat — v0.44. Author's mandate: filters always above the table; highly sortable and filterable with boolean logic; **search-within-results** as its own function ("some minds think this way"); plus the note to first improve the base search — with a short example of why the two are fundamentally different. Applies to Ledger & Lines and every table view; the points also become migration cards of the ledger gap list.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **The filter bar always sits above the table and governs everything below it:** Filter/search controls sit **above** the rows (never hidden in menus, never below); the **active filter state is visible at all times** (which conditions apply, how many rows out of how many: "12,944 of 1,304,328"); everything below the bar obeys the displayed state without exception — no row outside the filter, no silent additional condition. *Testable:* "Every table view renders the filter bar above the first data row; the displayed hit count == row count; an active but non-displayed condition is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Boolean filter logic as full capability:** Filters can be formulated per column **and** combined boolean-wise — AND / OR / NOT with bracketed groups — and combined with the full-text search; every boolean combination can be saved as a named view (v0.42). *Testable:* "AND/OR/NOT and at least one grouping level are expressible; the result matches the set logic (spot-check recalculation); a boolean view can be saved and restored."
- `[STANDARD]` **Search-within-results as a visible stage chain (`search.within_results`):** Every refinement searches **exclusively within the current subset** (never a silent fresh query against the entire dataset) and appears as a stage in a visible chain with counters ("1,304,328 → 12,944 → 231"); every stage is individually removable; a change to the **base search** resets the chain with a warning. **Mindset hint (mandatory element on first refinement per session):** *"Tip: First check whether an improved base search is actually what's meant — search-within-results only finds what is already within the subset."* **Short example (standard help text):** base search "status = open" → 12,944 of 1,304,328 rows; search-within-results "invoice 2023" only finds *open* 2023 invoices — the 2023 invoice that was *closed* yesterday exists in the database but not in the subset: the refinement **inherits all the limitations and blind spots of the first search**; the improved base search "invoice 2023" over the entire dataset would have found it. Mnemonic: *Search-within-results can only narrow — never discover what the first search excluded.* *Testable:* "Every refinement stage demonstrably operates on the subset of the previous stage (hits ⊆ previous stage); the chain display carries the counter per stage; the hint appears on the first refinement; a base-search change without a chain reset is a deviation."

*(from the Split-Pane chat — v0.45. Author's finding via screenshot: a window divider with a hover explanation "Click to collapse ⌘B · Drag to resize". Author's mandate: record it as a layout Golden Rule — vertical, horizontal, nested within each other — and ensure it everywhere it is wanted and useful. Finding: was not yet included.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **Divisible workspaces with self-explanatory dividers (split panes):** Wherever a view has adjacent areas (list/detail, navigation/content, editor/preview), the dividers are **draggable** (resize) and **clickable** (collapse/expand) — vertical, horizontal, and **nested within each other** (each level independent). Every divider **explains itself on hover** (hint: "Click to collapse ‹shortcut› · Drag to resize"; shortcut shown where one exists). Collapsed areas remain **visibly indicated and reversible** (nothing disappears lost — hide-not-delete applied to layout); the area split is **remembered per user and view** (pattern of saved views v0.42); dividers are **keyboard-operable** and large enough for touch (Aria: operability + hint contrast ≥ 4.5:1). Its use is explained per view template (where wanted/useful — not every view needs panes, but where areas sit side by side, this design pattern is the default). Catalog keys (§5 v0.43): `view.split_resize` · `view.split_collapse` · `view.layout_remember`. *Testable:* "Every rendered divider is draggable and clickable and shows the explanatory hint on hover (including shortcut, if defined); collapsing is visible and reversible by click/key; the split survives a reload per user/view; nested levels are independently operable; a view with adjacent areas without an explained pane decision in the template is a deviation."

*(from the RAG chat — v0.46. Author's question: "What is a RAG-layer knowledge-base concept — do we already have that, or do we need to adapt?" Finding: the governance half exists (§18 knowledge base, v0.40 locked-down prompts, data-classification gate, source-status); the retrieval architecture is supplemented here — at Universe standard, i.e. with rights filtering, freshness coupling, and degradation, which standard RAG usually lacks. Detailed architecture: §18.5.)*
- `[STANDARD]` **The retrieval index is a derived artifact with freshness coupling:** The knowledge index (classic + semantic/embeddings) is a derived form of the knowledge artifacts (§18) — every index piece carries the **source version stamp** of its artifact; a source change generates reindexing; pieces from outdated sources are flagged or excluded from retrieval. *(Eighth instance of "nothing ages unobserved" — the AI never answers unnoticed from stale knowledge.)* *Testable:* "Every index piece references an artifact + version; spot check: no retrieved piece stems from an obsolete artifact version without a flag; a source release without a reindex task is a deviation."
- `[HARD]` **Rights-filtered retrieval (the gate bites at the point of retrieval):** Every knowledge piece inherits the data class and access rights of its source; retrieval filters **at query time by the rights of the asker** — what the person is not allowed to read reaches neither the prompt nor the answer. RAG is never a rights bypass (§2 v0.7 "visibility is not security", applied to knowledge); external I2 generation is additionally gated by the data-classification gate. *Testable:* "A query test with a rights-restricted identity delivers no blocked piece in prompt or answer; the filter sits at the retrieval endpoint, not in the UI."
- `[STANDARD]` **Citation obligation for knowledge answers:** Every RAG-supported answer references the pieces (artifact + version) it relies on; statements without a source piece are visibly marked as "freely spoken" (no invented precision, v0.1; source-status rule applied). *Testable:* "Every answer carries its source-piece references; a portion without a reference carries the flag."
- `[STANDARD]` **Search degradation instead of knowledge failure:** On AI-off, budget exhaustion, or provider failure, the knowledge query degrades to a **classic search with result listing** — the relevant pieces themselves instead of a formulated answer. Loss of convenience, never loss of control (disconnection test applied to the knowledge query). Tier mapping: **retrieval/embeddings = I1** (own model, in the platform, model governance, token-free) · **generation = I2** (adapter, locked-down RAG prompts v0.40, budgeted) · frequent stable questions are distillable (§10.14). *Testable:* "In the degradation test, the query delivers result listings without I2; answer generation references a registered RAG prompt; the embedding procedure carries its model governance object."

*(from the author's data-quality mandate — v0.47, **approved by the author on 2026-07-16**. Author, roughly verbatim: "no fake nonsense data"; real-time notice of matching/similar data on entry and import, staged; prompt for merging/grooming/correction; auto-completion; avatars and sentinels watch over data maintenance; GDPR-critical because of deletion requests and duplicates with differing spellings; prefer broader deletion and more deletion confirmations — with a disclaimer and a feedback link. Builds on what already exists rather than duplicating it: §10.13 duplicate detection as a review task · §12.3 deletion as a control loop · §12.2 deletion record · §2 v0.11/v0.13 completion process · §2 v0.2 demo labeling.)*

- `[HARD]` **No fake/nonsense data in live operation:** Productive master-data records contain no invented, placeholder, or throwaway entries ("asdf", "Test Test", fantasy email addresses); test/demo data lives exclusively in labeled demo/test datasets (§2 v0.2) and never in deployable seeds with real personal data. Entries matching nonsense patterns (a pattern catalog per field type, thresholds `[OPEN]`) generate a visible follow-up question — no silent saving, no silent rejection. *Testable:* "Every productive master-data type references a nonsense-pattern catalog; a match without a follow-up dialog is a deviation; a demo/seed record with real personal data is a deviation."
- `[HARD]` **Duplicate watch at the moment of creation (real-time, staged):** Every entry mask and every import checks against the existing stock **during entry** and reports in stages: **stage 1 "identical"** (key fields identical) → creation blocked, adopt/merge dialog; **stage 2 "similar"** (similarity measure above threshold: typo distance, transposed names, mishearing variants; measure and thresholds per data type `[OPEN]`) → a visible hint with a side-by-side comparison and the three paths *merge · correct · deliberately create separately (with a reason)*; **stage 3 "remotely similar"** → a silent note on the record for grooming (§10.13 review task). No automatic merging — suggestion ≠ decision (§2 v0.2); every decision becomes a learning datum (§2 v0.29). *Testable:* "Every master-data mask and every import references the duplicate watch; a creation despite a stage-1 hit without a merge dialog is a deviation; every 'deliberately separate' decision carries a reason + attestation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Auto-completion from existing stock (prevention over repair):** Master-data entry fields offer existing entries for adoption from the first character (rights-compliant: only what the role is permitted to see); adopting an existing entry is the preferred path and prevents the duplicate before it arises. Formula/structure fields complete from the catalog (§5), never from free guessing. *Testable:* "Every master-data entry field with a reference to existing stock offers stock suggestions; a field without completion is documented with a justification."
- `[STANDARD]` **Data-quality grooming control loop:** Stage-2/stage-3 notes, import leftovers, and I1 duplicate findings flow into a standing grooming control loop (§11 status model) with caretaker role = data steward: merge (with origin reference, hide-not-delete), correct, or attest as "reviewed as different". Metrics (open candidates, age, merge rate) appear in the data management view (§12.4). *Testable:* "No duplicate candidate older than the deadline (`[OPEN]` per criticality) without escalation; every merge carries origin references from both source records."
- `[HARD]` **Deletion with a similarity tier (GDPR duplicate trap):** The deletion control loop (§12.3) checks **not only exact matches** but searches the stock, using the same similarity mechanism as the duplicate watch, for spelling variants of the requester (all identifiers named in the request). Found candidates by certainty: **certainly belongs** → deleted along with it ("broader deletion"); **probably belongs** → deleted along with it or submitted for confirmation (policy per jurisdiction: Lex `[OPEN]`); **unclear/possibly someone else's** → NOT deleted, with a documented reason — third-party data is neither deleted nor disclosed. The entire search run (measure, thresholds, candidates, decisions) is stored as a **record dossier** on the deletion control loop (accountability; legal classification per jurisdiction: Lex). Re-infiltration protection (block/suppression list with minimal check values instead of plain data): permissibility and design `[OPEN]` — a Lex decision. *Testable:* "Every deletion control loop references exactly one record dossier with a similarity search run; a deletion without a similarity tier is a deviation; a deleted candidate without a membership classification is a deviation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Deletion confirmation as a design pattern (with a duplicate disclaimer):** Every completed deletion generates a confirmation to the requester per the organization's own template (§5 "deletion-confirmation template"; recipient language, communication review track v0.40, mandatory signature): confirmation of the deletion · a **disclaimer** about possibly unrecognized remaining duplicates due to differing spellings or multiple entries · an explanation of the "broader deletion" including an apology for any excess confirmations · a **feedback path** (link/channel `[OPEN]`) for reports of remaining occurrences, without the feedback itself collecting new unnecessary data (data minimization, Vera). *Testable:* "Every deletion generates exactly one confirmation per template; a confirmation without a disclaimer + feedback path is a deviation."


*(from the author's fundamental question "why MySQL and not Cassandra?" — v0.48. Core of the ruling: **Convenience is not an architectural reason — but neither is a felt sense of performance.** Storage follows the shape of the load, based on evidence rather than feeling. We are **pro-SQL and pro-Cassandra — each only where the load demands it.** Detailed teaching pattern with a decision matrix and rollout examples: §5 "storage-per-workload".)*

- `[HARD]` **Storage follows the load, not habit (polyglot persistence):** The choice of data storage per application/area is a **reasoned decision based on the shape of the load**, never a default from "that's what we know" and never from "that's considered faster". The **transactional business core** (postings, rights, master data, cases) sits in a **relational store (SQL)** — not out of convenience, but because our own [HARD] rules demand it: **ledger integrity** (ACID, reversal-instead-of-edit, balances as aggregate), **referential integrity**, **ad-hoc queries** for duplicate/similarity search and deletion with a similarity tier (§2 v0.47), **aggregate management views** (§11.4).
