A **distributed wide-column/NoSQL store (e.g. Cassandra)** is chosen **only** where a **measured**, write-heavy, horizontally distributed load compatible with eventual consistency actually exists (e.g. sensor/telemetry streams, mass event logs) — as **its own profile alongside the core** (§5 profile principle), never as a replacement for the core. **No storage switch on suspicion:** "more performant" is only a statement once there is measured load behind it (I0/"Prove it, don't claim it"); a migration without a proven, measured load problem is a deviation. *Testable:* "Every application/area names its storage type with a load justification (shape of the load + measurement if applicable); a transactional business core on a store without multi-record ACID is a deviation unless the integrity rules are demonstrably satisfied by other means; a storage-switch proposal without a measured load problem is a deviation."


*(from the author's question "should we separate developers into Online/Web and Hardware/Embedded/Firmware?" — v0.49. Answer: yes, and specifically into **three** zones along the already existing §5 profiles — the third being the seam between them. Deepens the function/operations separation v0.35 on the development side.)*

- `[STANDARD]` **Developer roles mirror the technology profiles (three zones, one seam):** Development is separated into three responsibility zones because their disciplines, tools, approval gates, and failure patterns genuinely differ — not out of convenience or org-chart habit: **(1) Web/Online** (profile WEB/SERVER: pipeline, relational core, HTTP/browser, atomic deploy) · **(2) Embedded/Firmware** (profile SCLAN EMBEDDED/FIRMWARE: register-level C, timing, schematic/pin assignment, flash/recovery, hardware approval with a documented test plan — a different gate than web deploy; "don't invent a hardware assumption" [HARD] applies) · **(3) Gateway/Edge as a named seam** (profile SCLAN GATEWAY/EDGE: bridge BUS/BLE/WLAN ↔ web/API). The seam is a **chain-of-command seam**: it has exactly **one named owner** (a bridge role understanding both sides, or a deliberately staffed "belongs to both" slot with a clear decision-maker), otherwise the friction wanders invisibly into the gateway. **What is separated is who builds — not the rulebook:** the master, sentinels, deliverable gate, and coverage board remain **one** source across all three zones; the caretaker role per control loop (§11.3) is staffed appropriately per zone (a hardware-approval loop never staffed with a web role). *Testable:* "Every development task/control loop is assigned to exactly one of the three zones; the gateway/edge has a named seam owner; a hardware-approval loop carries a hardware-competent caretaker role; a task without a zone assignment or an unstaffed seam is a deviation."


*(from the Device-Trust/Key-Management chat — v0.50. Occasion: long-lived SCLAN devices (author's commitment: **25 years of compatibility**), key & licensing authority, takeover of orphaned devices, CRA obligations. The security triad is anchored here **prominently**; the full design pattern "[mn]medianet Key & Licensing Authority" follows as its own specification (spec before code).)*

- `[HARD]` **Security triad of longevity:** For long-lived devices, the security commitment rests on **three inseparable legs** — none holds without the others: **(1) Guarantee** — the longevity commitment (author's ruling: 25 years of compatibility); **(2) crypto disclaimer** — the **honest limitation**: cryptography is subject to the state of the art and may become outdated over the device's lifetime (a condition inherent to the entire industry); the disclaimer sits **above** the statutory minimum support (CRA: min. 5 yrs. support / 10 yrs. update availability) and must never fall below it (contract wording per jurisdiction: **Lex `[OPEN]`**); **(3) Mechanism** — **firmware replacement + takeover** via [mn]'s own root of trust, which keeps the crypto current **within the bounds of hardware capability**; beyond that capability, the physical replacement/takeover path applies. **No leg alone suffices:** guarantee without mechanism is an empty claim; mechanism without disclaimer promises the impossible; disclaimer without mechanism is an excuse (instance of "Prove it, don't claim it"). **Mnemonic:** *Longevity is not claimed, it is delivered — the guarantee promises, the disclaimer honestly limits, the mechanism keeps the promise.* *Testable:* "Every long-lived device/product line references all three legs (guarantee duration · crypto disclaimer above the statutory floor · firmware/takeover mechanism with a root of trust); if one is missing, the longevity commitment is a deviation."

- `[HARD]` **Crypto agility instead of fixed wiring:** Cryptography is never hard-wired into the code, but managed as a **negotiated suite with a binding minimum floor**, raisable over decades (generalizes the risk-proportional key rotation v0.4). The **root of trust must itself be agile** (updatable keys/algorithms) — only this way does the firmware swap carry through to the hardware limit; a hard-wired single-algorithm ROM is disqualified for long-lived devices (criterion of the vendor-neutral MCU requirement profile, §5/§13). **Separation of layers:** the **signing/trust layer** (root of trust, firmware signature, device identity) is **asymmetric** (ECDSA/ECC + hash), uniform across the ecosystem, and AES-**in**dependent; **data encryption** (AES) works via **negotiation with a minimum floor** — point-to-point the strongest common denominator, on a **shared bus** a common minimum floor (weakest link wins or stays out). Minimum floor per tier `[OPEN]` (proposal/author decision: long-term/partner tier **AES-256** because of the 25-year/post-quantum reserve; DIY/short-horizon tier AES-128). *Testable:* "Every device line names a minimum floor per tier, the uniform signing layer, and an agile root of trust; a hard-wired crypto without negotiation/floor, or a non-agile root of trust in a long-lived line, is a deviation."


*(from the CRA/Regulatory chat — v0.51. Occasion + author's finding: "why didn't my system flag the CRA on its own?" The master was strongly inward-facing (sentinels ahead of outgoing work), but without a **horizon scan for applicable legal norms**. This gap is closed — Lex goes from reactive to proactive. Register design pattern as in §13/§18: **the rule in the master, the list in the tool**.)*

- `[HARD]` **Legal-norms register — applicable norms are actively sought, not waited for:** Exactly **one** living register of legal norms applicable to us exists (regulations, directives, laws, mandatory standards), **maintained in the team's own tool** (not in the master — the document holds the rule, never the aging inventory, §18). Per norm: `applies-to` (which products/areas and why) · obligations · **deadlines as timers** (§13.3) · implementation status · proof/coverage board · Lex review status. Caretaker role: **Lex** (legal status) + **Chronos** (deadlines). The author/the system asks, for every new product/device profile and periodically: *"Which norms apply to what we are building?"* — the unknown remains `[OPEN]`, nothing is invented; legally binding classification remains with Lex. **First entry: CRA (EU 2024/2847)** for the networked automation devices (SCLAN). *Testable:* "Every product/device profile references the register entries applicable to it; an upcoming deadline without an implementation record generates a task with lead time; a profile without a norms review, or an applicable norm without a register entry, is a deviation."


*(from the Consolidation chat — v0.52. Author's mandate/concern: "not every app (WorkHub, Mares, Francesco …) gets its own to-do list — otherwise chaos; consolidate the basic functions, extend as needed, but every feature switchable on/off and configurable per role." Generalizes §10.2 (AI switch matrix) from AI states to **every capability** and §5 (the table's capability catalog) to **every service**.)*

- `[HARD]` **Generic service = switchable capability catalog (no app builds its own basic function):** A recurring basic function (e.g. to-do/task list, table, communication, document, AI call) is built **exactly once as a generic service** (§consolidation/service blueprint) — no app builds its own variant. Every service exposes its capabilities as a **versioned capability catalog**; **every capability is individually switchable** via the **switch matrix (§10.2, scope chain global → tenant → area → role → action, minimum wins)** and configurable by the authorized role. **Sensible defaults** ensure the service runs immediately — guiding principle "everything configurable, nothing has to be configured" (§1.2). Extension is a **catalog extension of the one service**, never a parallel implementation in an app. *Testable:* "For a basic function recognized as generic, exactly one service exists with a capability catalog; every capability carries a default + switch scopes per role; an app's own second implementation of the same basic function is a deviation (consolidation candidate)."

*(Boolean-testable, cf. F = V ∧ Z ∧ ¬A.)*

| Role | read | change | approve | lock | escalate | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Author/subject-matter review | + | + | + | + | + | `[GLOBAL]` |
| Administrator/authorized person (in-app, e.g. Captain) | + | + (master data only, role-bound) | + (confirm transaction) | – | `[OPEN]` | `[PROJECT]` |
| Participant/member | + | + (**own** confirmation/preferences only) | – | – | + (help request/substitute request) | `[STANDARD]` (v0.3) |
| T1 (platform owner) | + | + | + | + | + | `[GLOBAL core]` (v0.4) |
| T2–T4 (manager/operator) | + | + (scope-bound) | + (scope-bound) | – | + | `[GLOBAL core]` (v0.4) |
| T5–T6 (worker/reader) | + (scope) | + (own only) | – | – | + (restricted) | `[GLOBAL core]` (v0.4) |
| T7 (device/API) | + (bus scope) | – (via protocol only) | – | – | – | `[GLOBAL core]` (v0.4) |
| Technical role / developer (v0.7) | + (technical/log/tickets) | + (technical/config) | – | – | + | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| Master of the Universe (v0.10) | + | + | + | + | + | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |
| Server crew (v0.10, update) | + (technical/versions/tickets) | + (stack upgrade/config) | – | – | + | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| Role-agent instance (v0.24) | explicit scope only | explicit action only | in principle Z; only expressly delegated routine + | only technically via a fixed gate | + within policy | `[GLOBAL core]`; own machine rights, never automatic role inheritance |

*Additional rule, compliance register (v0.5):* write access to **legally mandatory disclosures** (imprint/register/regulatory/data-protection master fields) is restricted to an **authorized role** (Author/subject-matter review or dedicated legal admin) — **never** sales/operator/participant. *Testable:* "A write attempt on a mandatory-disclosure field by a non-authorized role cannot be executed."

*Additional rule, data-frugal technical access (v0.7):* A technical role (developer) has **no** read right to personal business data (leads, contracts, data-room contents); permitted are system state, log, tickets, configuration. *Testable:* "A read access by the technical role to a personal business object cannot be executed."

*Additional rule, instruction seam (v0.6):* The field `weisungsempfaenger` (instruction recipient: person/company/pool) and the unlocking of an assignment option locked as asymmetrically risky are restricted to **Author/subject-matter review or dedicated legal admin** — **never** sales/operator. Opening it requires a completed attestation field (checked-by/on, v0.5). *Testable:* "A change of `weisungsempfaenger` or the opening of a locked assignment option by a non-authorized role cannot be executed, and is not effective without the attestation field."

*Additional rule, AI switch (v0.26):* The **global** AI off/on switch (emergency stop) is reserved exclusively for the top authority (Master of the Universe / CEO / management). **Scope-/role-/action-level switches** are delegable to leadership roles within their scope — only more restrictive, or within the limit set from above (§2 v0.26 "most restrictive level wins"). **Every role** may tighten AI support for itself (personal opt-out), never wider than its own framework. **Agent instances** may never operate the switch — not even their own. *Testable:* "A global switch attempt by a non-top role cannot be executed; a scope-level switch attempt outside one's own scope cannot be executed; an agent has no write right on any switch object."

*Additional column, key rotation (v0.4):*

| Role | Who may rotate | Executes forced trigger |
|---|---|---|
| KMS / system | automatically (all tiers) | yes |
| T1/T2 | own keys + subordinates | yes |
| T3–T6 | own only | no |
| T7 | receives — does not trigger | no |

### 4.1 · Agents as digital role twins `[GLOBAL core]` (v0.24)

| Building block | Purpose | May act/approve? |
|---|---|---|
| **Agent** | pursues a commissioned purpose; plans, uses tools, processes, monitors and escalates | acts only within its own machine rights; approval only as an expressly narrowly delegated routine |
| **Avatar** | embodied subject-matter/review perspective | no; advises and reasons |
| **Sentinel** | deterministic, measuring, AI-supported, or humanly attested check gate | no; delivers `PASSED | FAILED | NOT_TESTABLE`; the process gate blocks/routes |
| **Human/role** | sets goal, boundaries, rights, exceptions and responsibility | yes, per rights matrix |

**Construction:** (1) one versioned role-agent profile per role; (2) one isolated instance per person × role × organization; (3) one short-lived transaction instance per assignment/case; (4) optionally a shared role service for role-wide monitoring and routing without personal memory or personal approval identity. A person with multiple roles has separate instances; a shared UI may display them but must never mix them internally.

**Mandatory fields:** `agent_id`, `agent_profile`, `profile_version`, `acting_for_person`, `role`, `organisation`, `organisation_unit`, `business_domain`, `auftrag` (assignment), `scope`, `data_scope`, `tools`, `rights_snapshot`, `autonomy_by_action`, `working_time_profile`, `timing_profile`, `escalation_policy`, `applicable_avatars`, `applicable_sentinels`, `ruleset_version`, `valid_from/to`, `audit_id`.

**Work cycle:** check assignment/right → goal/success criterion → information/sources → plan → consult mandatory avatars → result/proposal → run the full sentinel set → execute within autonomy or request approval → check effect → close with acknowledgment; stop/correct/escalate on missing information, right, competence, recipient, a hard sentinel, an exception, or failed attempts. Infinite loops are forbidden via an attempt limit and an escalation path.

**Memory separation:** global role knowledge · organizational knowledge · personal role memory · transaction memory. Personal conclusions are not confirmed facts; provenance, purpose, retention/deletion period and correction/revocation are tracked.

**Escalation resolution:** determine the substantively responsible role in the correct business/data/legal domain → check existence, staffing/appointment, deputization, competence, rights and priority-appropriate reachability → skip empty/unsuitable levels without waiting → the suitable recipient is given an acceptance deadline → without acknowledgment/first action/interim status/resolution, escalate time-triggered horizontally and vertically; P1/P0 in parallel/redundantly → if intermediate roles are missing, go directly to the highest staffed authority → alarm/exception shortens the route per policy. Every handover delivers a brief situation summary and prior attempts. `ASSIGNED_TO` (processing) and `REPORT_TO` (parallel information/oversight) are tracked separately; a report upward neither ends nor shifts the processing. An unresolved closure escalates up to board chair/CEO/management/Master of the Universe.

**Closed task state:** `created → routed → acknowledged → in_progress → result_proposed → result_confirmed → closed`; the branches `blocked/escalated` always lead back to a responsible processing step. Permitted end states: confirmed done · authorizedly/justifiably aborted · auditably merged · authorizedly/justifiably discarded. Every other open state carries `next_action`, `next_actor`, `next_due_at` and escalation.

**Reporting/overview level (`REPORT_TO`):** supervisors and defined oversight roles see the ongoing state in parallel via dashboard and freely combinable filter lists. Minimum filters: organization · area · role · `ASSIGNED_TO` · `REPORT_TO` · priority/risk · status · deadline/overdue · escalation level · exception · closure. Metrics are derived exclusively from the same individual tasks; rights determine level of detail and drill-down.

**Predictive view:** In addition to the current-state view, the system computes rolling horizons (e.g. shift/day/week/month depending on industry) for task load versus available, qualified and authorized capacity. It shows expected gaps, affected downstream tasks, single points of failure, confidence, and L0–L4. Supervisors can confirm/reclassify the situation, commission actions, and tighten thresholds within their rights; a downgrade to L3/L4 requires justification. Forecasting is a decision aid, not an automatic personnel assessment.

**Priority patterns:** P4 planned and P3 normal respect working/rest time; P2 high runs ahead of normal and is shortened; P1 critical uses on-call and partly parallel channels; P0 emergency stands immediately at the top and alerts in parallel/redundantly. Concrete minutes/hours are not universal truths: versioned industry/process profiles and legal/safety maximum deadlines take precedence.

**Audit attribution:** "Prepared by `<role agent>` on behalf of `<person/role>`"; after genuine approval, additionally "approved by `<person/role>` on `<timestamp>`". Every action binds sources, tools, avatars, sentinels, assumptions, changes, approval and final status.

## 5 · Standards                 [GLOBAL core · PROJECT details]

- **The tech stack is a profile, not a single universal stack** `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` (v0.11): Before project start, exactly one primary profile is chosen, and for bridge projects a documented combination. The web/server stack from v0.10 must not be silently transferred to firmware, gateway, or desktop. Deviations/additions only with author approval and a justified compatibility check. *Testable:* "Every production repo names its profile(s), supported target systems, API/protocol boundaries, build and test matrix; no profile in use remains implicit."
  - **Profile WEB/SERVER** `[STANDARD]`: CodeIgniter 4 (PHP 8) · MySQL 8 · React + Vite + Tailwind · Composer + npm/Vite · PDF via WeasyPrint · i18n lead language `de` · dated deliverables/build stamps. This profile applies to internet- or server-side [mn]universe applications, not automatically to SCLAN embedded/gateway.
  - **Deployment sub-profile WEB/SERVER** `[STANDARD]` (v0.20, target state): (1) **Git repository as one source** for backend, frontend and migrations; (2) **CI/CD pipeline** builds, tests and deploys — the frontend static build is produced **in the pipeline**, never on a developer machine; (3) **schema migrations** via CI4's own migration mechanism (versioned scripts in the repo, automatic application, rollback path); (4) **staging environment** as a production twin — every release lands there first, then in production; (5) **automated regression test suite** as a mandatory pipeline stage; (6) **atomic deploy with zero-downtime cutover** and a verified rollback. Concrete CI tool, staging server, deploy tool and test-suite scope: `[OPEN]` (see ToDo v0.20). *Testable:* "The production repo names the pipeline definition, migration directory, staging target and test suite; a production deploy outside the pipeline is a deviation."
  - **Profile SCLAN EMBEDDED/FIRMWARE** `[PROJECT details]`: MCU/board, HAL/LL/bare-metal/RTOS, CubeMX `.ioc`, compiler/SDK, linker/flash layout, debug/recovery and programming tool are bindingly fixed per hardware revision. For the current project: STM32G0B0CET6/LQFP48, SWD PA13/PA14; further details `[OPEN]` until schematic/pin list/clock/test target are available.
  - **Profile SCLAN GATEWAY/EDGE** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]`: bridge between SCLAN BUS, SCLAN BLE, SCLAN WLAN and web/API. Must work locally within the building and optionally with internet services; local core functions must not fail on internet outage. Target environments include Raspberry Pi and other suitable SBCs as well as macOS, Windows and Linux on supported architectures. Concrete implementation language, runtime, packaging and driver strategy remain `[OPEN]` until existing SCLAN drivers/software have been inventoried and assessed.
  - **Profile DESKTOP/LOCAL SERVER** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]`: the same substantive SCLAN server/gateway core should — to the extent hardware allows — run on macOS, Windows and Linux; the GUI and services access it via versioned internal APIs/adapters, not via OS-specific business logic.
  - **Profile PROTOTYPE/DELIVERABLE** `[STANDARD]`: standalone single-file HTML prototypes remain demonstration/coordination artifacts and are not automatically the production server.
- **SCLAN portability architecture** `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]`: protocol/domain core, hardware/transport adapters and UI/server are separated. SCLAN BUS, BLE and WLAN implement a versioned shared device/event contract; OS-specific drivers sit exclusively behind adapters. *Testable:* "The domain core can be tested with test adapters without real transport hardware; swapping an OS/transport adapter requires no change to the SCLAN domain logic."
- **Offline-first gateway with optional cloud** `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]`: the in-house server/gateway functions without internet, including local web GUI, configuration, event processing and defined history. Cloud/internet services are add-ons with clear degradation and synchronization rules. *Testable:* "Internet disconnection test passes; after recovery, the system synchronizes without loss, double action, or silent conflict, per documented rule."
- **Versioned bridge contracts** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]`: BUS/BLE/WLAN frames, gateway events, API schemas and web-GUI contracts are versioned, machine-readably documented, and checked for backward compatibility/migration. *Testable:* "For every active version there is a schema/fixture and a compatibility test; unknown versions are rejected in a controlled manner or migrated."
- **Platform support via CI/real-system matrix** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]`: supported macOS, Windows, Linux, Raspberry Pi/SBC versions and CPU architectures are explicitly tracked. Automated builds/tests are supplemented by real installation and end-to-end checks; an OS may not be advertised as supported until it has passed the matrix gate.
- **Test-routine catalog as one source** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.11): every routine contains at minimum `routine_id`, version/status, valid product/board/assembly/firmware ranges, required test bed/test equipment, preconditions, channel/event target sequence, time/color/audio tolerances, verification mode (`human` or `camera_ai`), abort/error rules, mandatory evidence and approval role. Outdated routines remain historically readable but may not be selected for new runs. *Testable:* "Every stored test run is immutably linked to exactly one routine version active at the time."
- **Unit-under-test status as a closed cycle** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.11): minimum status `untested → in_test → passed | rework | reject_inspection`; a flagged channel fault mandatorily leads to `rework` or `reject_inspection`. After repair, a new full or explicitly defined repeat test follows; the old failure record is retained. Disposal is a separate, authorized and attested decision, never an automatic consequence of a single test.
- **Prototyping / deliverables** `[STANDARD]`: standalone **single-file HTML prototypes** (vanilla HTML/CSS/JS, no external dependencies), runnable locally by double-click. *(v0.3: where needed, small vetted libraries are **inlined/embedded** — e.g. a QR generator — to preserve the single-file property and offline runnability.)*
- **Documents** `[STANDARD]`: generate PDFs programmatically — **WeasyPrint** (HTML/CSS source, styled look) **or ReportLab** (Python-direct, tabular work, charts). Both routes: title page, headers/footers, page numbers. *(v0.4: ReportLab added as an equivalent alternative to WeasyPrint.)*
- **Interactive HTML deliverables with slider controls** `[STANDARD]` (v0.4): parametrizable outputs (financial models, simulations, configuration overviews) are delivered as HTML with `<input type="range">` sliders. Sliders show the current value directly next to them; numeric values with 2 decimal places, unit, and thousands separator where applicable (locale `de-DE`). *Testable:* "Every slider visibly shows its current value; numeric outputs in German format."
- **Verification before delivery** `[STANDARD]` (sentinel-testable, see §6): HTML/JS deliverables pass `node --check` (syntax) **and** a load check (jsdom without `jsdomError`); PDFs are rendered and reviewed before delivery. *(v0.3 addition: **visual verification** — visual/contrast/layout claims are substantiated by rendering and review or pixel checking, not merely asserted.)*
- **External handover / interoperability** `[STANDARD]`: staged — standardized **document** → **machine-readable file** (CSV/XLS) → **portal format** → **API**. Never an email "to be interpreted" (free-form to be retyped); never dependent on the other side's cooperation — always deliver the best-supported path available.
- **Schema-tag import / clipboard import** `[STANDARD]` (v0.4): tabular bulk data is made importable by pasting a **schema row as the first row**. The schema row replaces column headers with system command tags (`+T`, `+ASt`, `+D` etc.) using the same delimiter as the data rows. Untagged columns are **silently ignored** (partial schema allowed). *Testable:* "Schema row + 3 data rows → 3 objects; a row with an unknown tag → ignored, no error; missing schema row → no import."
- **Provenance labeling of reference data** `[STANDARD]` (v0.3): reference data (e.g. addresses, tables/registers) is either **maintained locally** or sourced from an **authoritative upstream source** (API/association); the **source is visibly labeled**; if the upstream source is available, it is preferred (no manual maintenance). *Testable:* "Every reference record carries a visible source label (local/upstream)."
- **Module switch (on/off, hide-not-delete)** `[STANDARD]` (v0.3): function areas can be switched on/off by the authorized role; "off" **hides** the area along with dependent elements (navigation, downstream functions) but **deletes no data**; dependent switches become automatically inactive. *Testable:* "Module off → associated nav/function elements not visible; data unchanged and present; dependent switch deactivated."
- **Self-generated artifacts from configuration** `[STANDARD]` (v0.3): human-readable summaries/rule postings are **generated entirely from active modules + set values**; no parallel hand-maintained version (→ "one source"). *Testable:* "If a setting changes, the generated artifact changes accordingly; no second source exists."
- **Help-request broadcast** `[STANDARD]` (v0.3): if a person needs a substitute, the responsible role **and** all matching candidates are notified **simultaneously**; status remains visibly "to be resolved" until solved. *Testable:* "A substitute/help request generates notifications to the responsible role AND qualified candidates; the position remains 'to be resolved' until solved."
- **Two-person check on incoming payment** `[STANDARD]` (v0.3): deposits pass through **reported → confirmed** by the authorized role (two-person principle) before being posted. *(An instance of the approval gate, payment-related.)* *Testable:* "A booking is considered posted only after confirmation by a second authorized role."
- **Payment/billing audit as a periodic human control loop** `[HARD]` (v0.53, Sivaji's objection "don't rely 100% on automation"): automated payment/billing processing **accelerates the check, never replaces it**. Independently of the legal/compliance check (Lex), **every billing period** (at least weekly, additionally aggregated monthly) goes through its own audit control loop: (1) a **sentinel** deterministically checks computational integrity (sums, open/orphaned items, negative balances, double bookings — true/false, without discretion, §4.1); (2) the **responsible role** (finance/accounting) performs a **genuine inspection** — at minimum a sample, a full check on anomaly or threshold breach — never a mere rubber-stamp (§20.3 "the human is never a pass-through"; proposal ≠ decision); (3) the check is **attested** (who/when/finding) and is part of the audit chain (§20.4 evidence synergy); (4) a finding generates a **task within the control loop** (§2 v0.13), never a silent correction (ledger integrity v0.42 applies unchanged: correction only via reversal/counter-entry). This is an **instance of the existing control regime** (routine sampling, §10.6) — applied to payments/billing — and extends "two-person check on incoming payment" (v0.3, single entry) to the **periodic aggregate level**. Automation/agents may prepare the check (sentinel run, anomaly pre-sorting, I1 proposal), never replace it — the agent prepares, the role decides (§4.1, §20.2). *Testable:* "Every billing period has exactly one responsible reviewer, one timestamp of review, and one documented review finding; a period without human review acknowledgment is a deviation, even if the sentinel passed." *(→ duplicate of/extension to §5 v0.3 "two-person check on incoming payment"; instance of §10.6 control regime + §20.3 "human is never a pass-through".)*
- **Cryptography standard** `[STANDARD]` (v0.4): three independent protection layers: (1) **In transit** — TLS 1.3, no fallback to older versions; (2) **At rest** — AES-256-GCM at storage level, per-tenant keys; (3) **Per record** — Ed25519 signature on every write for non-repudiation. All three layers are complementary, not redundant. *Testable:* "A technical mechanism is implemented for every layer; no layer is missing."
- **Key management system (KMS) — hierarchy** `[STANDARD]` (v0.4): root master key (HSM, never leaves hardware) → tenant master key (AES-256) → user signing key (Ed25519) / device bus key (AES-256) / service API key / session token (JWT, short-lived). No key is ever transmitted in plaintext; delivery only as a key-wrapped package with the recipient's key. *Testable:* "No key material appears in logs, HTTP responses, or error messages."
- **Compliance mapping** `[STANDARD]` (v0.4): security controls are **implemented once and mapped to multiple frameworks** (GDPR, NIS2, ISO 27001, BSI IT-Grundschutz, SOC 2, eIDAS) — not separately per framework. An audit evidence package must be producible on request. *Testable:* "A mapping table exists for every implemented control; the package is producible in < 24 h."
- **Mandatory-disclosure register (compliance register)** `[STANDARD]` (v0.5): every legal/contractual mandatory disclosure is a **register entry with four fields**: (1) *what* — the obligation with its legal basis; (2) *value origin* — config / DB / file; (3) *who may set it* — an authorized role only, never sales/end user; (4) *fulfillment predicate* — machine-evaluable (all mandatory fields filled **and** no `.test` value **and** no `TODO`/empty string). The register is the *one source* for whether the system is ready for delivery. *Testable:* "Every register entry has all four fields; the fulfillment predicate is a Boolean expression that can be evaluated at runtime."
- **Graduated compliance severity (blocker/warning/notice)** `[STANDARD]` (v0.5): register entries carry exactly one of three severities. **Blocker** — prevents production start (see §2 go-live gate). **Warning** — operation continues, but every admin login shows a **non-dismissible banner**; acknowledgment only with name + timestamp in the audit log. **Notice** — the software cannot verify correctness and merely asks **who confirmed what, when**. *Testable:* "Every entry has exactly one severity; a blocker blocks the mode switch; a warning generates a non-dismissible banner + audit acknowledgment; a notice generates a confirmation prompt with name/time."
- **Placeholder convention (no invented value)** `[STANDARD]` (v0.5): missing values are **never invented**. Placeholder addresses/domains use the reserved **`.test` TLD**; missing legal mandatory fields are rendered as **visibly styled placeholders** (e.g. orange `⟨…⟩`) — never as a plausible-looking fake value. *Testable:* "In non-production state, missing mandatory values are marked as a visible placeholder; a production build contains neither a `.test` value nor a visible placeholder marker (otherwise the blocker applies)." *(→ tightening of §2 "no invented figures/sources" + "demo labeling", see duplicates.)*
- **Generate translations from one lead language** `[STANDARD]` (v0.7): all locale files are generated from **one** lead-language structure (key order) and checked for **exact coverage parity** before delivery; missing keys break the build instead of silently falling back to the lead language. *Testable:* "All active languages have the same key set as the lead language; deviation = build break."
- **Ticket routing table as one source** `[STANDARD]` (v0.7): category → role/team → channel → escalation, in exactly one place; the reporting UI reads from it and shows the target before sending. Rollout stages: (1) email (no server), (2) real ticket with case number, (3) webhook to a collaboration system (field prepared, empty until the target system is determined). *Testable:* "Every category has a target entry; the UI shows it; an empty target is a deviation."
- **Knowledge-gap control loop (FAQ/help request)** `[STANDARD]` (v0.53): an **answer not found** in the FAQ/help center is not a dead-end event (§2 "open is never a dead end"), but triggers the same process control loop as a quick-create item (§2 v0.13): the unsuccessful search/the "not helpful" feedback is entered as a **knowledge-gap task** with title, trigger (search term/article/timestamp), responsible role (content/support maintenance) and deadline in that role's task list — separate from individual support tickets (bug/feature/custom development, §5 ticket routing), since its goal is not solving *one* case but **closing the knowledge gap for all future askers**. Completion = a new or improved FAQ/help article that closes the gap (acknowledgment). The aggregate "open knowledge gaps" is a management metric for the content-maintenance role. *Testable:* "Every unanswered FAQ query/negative article feedback generates exactly one knowledge-gap task with a responsible role and deadline; a knowledge-gap task counts as done only after publication of the closing article." *(→ instance of §2 v0.13 "task/to-do list per role" + §2 "open is never a dead end", applied to the knowledge base itself, not just to business cases.)*
- **Currency/legal-status routing path (routing by category)** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.10): reports from the currency/legal-status sentinel (Chronos) follow the central routing table — exactly one recipient per category:

| Category | Recipient role | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| Legal situation (changed laws) | **Master of the Universe** | decides whether the version/set of rules needs to change |
| Case law (new rulings) | **Master of the Universe** | assesses impact on calculation/rules |
| Tech-stack versions (per profile: WEB/SERVER, EMBEDDED, GATEWAY …) | **Server crew** | plans & carries out upgrade/update |

*Testable:* "Each of the three categories has exactly one recipient; the reporting UI/log shows the recipient before sending; a category without a recipient is a deviation."
- **Task/to-do list per role (process sink & control-loop closure)** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.13): every role has a **task list** into which process tasks (e.g. from quick-create items) flow in with **title, trigger/origin, deadline and status**. Process management **creates, tracks, escalates and closes** these tasks; completed ones carry an **acknowledgment** (who/when). Aggregate figures (open/overdue/done) are derived from individual tasks (§2 "aggregate from individual status"). *Testable:* "Every generated process task has exactly one responsible role, one deadline, and one status; no task without a responsible party; completed ones carry an acknowledgment."
- **Deputy/escalation target per role in routing (resolution to staffing)** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.16): the central routing table (§5) additionally carries, per role, **staffing status, deputy and escalation target**. On delivery, the target role is resolved to a **staffed** recipient; if empty/absent, the deputy applies, otherwise escalation upward to the guaranteed-staffed authority (Master of the Universe). *Testable:* "Every role in routing has a stored deputy/escalation target; delivery never lands on an unstaffed role without a resolved substitute recipient."
- **Email case binding (subject + sub-address)** `[STANDARD]` (v0.7): cases conducted via email carry the reference redundantly in the subject (`[REF] …`) and the reply address (`local+REF@…`, RFC 5233). Pre-filled email texts use real line breaks (no literal `\n`). *Testable:* "A reply with a changed subject or changed address still lands on the correct case; generated email texts contain no literal escape sequences."
- **Status-report construction (traffic light · legend · aggregate · highlight)** `[STANDARD]` (v0.8): a status-report deliverable follows a fixed construction — per element a **status badge** from a fixed set; a **legend** listing exactly the statuses that occur; **aggregate tiles** computed from the individual statuses; a **summary/highlight block**; a visible **as-of/date stamp**; branded house style. Produced as HTML→PDF (WeasyPrint) or equivalent. *Testable:* "Report contains badges (fixed set) + matching legend + aggregates derived from items + as-of stamp." *(→ bundles §2 v0.8 "one status per element" + "aggregate from individual status"; delivery form as §5 "documents/WeasyPrint".)*
- **Demo header-band construction** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.21): fixed layout of the demo header band (§2 v0.21) for every demo artifact: **single-line band, full width, topmost page margin, persistent on every page/route** (sticky, does not scroll away). **Left:** warning text "DEMO — nur Fake-Daten!!" (or recipient language; EN: "DEMO — all fake data only!!"), bold, warning-color styling, contrast ≥ 4.5:1. **Right:** labeled role selector "Demo role: [active role ▾]" as a dropdown over **all** roles defined in §3/in the artifact; the active role is visibly shown in the closed state; a switch acts immediately on navigation, route guards, and role-dependent content (making the role difference immediately experienceable when demonstrating). The band is **not** part of the production layout (build exclusion, §2 v0.12). *Testable:* "Band present on every page · warning text on the left · selector on the right with active role · role list matching the defined roles · contrast ≥ 4.5:1 · production build without the band."
- **Adaptive demo showcase (construction)** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.25): fixed layout of the embedded live-demo area on every module landing page (§2 v0.25): **(1) Invitation/claim** above the showcase ("Experience <module> — live, without signup, on any device"). **(2) Wide viewport** (guideline ≥ ~1200 px, exact breakpoint `[OPEN]`): on the left, the demo in a **mobile frame** (~390-px viewport, smartphone look), on the right the same file in the **desktop view** — both live, both clickable; the one codebase rendering the two experiences *is* the responsiveness proof. **(3) Narrow viewport:** only one instance (the device-appropriate one) + badge "Fully responsive — the same demo runs on any screen. You are currently experiencing the mobile view." (wording per language `[OPEN]`, analogous to the demo warning text v0.21); optional toggle "show desktop preview". **(4) Immediately below the showcase, in this order:** a fullscreen button "⛶ Open fullscreen" (opens the direct URL in a new tab without site chrome — for flipchart/projector/screen-share), a **QR bridge** (the desktop view shows a QR code pointing to the direct URL: scanned, the demo runs on the viewer's own device — the strongest physical responsiveness proof and a presentation moment), and the **gated download CTA** ("Take this demo away as a file — show it offline, to the team") at the hottest point directly after trying it. **(5)** Every instance carries the demo header band (v0.21) and starts with a freshly marked fake seed (§2 v0.25 quick access). *Testable:* "Wide: two live instances of the same file side by side; narrow: one instance + badge; fullscreen button present and URL reachable without login; QR code points to the direct URL; download CTA immediately below the showcase; every instance with the demo header band."
- **Communication review lane (construction)** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.40): every outgoing communication template carries its **review profile** (dimension × state MANDATORY/COMMENT/OFF, role-dependent) and its **locked-down review prompts** (versioned, approved, with fixtures) as well as the reference to the **I1 style/spelling database** (knowledge artifact §18). Flow: draft (human/AI, origin recorded) → I1 dimensions (token-free) → I2 dimensions via locked-down prompts (budgeted) → comments as advice, MANDATORY dimensions as a gate before the approval/send gate (v0.2) → objection → conflict case to a higher authority or free clearance. Details/rules: §2 v0.40.
- **Table/list construction** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.42): the capability catalog of every data table (column choice/order · sorting · column filter + full text · saved shareable views · aggregate counter row · clipboard round-trip export with schema row · volume handling without silent truncation · rights-accurate actions · a speaking empty state) — wording and testable clause: §2 v0.42. For ledger views, additionally [HARD]: counter-entry principle, balances as aggregate.

(change highlighting). **H Collaboration:** `kollab.stufe` (collaboration level) · `kollab.identitaet_team` (team identity) · `kollab.aktivitaets_feed` (activity feed, with author/text filter) · `kollab.benachrichtigungen` (notifications) · `kollab.sync_status` (sync status, visible, honest queue). **I Help:** `hilfe.leitfaden` (guide) · `hilfe.beispieldaten` (sample data, demo build only, demo labeling). *Cannot be switched off by [HARD] rule: ledger integrity, audit, MANDATORY/data-class gates, rights-matrix boundaries.*
- **Read the format-handling specification first** `[STANDARD]` (v0.9): before the **first intervention** in a structured format artifact (spreadsheet, presentation, document, PDF), the **format-specific handling specification** is consulted first (it encodes environment constraints: available libraries, recalculation/render tooling, output paths, format pitfalls) — **before** writing code, creating a file, or opening the input file. Multiple specifications may apply simultaneously. *Testable (weak/review):* "For every structured format edit, the associated handling specification was read before the first write/code step." *(→ upstream of §2 v0.7 "verification against the artifact": preparatory knowledge **before** the intervention.)*
- **In-place editing before rebuild (design fidelity)** `[STANDARD]` (v0.9): a designed artifact (layout, fonts, colors, merged cells, images) is, for translation/change, edited **in-place in the original file** so the design is preserved 1:1. A **rebuild from scratch** is only a **fallback** (e.g. if the original is unavailable) and is explicitly labeled as an **approximation**, not presented as faithful to the original. *Testable:* "If the original is available, the operation changes it in place; a rebuild is generated only when the original is missing and carries the approximation notice." *(→ methodological addition to §2 v0.8 "translated deliverable mirrors the lead version"; see duplicates.)*
- **Storage-per-workload (construction + teaching example)** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.48): the binding way to **decide** data storage per application/area — as a repeatable principle, so the choice makes sense to everyone and never again falls out of habit.

  **Why this rule exists (the misunderstanding it removes):** two statements feel like architectural reasons and are not. (a) "Our developers know X well and are comfortable with it" — convenience is not a basis for a technology decision (an instance of "justified, not default"). (b) "Y is more performant/efficient" — abstract performance is meaningless without load. A storage system is never *generally* faster; it is faster for *one form of load* and slower or incapable for others. Both sentences are the same "feels like it's better" trap as with generated code (§ principle "prove it, don't claim it") — one out of inertia, one out of a progress reflex.

  **What the two families actually do:**
  - **Relational (SQL, e.g. MySQL/PostgreSQL/MariaDB):** ACID transactions across multiple records, referential integrity, joins, free ad-hoc queries, strong consistency. Price: vertical scaling is the primary path (plus read replicas, sharding as a last resort).
  - **Wide-column / NoSQL (e.g. Cassandra):** enormous write throughput, horizontally distributed across many nodes, highly available without a central point, geographically spreadable. **Price knowingly paid:** no multi-record ACID transactions, no joins, no referential integrity, no free queries; one models **per query** in advance, deliberately duplicates data, lives by default with **eventual consistency**; deletion creates tombstones (targeted deletion/similarity search become more costly).

  **Why our core is relational (rule-enforced, not convenient):** **Ledger integrity** (§2 v0.42 `[HARD]`) *is* double-entry bookkeeping — immutable entries, correction only via counter-entry, balances exclusively as an aggregate: the textbook example for ACID and strong consistency. **Duplicate guard and deletion with a similarity level** (§2 v0.47 `[HARD]`) require free similarity search across the entire dataset and targeted deletion including spelling variants with an evidence dossier. **Rights matrix** (§4) and **aggregate management views** (§11.4, "nothing maintained twice") are joins and aggregations. On a storage system without multi-record ACID, these guarantees would have to be rebuilt by hand — risking exactly the integrity violations that the `[HARD]` rules forbid. So the core is relational **because our own hard rules require it**, not because someone is used to it.

  **When a NoSQL profile is right:** when a **measured** load exists whose shape suits Cassandra — massive *inbound* data, append-heavy, horizontally distributed, cross-record queries rare, eventual consistency tolerable. Examples in our world: **SCLAN telemetry/sensor data**, **audit/event logs at enormous volume**. That load then gets its **own storage profile alongside the core** (the overall system stays polyglot: relational core + specialized stream storage), never replacing the core.

  **Decision matrix (shape of the load → storage type):**
  | Question about the load | more SQL (relational core) | more wide-column/NoSQL profile |
  |---|---|---|
  | Are multi-record transactions needed (booking, approval)? | yes | no |
  | Referential integrity / joins needed? | yes | no (denormalization) |
  | Free ad-hoc queries (duplicates, reports, search)? | yes | no (query-first modeling) |
  | Strong consistency mandatory (money, rights, GDPR deletion)? | yes | no (eventual consistency ok?) |
  | Load shape: mass writes, append, distributed, cross-record queries rare? | no | yes |
  | Is there a **measured** scaling problem in the core? | (stays SQL) | only then consider NoSQL |

  **Rollout examples (how the rule is applied):**
  - *Invoice/ledger/BillingTool:* relational — ACID + counter-entry; here SQL is not taste but obligation.
  - *Universe contacts, rights, cases, tickets:* relational — joins, integrity, duplicate search.
  - *SCLAN sensor/telemetry stream:* candidate for a wide-column profile — as soon as volume **measures** it; until then `[OPEN]`.
  - *Audit log:* relational as long as query/aggregation capability is needed; only becomes its own stream profile once mass volume is documented.
  - *Knowledge retrieval/RAG (§18.5):* the vector/search store is a **third** profile (similarity index) — this too is polyglot persistence, chosen by the same rule.

  *Testable:* "For every application/area, storage type + load justification is documented; the transactional core is relational; a NoSQL profile references the measured load shape that justifies it; a storage change without a measured load problem is a deviation." *(Concrete products per project — MySQL vs. PostgreSQL, Cassandra vs. ScyllaDB, vector store — are `[OPEN]` (§5 profile choice); the rule fixes the selection logic, not the product.)*

- **Languages / i18n** `[OPEN — TO FILL IN]`: target languages · fallback · key format. *(In this chat, Kö app: **genuine i18n implemented** with de/en/fr/it/es/nl — unlike the de-only state of the other Kö chat; see conflict report. In the [mn]medianet chat: EN/DE/PL/FR/IT implemented.)*
- `[STANDARD]` Multilingualism is the default (fire texts, don't hardcode them).
- `[STANDARD]` **Language choice per person, effective immediately, reachable in multiple places** (v0.3): each person chooses the display language for themselves (e.g. header **and** settings); the choice acts immediately on the entire interface and is stored per person. *Testable:* "Language switch takes effect immediately; the choice persists per person."
- `[STANDARD]` **No half-translated UI / no raw keys** (v0.3): if a translation is missing, a fallback (source language) applies — **never a raw translation key** in the UI; only **enabled** languages are offered in the language picker. *Testable (sentinel):* "No visible text matches the key pattern (e.g. `word.word`); the picker contains only active languages."
- `[STANDARD]` **AI delivers only suggestions** (v0.3): machine translations/outputs are **suggestions**; nothing is applied without explicit human **adoption** (multi-select/batch permitted). *(An instance of §2 "proposal ≠ decision".)*
- `[STANDARD]` Accessibility: observe WCAG; **contrast ≥ 4.5:1** for normal text — **also for emphasis (bold/italic) in dark containers** (v0.3); visible **focus styles**; respect `prefers-reduced-motion`. *Testable (sentinel):* "All text/background pairs including emphasis meet ≥ 4.5:1."
- `[CONVENTION]` **Copy/move (UI):** drag = move; **Alt/Option or Ctrl while dragging = copy** (OS convention, "+" cursor); touch fallback as a copy toggle.
- `[CONVENTION]` **Explainability (UI):** fields and functions carry mouse-over explanations (tooltips).
- `[CONVENTION]` **Support (UI):** a ticket/support element bottom-right on every page/app. *(v0.7: the support entry point additionally offers a path without mandatory login — start via email — and shows the target team before sending.)*
- `[CONVENTION]` **Build stamp & dated deliverables** (v0.7): deliverable builds carry a visible as-of/version stamp; filenames are dated and ascending, so the current version is unambiguous and not confused with cached older versions.
- `[CONVENTION]` **Text-on-image without full-area darkening** (v0.7): readability via glyph shadows and a targeted gradient only behind the text; the image motif remains untouched.
- `[CONVENTION]` **Communication:** product-facing descriptions without developer jargon.
- `[CONVENTION]` **Filenames speaking & system-safe** (v0.3): deliverable filenames name the content and are cross-system-safe — **no umlauts/spaces** ("oe" instead of "ö", hyphens). *Testable:* "Filename without umlauts/spaces; names the content type."
- `[CONVENTION]` **Optional convenience features switchable off** (v0.3): "taste" features (e.g. QR quick-contact) are optional and switchable on/off per person/team.
- `[CONVENTION]` **Slider precision** (v0.4): numeric sliders show values with **2 decimal places** after the comma and use the comma as the decimal separator (locale `de-DE` / `de`). The unit sits directly after the value (e.g. `4.90 %`, `7.25 years`). *Testable:* "No slider value without 2 decimal places and a unit, in German format."
- `[CONVENTION]` **Translating language ≠ changing number/locale format** (v0.9): a **pure translation** does **not silently** change the number/date/locale format (e.g. thousands/decimal separators). A change of numeric locale (e.g. `de-DE` → `en-US`) is a **separate, explicitly offered** option — not an automatic side effect of translation. *Testable:* "After a pure translation, the number/locale format is unchanged; a locale change happens only on explicit request." *(→ supplements §5 v0.4 "slider precision (de-DE)"; format is its own decision axis.)*
- `[CONVENTION]` **Document house style** (v0.4 — from the [mn]medianet chat): navy header band (#0D1B2A) · amber accents (#F59E0B) · Helvetica · confidential footer with page numbers · confidence traffic light (secured / observed / open). Applies to all technical specification documents of this studio.
- `[CONVENTION]` **Speaking style toward end users:** informal "you" form (personal), no developer jargon, constructive and without condescension.

- **Deletion-confirmation template (construction)** `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` (v0.47): mandatory building blocks of the confirmation letter per §2 v0.47 "deletion confirmation": (1) confirmation of deletion with request reference (date); (2) if applicable, note on locked retention remnants (legal situation via Lex); (3) **duplicate disclaimer** — open disclosure that very similar records (typos, transposed names, multiple entries, including by the user themselves) cannot always be technically identified with certainty as the same person; (4) explanation of the **broader deletion** (similarity search run, clearly attributable records deleted along with it) including an apology for possibly multiple or mistaken confirmations ("we prefer thorough over convenient"); (5) **feedback path** for suspected further spelling variants (link/channel `[OPEN]`; asks only about the spelling, displays nothing, collects nothing unnecessary); (6) note on the internal record; (7) mandatory disclosures/signature per legal jurisdiction (Lex). Recipient language mandatory (Lingua); wording reference: the author's draft document v0.47, section 4 (DE lead version + EN derivation). *Testable:* "Every sent deletion confirmation contains the seven building blocks; building blocks 3–5 are never missing; the language version matches the recipient's language."

*(Per sentinel: what it checks · trigger · pass/fail · which Golden Rule it enforces.)*

| Sentinel | checks | trigger | passes when | enforces rule | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Deliverable Gate | Syntax (`node --check`) + loadability (jsdom without errors) of HTML/JS; PDF renders without error; **visual/contrast inspection** (v0.3) | before every delivery | no syntax/load errors; PDF renders; visual/contrast check ok | §2 "no invented precision" / §5 verification | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| i18n Key Sentinel (v0.3) | no **raw translation key** in the rendered UI; picker shows only active languages | before delivery / on navigation render | no visible text matches the key pattern (`word.word`) | §5 "No half-translated UI" | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| Contrast Sentinel (v0.3) | contrast ≥ 4.5:1 for all text/background pairs including highlights (esp. dark containers) | before delivery of UI/PDF | all pairs ≥ 4.5:1 | §5 WCAG / accessibility | `[GLOBAL core]` (partially automatable via pixel/contrast check) |

| **Coverage-Board Sentinel (v0.35)** | exactly one living coverage board per product; every applicable master rule has exactly one card with status/owner (developer role)/deadline/dependencies; metrics == card tally; an applicable rule without a card = deviation | on rule intake (master version) · per sprint/review · before every Deliverable Gate | living status instead of a one-time proof; CPM-/Kanban-trackable | §2 v0.35 coverage board + §2 v0.19 | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |

| **Migration-Path Sentinel (v0.35)** | every legacy tool under migration references a gap list + migration cards; lead usage of the new version only after a confirmed real-lab test; the old state remains operable until then, the way back documented | at migration start · before switching lead usage | no hard cut; prove it before it takes effect | §2 v0.35 migration old→new + dogfooding | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| **Duplicate-Guard Sentinel (v0.47)** | mask/import references the real-time guard; a stage-1 match blocks with a merge dialog; a stage-2 hint with a side-by-side comparison + three paths; "deliberately kept separate" only with a reason + attestation | per deliverable with master-data capture · spot-check in operation | no creation past the guard | §2 v0.47 "Duplicate guard at the moment of creation" | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |
| **Fake-Data Sentinel (v0.47)** | productive inventories checked against a nonsense-pattern catalog; seeds/fixtures with no real personal data; demo data only when labeled | before delivery · periodically · on seed change | no nonsense in the real inventory, no real data in the seed | §2 v0.47 "No fake/nonsense data" + §2 v0.2 | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |
| **Deletion-Similarity Sentinel (v0.47)** | every deletion control loop includes a similarity search run + an evidence dossier + a membership classification per candidate | on every deletion request · spot-check | dossier complete; no unassessed candidate | §2 v0.47 "Deletion with similarity level" + §12.3 | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |
| **Deletion-Confirmation Sentinel (v0.47)** | confirmation per template: seven building blocks including a disclaimer + a feedback path, recipient language, mandatory signature, check lane | on every deletion closure | template fidelity complete | §2 v0.47 "Deletion confirmation as a form" + v0.40/v0.41 | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| **Payment/Billing-Audit Sentinel (v0.53)** | every billing period (at least weekly) has a deterministic integrity run (totals/open items/orphaned items/negative balances/double bookings) **and** a documented human review receipt (reviewer, timestamp, finding); a finding generates a control-loop task, never a silent correction | at the end of every billing period (weekly + monthly aggregated) · immediately on every anomaly | integrity run passed **and** review receipt present; either one missing = a deviation, even with a green sentinel | §5 v0.53 "Payment/billing audit as a periodic human control loop" + v0.3 four-eyes on incoming payments + §10.6 control regime | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |

*Generic pattern:* A sentinel is a **check gate before an outgoing or irreversible action**. Result binary (pass/fail); a failure blocks the action. *(v0.5 extension:* A sentinel can also be a **state gate at mode change** — it doesn't check a single outgoing action, but whether the **system as a whole** may adopt a new operating state at all [e.g. production]. The trigger is then the mode change, not the click.)*

**Decision algorithms (generic patterns, v0.3 — not sentinels, but proposal generators):**
- **Triage pattern** *(who/what is selected)*: ranks/selects candidates based on **preferences** + **history (performance/behavior)** + **risks**; produces a justified proposal; **special case "outcome is at stake"** → priority tips from performance to **participation** (those with little consideration so far). Binding only after confirmation (§2 "Proposal ≠ decision").
- **Arbitrage pattern** *(what is fair)*: settles **distribution questions** (cost, load, space allocation) according to **agreed rules** + **stored values** (rates/deadlines/caps) + **history**; the decision comes "from the rule, not from a person."
- Both display their **rationale** openly (§2 "Justified automation") and use only disclosed factors (§2 "Disclosed decision factors").

## 7 · Avatars                   [GLOBAL core · PROJECT details]

*(Per avatar: name · role · voice/purpose · what it embodies.)*

*(from the delivery/dispatcher legal-framework chat — v0.6: first named **avatars**. These seven appeared in that chat as review voices — each a perspective embodying one Golden Rule. **Important (confirmed by the author): These are avatars, NOT sentinels.** An avatar is the embodied voice/perspective (§7); a sentinel is the machine check gate (§6). The mapping avatar → enforced sentinel(s) is still incomplete and will be completed later by the author — see the to-do list at the end of the file.)*

| Avatar | Perspective / embodies | assigned rule(s) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Prof** | Computational/QA correctness; disclosed assumptions; "Simulation without visible assumptions is a lie with decimal places" | §2 "no invented precision," "disclose assumptions" · §5 verification | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| **Lingua** | Multilingualism as safety, not convenience; recipient language; native language of safety-relevant roles | §2 "Transaction message in recipient language" · §5 i18n | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| **Coach** | Optimization perspective; names the real lever (utilization before route length); warns against overreach | §6 decision algorithms (triage/arbitrage) | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| **Aegis** | Security & abuse protection; hard limits (e.g. cash/quantity caps); emergency function never behind an entitlement | §2 "Security before engagement" [HARD] | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |
| **Aria** | Accessibility/WCAG; one-handed operation; tap targets; sunlight contrast; show only what's necessary | §5 accessibility · Contrast Sentinel | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| **Vera** | Data protection/GDPR; data minimization; no personal movement trail; data dies with the case | §2 "Coarse progress instead of location trail" · "Tenant separation" | `[GLOBAL core]` `[STANDARD]` |
| **Lex** | Law/compliance; imprint/mandatory disclosures; **always** with the note "confirm with a real lawyer/tax advisor" | §2 "Financial/legal-advice disclaimer" [HARD] · "Human attestation" | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |
| **Chronos** | Currency & legal status; deadlines; **version-year lead time**; "no silent stale state" — monitors **three tracks** (legal situation · case law · tech-stack versions per profile) and **alerts to the correct category**: legal tracks → *Master of the Universe*, tech track → *Server Crew* | §2 "Currency/legal-status obligation" + "Category-appropriate currency alert" · §6 **Currency/Legal-Status Sentinel** | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |
| **Tekton** | Physical truth of hardware/firmware/gateway; asks "Is it electrically, temporally, recoverably and end-to-end really tested on the correct board revision?"; separates build success from real release | §2/§5 v0.11 embedded/gateway rules · §6 target, recovery, hardware-release, E2E, offline and cross-platform sentinels | `[GLOBAL core]` `[HARD]` |

*Generic pattern (avatar ⟷ sentinel):* An **avatar** is the humanly named voice of exactly one perspective (§7). A **sentinel** is the machine check gate (§6). They are **not the same thing**: an avatar can enforce none, one, or several sentinels, and a sentinel can be "represented" by an avatar. **[mn]medianet / SCLAN 5.0 — project-specific references (v0.4, generic only):**
- SCLAN basic principle: BUS/modules operate decentrally; server/web GUI serve visualization, parameterization, integration and diagnostics, not as a necessary runtime center.
- Gateway/server bridge comprises SCLAN BUS, SCLAN BLE and SCLAN WLAN; deployed both internet-/cloud-side and locally in the building on Raspberry Pi/other SBCs as well as macOS, Windows and Linux systems. Concrete existing drivers/software, supported OS versions/architectures, packaging and runtime remain to be inventoried (`[OPEN]`).
- Current embedded target (v0.11): **STM32G0B0CET6**, **LQFP48**, 512 KB flash, 144 KB RAM; CubeMX target created; supply pins are documented device-specifically; **Serial Wire/SWD enabled, PA13/PA14 reserved**. No release-ready `.ioc`/firmware yet: schematic, confirmed pin/function list, clock source, board revision, SCLAN transmit/receive pins and the first test scope are missing.
- Hardware release requires a documented test plan including electrical, functional, timing, fault, recovery, repeat/endurance and full system-chain tests. Several matching, version-bound routines are planned. **Routine A without camera:** fixed channel sequence with an audible relay/testbed sequence and a visible LED chase-light/color sequence; human confirmation, channel fault via ~2-second long-press → rework/reject inspection. **Routine B with camera/AI:** the same target sequence additionally confirmed via image/audio evaluation with raw evidence records. Mechanical setup, acoustic feedback for LED channels, sequences, perception/measurement tolerances, camera/microphone methods, thresholds and calibration remain `[OPEN]`.
- Not entered: MSH contract, concrete confidential hardware specs and WorkHub field details — project-specific/confidential.
- Adopted generically: three-column make-or-buy (§2), tier model T1–T7 (§3/§4), cryptography standard (§5), schema-tag import (§5), timezone three-context rule (§2/§6), injection sentinel (§6), disclaimer obligation (§2/§6).
- `[OPEN]`: concrete command-tag vocabulary per project, bus protocol details, firmware certification path.

**PFE / Process Flow Editor — master-status review (v0.54, generic only):**

- **Vocabulary correction:** PFE's editable base unit is called a **Context** (not "machine"/state machine alone) — a context carries its own state machine and draws **Function/Channel** from modules (SCLAN hardware as well as SaaS alike); plus **Scene/Scenario/Invoked Context/Merged Context**. A later integration should adopt this real, already-versioned vocabulary rather than inventing a new one.
- **Proposed two-layer mapping to §11 (additive, author confirmation pending):** the generic control-loop status layer (§11.1: open/running · paused · aborted · closed · closed by decision · deleted/archived) corresponds to the **lifecycle of the context instance** (Draft → Validation → Simulation → Release → Publication → Activation → Monitoring → superseded → archived — derivable from PFE v0.3's own editor/runtime separation), **not** the internal states of the context's own state machine (those remain domain-specific/technical, e.g. Off/On/Warning of a lighting policy). Changes nothing about §11 itself — a purely additive reading.
- **Two terminology collisions (author clarification needed, no unification undertaken):** "Digital Twin" means in PFE a simulated Universe copy for what-if analysis; in the master (§4.1) the closest concept is the "digital role twin" (person×role agent instance) — a different concept, the same word. "Avatar" means in PFE in-app tutor figures (ProcessProfessor/StateMachineProfessor); in the master (§7) avatars are internal review perspectives (Prof/Lingua/Coach/Aegis/Aria/Vera/Lex/Chronos/Tekton) — likewise a different concept, the same word.
- **Not** global (project-specific → own PFE project knowledge base, once set up): the content of `PFE_VISION_v0.2.md`/`v0.3.md` itself, the 11+1 editing paradigms, the decision to forgo XState (D12), the AI-optional/skill-adaptive principle (D13), context merging (D14–D16) — all product-specific PFE architecture, does not belong in the global master.

Leaves out Access, Media and Blinds entirely; introduces a discipline "Function" documented nowhere else; and lists "CO2/Energy" as a primary discipline — **which directly contradicts the correction memo (§9, "Lens Disciplines")**, which explicitly classifies the CO2/energy monitor as a pure visualization-layer construct, never as a module's primary discipline ("no module considers itself the −CO2 module").

## 9 · IP-Protection Rules           [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.22. A "how do we protect what belongs to us while we sell." Principle from §2: showcase ≠ blueprint — the only absolute protection is not delivering the HOW.)*

### 9.1 · Three disclosure levels

| Level | Who | What is shown | What NEVER | Protection |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 · Public** (landing page) | any visitor | value proposition, curated screenshots, short workflow videos — all WHAT/BENEFIT, all fake data | architecture, algorithms, data models, internal screenshots, real figures/data | §2 "no invented precision" + WHAT-only review |
| **2 · Lead** (after contact data) | identified prospect | **Model A:** trial tenant (real product, in the browser, time-limited) · optionally **Model C:** facade demo file | production source code, server logic, keys, confidential project names | A: no code leaves the server · C: facade principle + license + lead ID |
| **3 · Contract** (NDA/engagement) | customer/partner | specifications, architecture, integration details, possibly model B (appliance) | internal roadmap/pricing internals without approval | attestation field (approved-by/on) per disclosure |

### 9.2 · Delivery models per module

- **Model A — hosted trial tenant** `[STANDARD, default]`: its own, isolated tenant on our server; the prospect uses the **real product** in the browser; no code leaves the building. **Preferred path for every lead.**
- **Model B — activated appliance** `[STANDARD, opt-in per module/customer type]`: for customers with a genuine need for local operation (data sovereignty, offline, IT policy). Docker-based, one installer, no toolchain in the field (§2 v0.4); PHP sources encoded (ionCube/SourceGuardian, `[OPEN]`); **license activation** against our license server (trial time-limited + feature flags; upgrade = license key, no reinstallation; grace period when offline per §2 v0.4; revocation possible). Crown-jewel logic can additionally remain server-side (API) even in the trial. **Honesty:** encoding is deterrence (minutes → weeks), not a wall.
- **Model C — facade demo file** `[STANDARD, marketing download only]`: single-file HTML shows behavior/workflow/look with fake data; the **decision logic within it is simulated or simplified, never the production implementation** — because every single file is open source code by nature. Demo banner (§2/§5 v0.21) + license notice + lead ID mandatory.

*Testable:* "Every module runs its delivery model; A is the designated default path from the landing page; B/C only with a documented author decision."

### 9.3 · Model-A requirements (what module code and server must satisfy)

- `[HARD]` **Logic boundary:** business/decision logic lives exclusively server-side (controllers/models/services); the browser receives only rendered UI and the compiled frontend bundle. **Frontend code is by definition public** — crown-jewel logic (e.g. a task-routing engine) has no place there, not even "temporarily." *Testable:* "The delivered frontend bundle contains no logic marked as a crown jewel and no secrets (machine scan)."
- `[HARD]` **Tenant isolation:** every trial tenant is strictly separated (tenant ID in every query, or its own schema; per-tenant keys per the §5 cryptography standard/KMS); no tenant sees another's data, files or configuration. *Testable:* "Tenant-separation test: an access attempt across a tenant boundary returns a denial; an instance of §2 v0.6 tenant separation."
- `[STANDARD]` **Automatic provisioning:** lead form → tenant is up in under a minute, pre-populated with **marked fake seed data** (§2 demo labeling; no real personal data). *Testable:* "The provisioning run creates tenant + seed + access without manual work; seed data carries the demo label."
- `[STANDARD]` **Feature flags & time windows:** modules/feature scope per tenant controllable via flag (instance of §5 "module switch hide-not-delete"); the trial carries an expiry date; expiry ⇒ read-only mode, then deletion after a documented period (GDPR purpose limitation). *Testable:* "Every trial tenant has an expiry date + flag set; an expired tenant is write-locked; the deletion period is documented."
- `[STANDARD]` **Trial banner:** the trial tenant carries (analogous to the demo banner v0.21, but as a **trial** variant) a persistent banner: on the left "TRIAL — test environment, fake data" + remaining time, on the right the upgrade CTA with the [mn] relationship message. No role switcher needed — the prospect is the genuine owner of their tenant; if they want to show roles, they create users (that *is* the demonstration of the rights matrix on the real product). *Testable:* "Every page of the trial tenant shows the trial banner with remaining time + upgrade CTA."
- `[STANDARD]` **Conversion = flag-flip:** trial → paid is a switch (time limit removed, flags per contract); the prospect's data and configuration are retained — the seed grows into the product. *Testable:* "Conversion requires no migration/re-creation; the tenant ID stays identical."
- `[STANDARD]` **Abuse protection:** resource quotas, rate limits and bot protection per trial tenant; conspicuous tenants are throttled/locked, never silently deleted. *Testable:* "Quotas/limits configured per tenant; a lockout produces an audit entry."
- `[STANDARD]` **Lead data minimization (Vera):** the form collects only name, email, consent (double opt-in); technical roles have no access to lead data (§2 v0.7 data-minimal technical access). *Testable:* "Form field set == minimal set; the technical role has no read right on leads."

### 9.4 · Preparing the facade demo correctly (model C)

1. **Facade principle:** simulate the logic (hard-wired example flows), never copy production code and "strip it down" — leftover fragments otherwise stay readable inside.
2. **Secrets zero-check** before delivery: no keys, real endpoints, confidential project names, developer comments/TODOs (machine scan).
3. **Demo banner** (v0.21) + fake-data seed + license notice + **version stamp** + lead-ID marking (visible in the footer, machine-readable in the markup). *(v0.25: the version stamp makes the time machine usable for marketing artifacts too — if a lead says "in your demo I saw …," it is precisely traceable which version they saw.)*
4. **Curated scope:** only the workflows from the landing page — the facade shows the showcase, not the floor plan.

### 9.5 · Module-landing-page construction pattern & customer funnel

Every module landing page contains, in this order:
1. **Value proposition** (WHAT/BENEFIT, recipient's language, informal-address policy per target group) — no HOW.
2. **Curated screenshots** (3–6): fake data only, no internals/architecture visible, consistent house style.
3. **Short workflow videos** (30–90 s per core workflow, fake data, playable muted with subtitles — Aria).
3a. **Embedded adaptive demo showcase** *(v0.25, mandatory element)*: the currently released facade demo runs **live on the page** — construction pattern §5 v0.25 (dual preview mobile/desktop on a wide viewport, otherwise one instance + a responsive badge; full-screen direct URL; QR bridge). Screenshots (2) and videos (3) supplement the live proof, they do not replace it (§2 v0.25 "live proof instead of a claim").
4. **Primary CTA "Try it live now":** minimal lead form → double opt-in → **trial tenant in under a minute** (model A). At this CTA point stands the **[mn] relationship message** (we love hospitality & gastronomy, we make your business the best it can be, we go through thick and thin with you) — mandatory per standing rule.
5. **Secondary CTA "Download demo":** facade demo (model C) for offline viewing, with license/version stamp/lead ID, unlocked via exactly one of the two paths per §2 v0.25 (**path A "conversation"** → marketing/consultation appointment · **path B "concern"** → email + problem description, sending by mail = address verification). This CTA additionally sits directly below the embedded showcase (3a) — the moment "I want to show this to my team" is hottest right after trying it.
6. **Price/financing note:** the [mn] USP (pre-financing of software/customizations/hardware within the SaaS contract, lowest monthly rate, fixed price or SaaS financing, preferably with a defined end) — to be woven into marketing documents per standing rule.
7. **Reporting channel/support entry point** without mandatory login (§2 v0.7) + imprint/mandatory disclosures (compliance register).

**Funnel into the target application (humpl.org):** landing page → lead (double opt-in) → trial tenant (model A, immediate) → onboarding checklist within the tenant (first task, first document, create first role — guided success moments) → trial-expiry reminders (remaining time in the trial banner) → **conversion = flag-flip**, data retained → engagement with the relationship message. Every stage is a closed control loop (§2 v0.13): a lead with no follow-up action is an open task with a deadline and escalation, not a dead end.

### 9.6 · Developer prompt (copyable — "check/establish model-A conformance")

```
TASK: Check module <NAME> for model-A conformance (universemaster.md §9.3) and establish it.
1. Logic boundary: List every piece of business/decision logic currently in the frontend;
   move it behind a server-side API. The frontend receives only display/interaction.
2. Tenant isolation: Prove that every query/file store is tenant-bound;
   write the tenant-separation test (access across a tenant boundary => denial).
3. Provisioning: Implement/check automatic tenant creation < 1 min incl. marked
   fake seed; no manual work, no real personal data.
4. Flags & expiry: Module flags per tenant; trial expiry date; expiry => read-only mode;
   deletion period documented.
5. Trial banner: persistent banner (remaining time left, upgrade CTA right) on every page.
6. Secrets zero-check: scan the frontend bundle for secrets/crown-jewel markers.
7. Deliver the rule-coverage table (§2 v0.19) for §9.3 with the implementation location per rule.
Invent NOTHING: report missing infrastructure (license server, flag system) as [OPEN].
```

### 9.7 · Discussion openers with the programmers (existing code)

*(To Sivaji / server crew — one written answer per question; the result is captured as project knowledge and feeds the v0.22 ToDo items.)*
1. **Multi-tenancy today:** Is the existing code (humpl.org/BillingTool) tenant-capable (tenant ID throughout? separate schemas?) — or single-tenant? What exactly is missing?
2. **Logic inventory:** What business logic currently sits in the frontend bundle and would need to move behind the API (crown-jewels list)?
3. **Provisioning:** What does it cost (effort/approach) to create a tenant automatically in < 1 min — DB, configuration, seed, access?
4. **Flag system:** Does a feature-flag mechanism per tenant/module exist, or do we build one (where)?
5. **Trial mechanics:** expiry date, read-only mode, deletion period — what can the existing code do, what is new?
6. **Secrets scan:** Do today's deliverables (frontend builds, demo files) contain secrets/internals? A one-time existing-stock check.
7. **Effort & sequence:** Rough estimate per point; proposal for which module becomes model-A-capable first (pilot module).
