## 10 · AI On/Off-Switch Governance    [GLOBAL core]

> **Guiding principle `[HARD]` (author determination, binding verbatim): "AI is an accelerator, never a load-bearing wall."**
> Supplemented by the tier clarification (v0.28): **"Machine learning is intelligent code — not intelligent agents and not artificial intelligence."**

*(New in v0.26, expanded v0.27/v0.28. A "Who is allowed to cut the power — and does the house still stand afterward". The proof is the AI/I0 disconnection test. Applies system-wide to all [mn]universe applications — a generalization of the Humpl delegation ladder "AI off → comments → proposes/human approves → decides and informs".)*

### 10.1 · Function classes — what the switch switches (and what it never does)

| Function class | "AI off" means | remains **always** active (not an AI matter) |
|---|---|---|
| **Agents** (§4.1) | no agent instance runs; running processes stop and are handed over to the responsible human role with a short briefing (§2 v0.26) | the tasks themselves, their deadlines, timers, escalation chains, and the closed control loop (deterministic mechanics) |
| **Avatars** (§7) | no AI-generated review voices; every avatar perspective exists as a **human review checklist** and is worked through by the review role | the review duty as such (the avatar is the perspective, not the tool) |
| **Sentinels** (§6) — three-way split | **AI-supported** sentinels fall back to their stored fallback: deterministic replacement check or human attestation (`verification_mode=human`, attestation field checked-by/on) | **deterministic** sentinels (contrast, schema, migration, quotas …) — they are measuring instruments, not AI; **humanly attested** gates — unaffected. **The gate stays, the checker changes.** |
| **AI proposals/generation** (translation suggestions, text drafts, rankings, triage justifications, generated summaries with an AI component) | proposal fields stay empty or show "AI off — manual entry"; human input/rule templates take over | artifacts deterministically **generated from configuration** (§5 "Self-generated artifacts") — template mechanics, not AI |
| **Learning** (learning control loop, §2 v0.24 learned routines) | its own switch; AI-off also stops learning **and** the data collection for it; existing learning states are preserved but neither used nor advanced | the audit history of human decisions (it is a log, not a learning run) |

### 10.2 · Switch matrix — scope × state

**Scopes (chain, inherited like rights):** Global → Organization/tenant → Business unit/site → Role → individual action/process step.
**States per scope × function class (the delegation ladder as a scale, ≙ A0–A5 §4.1):** `off` · `comments` (AI observes and notes, executes nothing) · `proposes` (AI prepares, human approves every effect) · `decides and informs` (only within explicitly delegated routine boundaries, §2 v0.24).
**Resolution:** effective state = **minimum of the chain** — lower down may only tighten, never open up. **Safety net:** on mismatched preconditions, every action automatically falls back to `proposes`.

### 10.3 · Switching rights (who may do what)

| Level | who switches | character |
|---|---|---|
| **Global (emergency off/on)** | exclusively the topmost authority (CEO/management/Master of the Universe) | emergency-off: immediate, without consultation, without grace period; turning back on is a deliberate act of approval |
| **Organization/tenant · business unit** | delegable to leadership roles within their own scope | only more restrictive, or within the boundary set from above |
| **Role** | the role's leadership level; additionally **every role may tighten itself** (personal opt-out) | self-opt-out never beyond the frame; re-opening the frame only by the leadership level |
| **Action/process step** | process owner within their scope | finest granularity; typical for piloting ("travel-expense pre-check only, set to `proposes`") |
| **Agents** | **never** — no agent operates a switch, not even its own (§4 supplementary rule) | technical prohibition, not an appeal |

### 10.4 · Configuration object (per switching action, versioned)

`scope` · `function class(es)` · `state` (off/comments/proposes/decides) · `reason_category` (law/compliance · co-determination/works council · data protection · quality/trust · cost · pilot/trial · incident/investigation — catalog `[OPEN]`) · `reason_freetext` · `valid_from/until` (time-limited switching with an automatic follow-up task to the approver) · `switched_by/on` · `review_on` · `audit_id`. **Every** switching action creates a signed audit entry; a switching action without a reason cannot be executed (§2 v0.26).

### 10.5 · Behavior when switching off (orderly transition)

1. **Immediate effect** within the affected scope (one evaluation cycle).
2. **Running agent processes:** stop → for each process exactly one **handover task** to the responsible human role with a short briefing (status · steps/attempts so far · next due step · deadline) — routing, deputizing and escalation like any task (§2 v0.16/v0.24); no process ends in a void.
3. **Hide-not-delete:** profiles, configuration, learning states, history are fully preserved and auditable — only rendered ineffective.
4. **Visibility:** affected roles see a status element "AI: off — reason, since, by role"; no one works unknowingly without their usual support.
5. **Gates degrade, they do not fall:** AI-supported mandatory sentinels switch to their fallback (10.1); every gate continues to be enforced.

### 10.6 · Behavior when switching on (conservative restart)

1. Switching on is an **act of approval** by the authorized role (proposal ≠ decision) — never automatic, never by time elapsing alone (expiry of a time limit generates a follow-up **task**, not an auto-flip).
2. **Safety net active:** the first N actions per scope run one level more conservatively (`proposes` instead of `decides`) until the **control regime** (routine sampling every N decisions; stricter regime for uncertain decisions) has re-established trust.
3. **Learned routines (A3–A5):** after an off-duration above the threshold, or on context change, a renewed approval check is required (threshold/criteria `[OPEN]`, see ToDo v0.24 "routine learning approval").
4. **Learning control loop:** human decisions from the AI-off phase become available to learning only after the learning switch is turned back on — the human may accept/ignore/change them.

### 10.7 · Full operation without AI — the proof (AI disconnection test)

This is how every core function operates in the global-off state — this is the acceptance standard of the AI disconnection-test sentinel (§6):

| Function | carried without AI by |
|---|---|
| Task routing & escalation | routing table (one source, §5), staffing/deputizing, time-driven deadline timers, P0–P4 mechanics — fully deterministic (§2 v0.24) |
| Approvals & rights | rights matrix (§4), approval gates, attestation fields — were never AI |
| Documents/artifacts | templates + set values (§5 "self-generated artifacts from configuration"); AI text suggestions are dropped, mandatory content comes from the register |
| Reviews | avatar perspectives as human checklists; the review role works through them and attests |
| Verification gates | deterministic sentinels unchanged; AI-supported ones via fallback (replacement check or `verification_mode=human`) |
| Process completion | closed control loop, receipts, `REPORT_TO` dashboards from individual tasks — deterministic aggregation |
| Translation/i18n | lead language + approved locale files; new translations manual (AI only ever supplied suggestions anyway, §5 v0.3) |
| Predictive view | dropped without replacement or runs purely rule-based (calendar arithmetic without a model) — declared as a loss of convenience, never as a loss of control |

**Reading for the owner:** The AI disconnection test is the same figure of thought as your internet disconnection test (§5 offline-first) — what the cloud is to SCLAN, AI is to the organization: a valuable addition with defined degradation, never a single point of failure.

### 10.8 · AI provider abstraction (adapter architecture, v0.27)

**Principle (§2 v0.27 [HARD]): AI behind an adapter, never in business logic.** Between business logic and every AI provider sits the **versioned internal AI contract**:

| Layer | content | analogous to (proven pattern) |
|---|---|---|
| **Business logic** | calls only capability classes of the internal contract ("translate", "propose a reply", "classify a document", "extract fields", "justify a ranking", "plan an agent step") | SCLAN domain core (§5) |
| **AI contract** | per capability class: input/output schema, context limits, quality/latency expectation, fallback behavior, golden fixtures — versioned, machine-readable, backward-checked | Versioned bridge contracts (§5) |
| **Provider adapter** | translates the contract to exactly one provider/model (cloud API, customer's own contract, or local model); contains all provider-specific details (auth, endpoints, prompt construction, model version) | OS/transport adapters (§5) |
| **Null/test adapter** | P0 adapter (delivers "AI off", deterministic) and mock adapter for tests without a real provider | test adapter without real transport hardware (§5) |

**Capability classes instead of provider features:** business logic knows only classes, never providers. A provider declares in the register which classes it fulfills; if a class is missing, the defined degradation applies per class (another approved provider in the fallback chain, or P0 behavior "field stays empty, human takes over"). Capability class catalog `[OPEN]`.

### 10.9 · Provider register & approval (v0.27)

Register entry per provider — construction analogous to the mandatory-fields register (§5 v0.5, four-field thinking):

`provider_id` · `name` · `operating_model` (shared cloud / dedicated cloud / on-prem) · `hosting_locations/data_residency` · `jurisdiction_approvals[]` (per jurisdiction: approved / blocked / under review, with legal basis/justification) · `dpa_contract` (Art. 28 GDPR, reference) · `certifications/regulatory_role` (e.g. ISO 27001, SOC 2, EU AI Act role) · `training_on_tenant_data` (no / only with documented tenant approval) · `capability_classes[]` (+ fixture status per class) · `allowed_data_classes[]` · `cost_model` · `status` · `approved_by/on` (attestation field) · `review_on`.

**Rules:** allowlist, default deny (§2 v0.27 [HARD]); approval/blocking only by the topmost authority or a dedicated legal-admin role (never sales/operator — §4 pattern from mandatory fields); blocking takes effect immediately for all tenants of the jurisdiction; running calls fall back in a controlled manner to the fallback chain or to P0 (never silently to a different external provider).

### 10.10 · Tenant sourcing models P0–P3 ("Bring Your Own AI", v0.27)

| Model | Description | Data/contract situation | typical occasion |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P0 — no AI** | §10-off state; full operation via humans + deterministic mechanics (10.7) | no AI data flows | prohibition by law/regulator/works agreement |
| **P1 — platform provider** *(Default)* | provider from our register, operated by us; per-tenant key (KMS §5), tenant separation | our DPA contract covers the tenant; only approved data classes | standard SaaS customer |
| **P2 — customer's own cloud provider** | tenant brings its own provider contract/key; same adapter, different credentials | contract + responsibility rests with the tenant; register approval still mandatory (jurisdiction check) | group framework contract, parent company's provider mandate |
| **P3 — customer's own/local AI** | self-hosted model (on-prem/private cloud) behind the same adapter | data never leaves the organization; the only option for crown-jewel/prohibited classes | data residency laws, high-security/government environment |

**Resolution:** the effective provider = f(tenant profile × scope × capability class × data class × jurisdiction), deterministic, with a defined fallback chain; a fallback may become **more restrictive** (P3→P0), never more permissive (P3→P1 would be a data leak). **Partial permission** is thus the same matrix as §10.2 — merely extended by the provider axis: "translation suggestions allowed via P2, agent autonomy forbidden, document classification only via P3" is a valid, testable state.

### 10.11 · Provider switching & operation (v0.27)

1. **Switching = an act of approval** by the authorized role, with reason + audit (§10.4 object, extended by `provider`).
2. **Regression proof:** a golden-fixture run for every affected capability class against the new adapter — passed before the switch takes effect (no silent behavior change; outputs remain proposals regardless, §2 "proposal ≠ decision").
3. **Conservative restart** (§10.6) after every switch.
4. **Provenance record per call** (§2 v0.27): provider/model/version/class/data class/cost — the basis for compliance evidence to the tenant's regulator and for per-tenant cost control.
5. **A provider outage** is a routing case, not a special case: fallback chain, otherwise P0 degradation with visible status ("AI provider unavailable — manual processing"), never a silent provider switch.

**Reading for the owner:** the provider is a drive belt, not the load-bearing frame. Whether no AI at all (P0), ours (P1), yours (P2), or one in your own basement (P3) — the application, its processes, gates and rights are identical; only the adapter behind them changes. This is the same promise SCLAN gives to hardware, transferred to intelligence.

### 10.12 · Intelligence tiers I0–I3 (v0.28)

*(Author determination: "Machine learning is intelligent code — not intelligent agents and not artificial intelligence." The tiers make configurable: AI yes/no, for which area, exactly where — and ML as its own, mostly permitted tier beneath it.)*

| Tier | Name | what it is | compliance attack surface | switch/governance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **I0** | Deterministic rule/code | routing tables, templates, arithmetic, approval gates, timers — the load-bearing frame | minimal (classic software) | always on; is the fallback of all higher tiers |
| **I1** | Machine learning | statistical models per the **I1 criteria catalog** (§2 v0.28): own data only · runs on the platform/at the tenant · no external data flow · versioned/reproducible · explainable (disclosed factors) · only scores/proposals/forecasts | drastically smaller: no provider, no residency question, explainable — **but:** tier classification does not replace a legal review (§2 v0.28, Lex) | switchable per scope; default **on**; model governance object mandatory |
| **I2** | Generative AI / language models | text/content generation, extraction with world knowledge, translation suggestions via LLM — the provider world | provider register, data-classification gate, residency, P0–P3 (§10.8–§10.11) | full AI switch governance (§10.1–§10.11) |
| **I3** | Agentic AI | agents (§4.1): plan, use tools, act multi-step within autonomy | largest surface: autonomy, rights, escalation | additionally the delegation ladder/A-tiers, agent sentinels (§6 v0.24) |

**Rules of the ladder:**
1. **Label follows capability** (§2 v0.28 [HARD]) — upgrading on a missed criterion is automatic; downgrading never without proof.
2. **Smallest sufficient tier:** where I1 solves the task, no I2/I3 is deployed (an exception requires justification).
3. **Every tier falls back controlled to the one below:** I3→I2→I1→I0; the I0 load-bearing frame always carries (I0 disconnection test).
4. **Matrix integration:** §10.2 receives, per scope × function class, the **maximum I-tier** in addition to state (delegation ladder) and — for I2/I3 — provider (P0–P3). Example of a valid, testable state: *"Organization: max I1 (ML yes, AI no) · exception, translation area: I2 via P2, internal data class only · role accounting: document classification I1, factors disclosed."*

**Model governance object per I1 model (versioned):** `model_id` · `purpose/scope` · `training_data_snapshot` (origin, purpose limitation, time period) · `factors[]` (disclosed, §2 v0.3) · `metrics` (quality, error rates) · `model_version` + reproducibility proof · `golden_fixtures` · `approved_by/on` (attestation) · `legal_classification_ref` (Lex, per jurisdiction) · `review_on`. Retraining = new version = act of approval with a fixture run (pattern §10.11 provider switch).

### 10.13 · ML deployment patterns — making control loops learning-capable (v0.28, refined v0.29)

**Author correction v0.29 ("rule before model"):** It is not ML that closes control loops — **I0 closes every control loop first**; deterministic code and human roles carry the loop from day 1, especially while there isn't yet enough evaluation and training data. I1 is the expansion stage, reached via the **control-loop maturity path**:

| Phase | Carrier | what happens |
|---|---|---|
| **0 — rule-based operation** | I0 + human | control loop runs fully deterministically (tables, thresholds, timers, gates); human decisions are captured, purpose-bound, as future training data (§2 v0.29) |
| **1 — shadow mode** | I0 acts · I1 measures | model runs in parallel, has no effect; its suggestions are measured against the actual human/I0 results (period + metrics per deployment pattern `[OPEN]`) |
| **2 — live operation with guardrail** | I1 proposes · I0 limits | after the data-maturity gate + attested approval, I1 becomes the proposal generator; the I0 rule remains active as a plausibility corridor and immediate fallback |

The following deployment patterns are **target expansion stages** (phase 2) — each row a proposal generator within the existing gate, never a new decision-maker; each begins its life as an I0 rule in phase 0:

| Control loop (existing) | I1 contribution | leads into |
|---|---|---|
| Predictive view/capacity (§2 v0.24, §4.1) | load/bottleneck forecast, deadline-risk scores, single-point-of-failure detection | L0–L4 situation classes, action **proposals** to `REPORT_TO` |
| Task routing & prioritization (§2 v0.13/v0.16/v0.24) | suggestion of the likely best-staffed recipient, effort/duration estimate, priority suggestion | routing table remains the gate; human/rule confirms |
| Triage/arbitration pattern (§6 v0.3) | weights learned from history instead of hand-set ones — factors remain (a) preferences (b) history (c) risks, disclosed | justified proposal, confirmation by the authorized role |
| Anomaly & quality | duplicate/outlier detection (documents, master data, bookings), deviation early warning | verification task within the control loop, never an auto-correction |
| Classification & extraction (structured) | document/category suggestion, field pre-fill from its own historical patterns | proposal field, human takes over/changes |
| Learned routines (§2 v0.24) | statistical detection of "sufficiently comparable, consistently decided cases" as a **routine proposal** | approval object of routine-learning approval — autonomy arises only there |
| Benchmarking/aggregation | band/percentile models on anonymized aggregates (k-anonymity remains a gate) | percentile output, anonymization rules unaffected |

**Distinction of learning:** I1 model training (platform-internal, model governance) ≠ provider model training (external, register field §10.9, forbidden by default) ≠ routine learning approval (autonomy acquisition, §2 v0.24) — three levels, three gates, never mix them.

**Reading for the owner:** the ladder gives you exactly the configurability you need: *AI yes/no, for which area, exactly where* — and beneath it an ML foundation that closes your control loops everywhere and keeps learning, stays in-house, discloses its factors, and delivers strong AI-like functions, while the actual AI governance (provider, data classes, agents) only applies where you actually switch it on.

### 10.14 · Teacher-student distillation — AI teaches, ML works (v0.40)

Relationship between the tiers during **training time** (no runtime intermediate tier — label follows capability): **Phase 0+** — I2 pre-labels existing data as suggestions; human confirms under a sampling regime; only confirmed items become training data (provenance field `label_origin[]`, `teacher_ref` in the model governance object; unconfirmed labels only with a quota + marking). **Phase 1** — data-maturity gate + shadow mode unchanged (distillation accelerates collection, never approval). **Phase 2** — I1 works alone, **the teacher withdraws**: the completion goal of every distillation project is *zero runtime tokens*; retraining on drift as a limited budget event (§16). The data-classification gate also applies to teaching (sensitive data: P3 teacher or human). **Effect:** token rent becomes token investment; fewer AI calls = smaller attack surface; I2 only where free-form speech is genuinely required. Success metric: *tokens per learned capability → 0.*


---

## 11 · Control-Loop Lifecycle & Management Layer    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.30. The layer above all control loops: controllability, handover, care, and visibility. Principle: automation serves — it never runs wild; humans can steer any loop at any time without losing information or learning value. This also resolves the hidden tension in the principle "no task ends in a void": the deliberate status decision of the responsible role **is** a legitimate ending — documented rather than left to die out.)*

### 11.1 · Lifecycle status of every control-loop instance

| Status | Meaning | Effect on automation | Duties |
|---|---|---|---|
| **open/running** | loop works as normal | active per the effective I-tier/delegation | caretaker role monitors; deadlines/escalation apply |
| **paused** | deliberately halted (e.g. lead "will check back after vacation") | **all automation stops immediately**; no reminders, no escalation | **follow-up date mandatory** or an attested open-ended decision; participants informed |
| **aborted** | ended prematurely (process moot) | stop; open subtasks receive an abort receipt | reason + audit; participants informed; learning-value capture |
| **closed** | regularly completed | — | receipt mechanics as before |
| **closed by decision** | declared finished by the responsible role although factually unresolved (e.g. lead never decides, never cancels) | stop; loop counts as ended | reason + audit mandatory; participants informed; information/history remains (hide-not-delete) |
| **deleted/archived** | removed from active inventory | — | only per legal requirements (retention vs. deletion duties, Lex `[OPEN]`); audit of the deletion decision |

**Rules:** exactly one status per instance; status change only by authorized roles (caretaker role always; lower roles within their scope: **stopping is always allowed**, re-opening only if authorized); every change = reason + audit + participant information in one cycle; permitted transitions per role `[OPEN]`.

### 11.2 · AI/ML handover protocol on role change

Automatically generated from configuration (§5 "self-generated artifacts"), acknowledged by the receiving person:

1. **Inventory:** all intelligent functions within the role scope — I-tier, models (model governance reference), autonomy/delegation levels, valid trust grants, running control-loop instances with status.
2. **How it works:** for each function, the disclosed factors, plausibility corridors, connected control loops (opens/runs/closes — reference to the process description §2 v0.30).
3. **Levers:** which parameters this role may adjust/regulate (rights-precise, derived from §4/§10.2) — and which only the leadership level sets.
4. **Do-NOT notes:** what is better left unchanged so as not to open control loops and leave them unobserved (typical traps per function, generated from the process description).
5. **Receipt:** attestation of takeover; **until then, everything autonomous within the scope operates one level more conservatively** (proposal mode).

### 11.3 · Caretaker role & explainability

- Every loop references exactly **one staffed caretaker role** (staffing/deputizing rules apply); orphaned loops escalate immediately.
- The **process description** (opens/runs/closes/consequences-if-left-open) is a mandatory part of every control-loop definition and the source for handover-protocol sections 2 and 4.
- The system **prompts the caretaker role at the right point**: due follow-ups, unhealthy loops (long open, frequently escalated, never closed), missing receipts — in the work context, not in a distant report.

### 11.4 · Management layer (the new upward view)

- **Feed:** exclusively from the status events and health signals of the individual loops (aggregate from individual status — nothing is maintained twice).
- **Health signals per loop type** (thresholds `[OPEN]`): throughput time, escalation rate, share "closed by decision", pause duration, follow-up fidelity — from these, the traffic light "running smoothly / needs support / alarm".
- **Alerting is itself a control loop:** a threshold violation generates a task for the responsible leadership role with a deadline and escalation — dampened (aggregated, not every individual notification), but lossless; the learning value (why paused/aborted/closed by decision) flows as a training-data source into the maturity path (§2 v0.29).
- **The layer's answer:** "Are my processes running? How successfully, where not, where well?" — for every loop, from the marketing funnel through SCLAN maintenance to the smallest internal process.

### 11.5 · Pattern: lead sunset (example of the status rule)

A lead that never reaches a decision but also does not want to cancel is, at some point, no longer pursued: the caretaker role of the lead loop decides **pause** (with a follow-up date), **closed by decision**, or — per legal requirements — **delete/archive**. Automatic nurturing steps stop immediately; the lead does not experience pointless endless contact; the history and the learning value (how such leads unfold) are preserved and feed the management view and future I1 models. Sunset criteria per funnel, project-specific `[OPEN]`.


---

## 12 · Data Governance    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.31. The subject: the datum itself — not the process that uses it. Built following the seven-component pattern of §10/§11.)*

### 12.1 · Governance fields per data-object type
`data_class` (§2 v0.27: public · internal · personal · special categories · crown jewels) · `steward_role` (staffed, deputizing regulated) · `purpose[]` (captured at collection) · `status` (lifecycle 12.2) · `collected_on` · `confirmed_on` + `freshness_threshold` · `retention_until`/`deletion_rule` + `legal_basis_ref` (Lex) · `origin` (source/control loop) · `usages[]` (which processes/models read it — basis for purpose review and data-subject inquiries).

### 12.2 · Data lifecycle
| Status | Meaning | Automation |
|---|---|---|
| **collected** | captured, possibly unconfirmed | freshness timer starts |
| **active/confirmed** | in use, freshness within range | purpose-bound use is free |
| **possibly outdated** | freshness threshold exceeded | visible marking + verification task to the steward |
| **archived** | taken out of active use | read/proof access only; retention timer running |
| **deleted** | removed per legal requirements | deletion proof (audit) remains |

### 12.3 · Data-subject rights as standard control loops
Access · rectification · erasure · portability: every request opens a control loop with the statutory deadline as the timer, caretaker role = steward (+ data-protection role), escalation before the deadline expires. The disclosure draws from `usages[]` — no manual search. Concrete deadlines/duties per jurisdiction: Lex, genuine review `[OPEN]`.

*(v0.47: The deletion control loop receives the **similarity tier** together with a proof dossier and a deletion-confirmation form — §2 v0.47; the duplicate watch at the moment of creation and the grooming control loop feed §12.4 with data-quality metrics.)*

### 12.4 · Management view: data
Freshness traffic light per data pool · open data-subject requests with remaining time · overdue deletions · orphaned objects (steward unstaffed) — aggregated in `REPORT_TO`, alarm as its own control loop (§11.4 mechanics).

---

## 13 · Dependency Governance    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.31. The subject: everything external that operations depend on. The AI provider register §10.9 is, from now on, a special-case view of this register — one register, not two.)*

### 13.1 · Register field catalog (construction §10.9, generalized)
`dependency_id` · `type` (hosting · payment · domain interface e.g. DATEV · certificate/key · library/service · AI provider → special fields §10.9) · `caretaker_role` · `criticality` (13.2) · `contract/dpa_ref` · `expiry_dates[]` (contract, certificate, key — each generates lead-time tasks) · `degradation_path_ref` (13.3, mandatory from K2) · `exit_path_ref` (mandatory from K3) · `status` (evaluated → approved → active → phasing out → ended) · `review_on` · `approved_by/on` (attestation).

### 13.2 · Criticality tiers
| Tier | Meaning | Duties |
|---|---|---|
| **K0** | dispensable/cosmetic | register entry |
| **K1** | disruptive on failure | + active caretaker role, review |
| **K2** | operationally important | + documented, **tested** degradation path (supplier disconnection test) |
| **K3** | existentially critical | + exit/switch path (data export, adapter principle §10.8) + rehearsal duty (§14) |

### 13.3 · Supplier disconnection test & degradation path
For each K2/K3: "X fails" → defined degradation (which functions keep running, which pause with a visible status, who is informed, which replacement path applies) — the same figure of thought as the internet disconnection test (§5) and the AI/I0 disconnection test (§10): **a valuable addition with defined degradation, never a single point of failure.** Proof through a real rehearsal (§14), not paper alone.

### 13.4 · Management view: dependencies
Expiry radar (next 90 days) · criticality map · rehearsal status per K2/K3 · expiring entries without a successor decision — aggregated, alarm control loop.

---

## 14 · Emergency Governance — "The Rehearsed Emergency"    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.31. The difference between "we would have a plan" and "we can do it". Subject: the state of emergency itself.)*

### 14.1 · Rehearsal control loop
For each critical degradation (AI off · internet off · K2/K3 supplier off · restart) a rehearsal calendar: real, announced or partly-announced drill → measurement (does the degradation hold? timings? gaps?) → **finding tasks** to the caretaker roles → next date. An overdue rehearsal = escalation (follow-up-date principle §11). Rehearsal frequency per criticality `[OPEN]`.

### 14.2 · Incident lifecycle
| Status | Role/deadline | Duty |
|---|---|---|
| **reported** | any role may report; emergency role takes over within the deadline | initial situation documented |
| **contained** | emergency role | effect limited; participants informed (§11 mechanics) |
| **resolved** | domain/caretaker role | normal operation confirmed; conservative restart if applicable (§10.6) |
| **learned** | caretaker role + leadership | **learning entry mandatory:** cause, adjustment (rule/sentinel/model/rehearsal); without it, no closure |

### 14.3 · Emergency roles & offline plan
Emergency roles and deputies named in advance (staffing rules §4); the **emergency plan** (roles, reachability, first measures, short-form degradation paths) exists as a current **offline artifact** — exported/printed, with an as-of date and its own freshness control loop (§12.2 mechanics applied to the plan itself). The plan for "system off" never lives only in the system.

### 14.4 · Learning from the emergency
Learning entries flow back: new/tightened sentinels (§6), adjusted degradation paths (§13.3), rehearsal scenarios (§14.1), training data for I1 early warning (§10.13 anomaly) — the emergency is the most expensive, most valuable training-data source; wasting it is forbidden.

### 14.5 · Management view: emergency
Rehearsal fidelity (planned vs. carried out) · open finding tasks · incident metrics (time per status change, recurrence causes `[OPEN]` thresholds) · plan freshness — aggregated, alarm control loop.


---

## 15 · Identity & Rights Governance (Joiner–Mover–Leaver)    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.32. The subject: identity and its rights over time. The rights matrix (§4) remains the source of WHAT a role may do — §15 governs WHEN a person effectively holds that role. Seven-component pattern.)*

### 15.1 · Identity lifecycle
| Status | Meaning | Effect on rights | Duties |
|---|---|---|---|
| **incoming** | joiner in onboarding | staged per receipt (15.2) | onboarding control loop running; sponsor role named |
| **active** | regular assignment | full per roles + recertification | confirmation dates current |
| **changing** | mover between roles | diff: old ones end, new ones begin; conservative until handover receipt (§11.2) | handover protocol + rights diff |
| **outgoing** | leaver until cutoff date | phasing out; sensitive rights possibly reduced early (`[OPEN]` per case) | offboarding checklist; orphaning lock (15.4) |
| **deactivated/archived** | after cutoff date | none; account unable to log in | audit/proofs remain; no deletion of identity history except per legal requirements (§12) |

### 15.2 · Joiner — staged onboarding
Onboarding is a control loop (caretaker role: the area's leadership role; sponsor role assists): stages such as basic access → domain access → write/approval rights → full delegation breadth, **each stage against a receipt** (briefing, acknowledgment of the role's handover protocol §11.2, training proof where applicable). Deadlines + escalation like any loop; a stuck onboarding becomes visible, not forgotten.

### 15.3 · Mover — diff & handover
Reference §11.2 (handover protocol) — supplemented by the rights side: the change generates the **rights diff** (comes/goes/stays-time-limited), effective atomically at the moment of change; caretaker roles/stewardships migrate explicitly (orphaning lock also applies here). Deputizing assignments are temporary with an expiry, not permanent possession.

### 15.4 · Leaver — offboarding with orphaning lock
The offboarding checklist is generated from configuration (§5) and automatically contains: all caretaker roles (§11), data stewardships (§12), dependency responsibilities (§13), open control-loop instances, keys/devices/certificates (§13 register), knowledge handover (leaver variant of the handover protocol). **Deactivation is only possible once every position has been handed over with a receipt.** Rights end on the cutoff date automatically — never "forgotten active".

### 15.5 · Recertification
Periodic confirmation of every rights assignment by the leadership role (interval per rights criticality `[OPEN]`; sensitive rights more frequently). Sequence: overdue → conservative restriction (e.g. read/proposal mode) + task → persistently overdue → revocation + escalation. Confirmation is a one-click control loop in the management view, not a bureaucratic battle.

### 15.6 · Break-glass (emergency access)
Predefined scenarios (`[OPEN]` catalog, related to §14 rehearsals), time-limited access, full auditing, immediate notification of the leadership role, automatic incident review (§14.2). Break-glass is rehearsed like any degradation (§14.1) — an emergency access that no one can operate is not one.

### 15.7 · Management view: identity & rights
Rights map per area · overdue recertifications · stuck on-/offboardings · temporary rights near expiry · break-glass usages · accounts without activity (`[OPEN]` threshold) — aggregated in `REPORT_TO`, alarm control loop (§11.4 mechanics).


---

## 16 · Budget/Cost Governance    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.33. The subject: money as a running variable of the automation. No second set of books — the costs come from the individual events that already exist.)*

### 16.1 · Budget object
`scope` (organization → area → role → automation/function class) · `period` · `amount/units` · `caretaker_role` · `thresholds[]` (e.g. 70/90/100% `[OPEN]` defaults) · `degradation_chain` (normal → throttled → proposal mode → off) · `status` · audit per change. Sources: AI provenance records (§2 v0.27), call/consumption events — aggregate from individual status, nothing recorded twice.

### 16.2 · Staged degradation
Like the AI switch, only economically triggered: threshold 1 → yellow light + information; threshold 2 → throttling (quotas, batch instead of immediate); exhaustion → proposal-only mode or off, visible status ("budget exhausted — manual processing"), decision task to the caretaker role. Never silent continuation, never a hard death mid-process (running processes are handed over in an orderly manner, §10.5 pattern).

### 16.3 · Management view: costs
Burn-rate traffic light per scope · period-end forecast (I1 candidate, maturity path!) · biggest contributors · degradation events — in `REPORT_TO`, alarm control loop.

### 16.4 · Cost maxim (v0.40)
**"Tokens only arise where free-form speech happens."** I0/I1 (gates, routing, templates, classification, forecasting, style/spelling watch) = token-free; I2/I3 (free-form speaking/planning) = budgeted token rent; distillation (§10.14) converts rent into a one-time investment. The strict parser ahead of the AI layer (v0.12) and the smallest sufficient tier (v0.28) are the built-in cost brakes.

### 16.5 · Decision monetization — "what is delegated is what is paid for" (v0.40)
| Unit | Tier | Marginal cost | Pricing logic |
|---|---|---|---|
| Check (sentinel/gate) | I0 | ~zero | included in the plan — gates are the trust, not sold individually |
| Suggestion/score/forecast | I1 | ~zero after training investment | quotas per plan/headroom — the margin carrier |
| Comment/review (avatar speaks) | I2 | token pass-through | measured per call, budget-capped by customer config |
| delegated routine decision | I3/A3+ | small | premium per execution — **only** within the customer's trust grant |

The customer's switch matrix is at the same time their price configurator; the delegation click is an act of trust, approval, and order in one. Billing exclusively as an aggregate from provenance records (auditable down to the individual call); budget degradation = built-in customer protection; the **distillation dividend** is shared (aligning interests: we earn on the maturity path, the customer saves on it). **Hard limits:** §2 v0.40 [HARD] — never sell "the decision" as a legal act; unit types only with attested Lex approval per jurisdiction (HR = high risk); liability allocation of delegated routines contractually `[OPEN]` (Lex).

---

## 17 · Reporting/Whistleblower Governance    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.33. The subject: the protected voice. The special control loop — shielded rather than aggregated upward. At the same time a Humpl-module candidate: legal details per jurisdiction via Lex, only the mechanics here.)*

### 17.1 · The shielded loop
Intake (named or anonymous; anonymous return channel via a case code) → intake confirmation (deadline timer, value via Lex) → review/measures by the **confidentiality role** → feedback (deadline timer, value via Lex) → closure with a learning entry (anonymized). Escalation on deadline risk remains **within the shielding** (deputy confidentiality role, never line management).

### 17.2 · Shielding architecture
Its own data class "report" (§12) · access exclusively by the confidentiality role(s) · no person linkage outside it · management view exclusively k-anonymously aggregated (count, categories, throughput times; automatic widening for small groups — benchmark pattern) · retention/deletion of cases per legal requirements (§12.3, Lex).

### 17.3 · Confidentiality role
Staffing + deputizing arranged in advance (staffing rules §4), explicitly **outside** the line chain of command for this subject matter (chain-of-command-seam exception, documented); its own kind of training/onboarding stage (§15.2). Conflict of interest per case → deputy path.

---

## 18 · Knowledge Governance — "Knowledge Ages"    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.33. The subject: the organization's documented knowledge. The master already lives this layer — changelog, review, time machine; here it is generalized.)*

### 18.1 · Knowledge-artifact register (lightweight)
`artifact_id` · `type` (guide · process/procedure description · template · training · rulebook) · `caretaker_role` · `as_of_date` · `review_interval` · `source` (generated from configuration ↔ hand-maintained) · `references[]` (where it appears — reference instead of copy) · `status` (current · possibly outdated · being revised · archived).

### 18.2 · Freshness & gaps
Overdue review → marking + verification task (pattern §12.2). Detected gaps — from incidents ("documentation was missing"), handovers ("protocol section empty"), onboarding feedback — become tasks with a role + deadline. **Findability at the place of work:** knowledge appears in the work context (linked at the control loop, at the form, at the function — "software comes to the user"), not only in a distant filing location.

### 18.4 · Freshness anchor — freshness beyond the system boundary (v0.37)
The anchor (§2 v0.37) extends knowledge freshness to **exported copies** (paper, PDF, mailings): the printed document is a derived artifact and would otherwise silently age — the QR code is its derivation sentinel (the same figure of thought as the EN edition of the master, §2 v0.34/v0.36). Building blocks: anchor policy per document type in template management (MANDATORY · OPTIONAL · FREE) · public status endpoint (only current/outdated + version/date, never content, honest degradation) · anonymous scan counting as a usage signal → freshness prioritization (frequently checked = shorter review interval) and the "actively used" view in 18.3. QR generation inline-embedded (§5 v0.3 pattern, like the demo QR bridge v0.25).

### 18.5 · Knowledge retrieval layer (RAG) — the knowledge base becomes queryable (v0.46)
**Flow:** question → **retrieval** (classic search + semantic similarity/embeddings = I1, rights-filtered at the endpoint) → **assembly** (chunks into a locked-down RAG prompt, v0.40) → **generation** (I2 behind the adapter, budgeted) → answer **with source-chunk citations** (artifact + version). **Building blocks:** index as a derived artifact with freshness coupling (source version stamp, reindex on approval) · rights inheritance source→chunk with query-time filter [HARD] · citation duty with a "freely spoken" marking · degradation to plain reference search (AI-off/budget/provider outage) · distillation bridge for frequent stable questions (§10.14) · data-classification gate before external generation. **First use case (candidate):** the master itself as a queryable knowledge base for the developer team (DE lead edition as source, EN answers via the recipient-language rule). Chunking/embedding method `[OPEN]` (own I1 model vs. provider — register decision).

### 18.3 · Management view: knowledge
Freshness rate per area · open gap tasks · most-used vs. never-used artifacts (archive candidates) — aggregated, alarm control loop.

---

## 19 · Outside-Voice Governance    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.33. The subject: every voice from outside — complaint, ticket, feedback, review, reason for cancellation. The outward counterpart to Humpl's internal satisfaction measurement.)*

### 19.1 · Intake & loop
Every voice → loop instance (§11 status model) with category, caretaker role (per channel/topic), deadline. Triage following the existing pattern (§6 triage/arbitration; I1 classification suggestion as the target expansion stage, maturity path). Duplicate/repetition detection links voices into a topic loop instead of handling them individually.

### 19.2 · Learning duty & feedback
Closure only with a categorized, anonymizable **learning entry**; collected findings flow into product to-dos, training (§18), and I1 early warning. **Feedback to the sender** belongs to the loop — what was changed, or why deliberately not (a justified refusal is documented). The closed control loop thereby becomes bidirectional: the outsider, too, learns of the ending.

### 19.3 · Management view: outside voices
Voice traffic light (volume, categories, recurring topics, time-to-response, time-to-closure) · share "closed with feedback" · topic trends (`[OPEN]` thresholds) — the question "Are my processes running?" answered from the outside, on equal footing alongside the inside view (§11.4).

## 20 · The Interplay — Human, Roles, Agents, Twins, Avatars, Sentinels (Synergies & Coexistence)    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.38, title & human primacy v0.39. **The human stands before all actor types** — they are the sovereign who fills roles, carries avatars, and grants trust; the five actor types are individually defined in §3, §4.1, §6, §7, and §2 v0.24 — this chapter defines nothing twice; it shows how they work together, where their boundaries lie, and how humans staff them. Guiding image: **four pairs of eyes of different natures around one human decision-maker.**)*

### 20.1 · The human and the five actor types at a glance

| Actor | Nature | may | may never | defined in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Human** | the sovereign — the sole source of responsibility, trust, approval, and value/boundary decisions (§2 v0.1) | fill roles (§15), carry avatars, grant and revoke trust, stop any control loop at any time (§11) | be replaced, disempowered, or turned into a rubber stamp | §2 v0.1, §10 guiding principle, §11, §15 |
| **Role (humanly staffed)** | the human's garment: bearer of responsibility within the organizational structure | decide, approve, grant trust, set boundaries, change status | hand off responsibility to machines | §3, §4, §15 |
| **Agent** | actor on behalf of another | plan, prepare, execute within the delegation level and machine rights | approve, pose as a human, operate a switch (not even its own), create rights | §4.1, §2 v0.24 |
| **Digital role twin** | the **personal agent instance** per person × role × organization | carry the context of exactly *one* person in exactly *one* role and prepare work for them | mix contexts, approve in someone else's name | §2 v0.24 |
| **Avatar** | advisory perspective, embodied voice | advise, check *from a viewpoint*, name concerns, be a review checklist | approve, be a gate, execute | §7 |
| **Sentinel** | mechanical verification gate | check true/false, block, generate finding tasks | advise, weigh, decide, pose as a user | §6 |

**Maxim of coexistence:** *The human is the sovereign — the role is accountable, the agent acts, the twin personalizes, the avatar advises, the sentinel blocks.* Every actor carries exactly **one** of these natures — label follows capability (§2 v0.28), category discipline (§1 rule 9).

**The relationship avatar ⟷ sentinel is n:m:** an avatar can represent **none, one, or several** sentinels — the perspective is singular, its enforceable consequences are many (*Tekton* represents six hardware gates; *Chronos* the freshness/legal-status sentinel and the freshness anchor v0.37). And **a sentinel can embody several perspectives at once** (the AI data-flow/residency sentinel enforces both Vera's data minimization *and* Lex's jurisdictional permissibility — two voices, one gate). Initial mapping: 20.7.

### 20.2 · The interplay within the control loop (the normal case)

1. **The human in the role** sets the goal, rights, delegation level, and — where needed — the trust grant (§2 v0.30). Without them, nothing effective begins.
2. **The person's digital role twin** takes up the process in *their* context: their preferences, their open cases, their organization — never someone else's.
3. **The agent** prepares the work: gathers, classifies (I1 suggestion), drafts, plans — exactly up to the effective level of the switch matrix (§10.2), not one step further.
4. **Avatars** appear in the review: Lex asks about the legal situation, Vera about data minimization, Aria about accessibility — each voice exactly one perspective, each as advice, none as a gate. *(With AI-off, the same voices become human review checklists, §10.1 — the perspective remains, only its carrier changes.)*
5. **Sentinels** lock the gates: true or false, without discretion. A failure generates a finding task within the control loop, never a silent pass-through.
6. **The human in the role** decides and approves (proposal ≠ decision); the approval is attested wherever software cannot itself verify correctness (§2 v0.5).
7. **The learning control loop** closes: the human decision becomes — purpose-bound — training data (§2 v0.29); the `REPORT_TO` layer sees the aggregate (§11.4).

### 20.3 · Coexistence boundaries (what never blurs)

- **An avatar never becomes a sentinel** — a perspective may voice concern but never block. Three activities are permitted to the avatar in dealing with "its" gates, none of them is operating: **(a) sponsor of new gates** — should an avatar's concern need to become enforceable, a new sentinel with a testable rule arises from it; the avatar remains the voice, the gate takes over the blocking (pattern Aria ⟷ contrast sentinel); **(b) interpreter of findings** — if one of its gates fails, the perspective provides the framing in review (*why* it matters, not just *that* it is red); **(c) checklist bearer under AI-off** — all gates of its perspective that degrade to human attestation (§10.1) appear as check items on its review checklist. What the avatar **never** does: operate, open, override, or itself lock the gate.
- **An agent never becomes a role:** machine rights arise only through explicit grant per verb (§4.1), never by inheriting human role rights; `acting_for_person` is an assignment, not an identity.
- **A twin never becomes a collection point:** one person, one role, one organization per instance — context mixing is the forbidden state.
- **A sentinel never becomes an advisor:** a gate that voices "recommendations" is mislabeled; recommendations belong to an avatar or to an I1 suggestion within the control loop.
- **The human never becomes a rubber stamp:** a role that merely nods things through is an alarm signal for the control regime (sampling, §10.6) — approval is verification, not a gesture.

### 20.4 · Synergies (why five types are more than five tools)

- **Degradation synergy:** if one type fails, the next carries — AI-off turns avatars into checklists and AI sentinels into human attestation; the gate stays, the checker changes (§10). The system knows no actor whose failure would paralyze it.
- **Scaling synergy:** *one* versioned role profile (§2 v0.24) feeds any number of twins — expertise is maintained once and personalized a thousand times over, without contexts touching each other.
- **Proof synergy:** avatar advice + human attestation + sentinel log together form the audit chain: *who* was warned, *who* checked, *what* was enforced — three independent traces of the same process.
- **Learning synergy:** sentinel findings, avatar remarks, and human corrections are three separate training-data sources for the maturity path (§10.13) — tomorrow's agent learns from today's interplay.

### 20.5 · Human staffing (who fills what)

| Actor | Staffing | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| **Role** | always humanly staffed or guaranteed via deputizing/escalation (up to the top) | §2 v0.16/v0.24; §15 |
| **Agent/twin** | never "staffed" — but always with a human responsible party (`acting_for_person`) and a role frame | §4.1 |
| **Avatar** | is *carried*: speaking AI-supported (I2) **or** played by a human as a review checklist — the attestation of the check is human in both cases | §7, §2 v0.5, §10.1 |
| **Sentinel** | never staffed — but **maintained**: every gate has a caretaker role (application sentinels → developer role, infrastructure sentinels → server crew) | §11, §2 v0.35 |

Plus the two rules from §2 v0.38: **No gate without care, no perspective without a bearer** · **One person, many hats, clear hat changes** (wording and testable clauses in §2).

### 20.6 · One process, played through (miniature)

A document arrives → the **twin** of the accountant takes it into her context → the **agent** classifies via I1 (suggestion, factors disclosed) → the **accountant** confirms (proposal ≠ decision) → the **mandatory-fields sentinel** checks the gate; a finding would generate a finding task → on legal uncertainty, **Lex** speaks up in review ("confirm with an actual tax advisor") and the check is **attested** → `REPORT_TO` sees only the aggregate → the decision becomes purpose-bound **training data**. Five natures, one control loop, no boundary violation.

### 20.7 · Initial mapping avatar ⟷ sentinel (n:m — proposal, author confirmation pending)

*(Puts the open ToDo from v0.6 into a table for the first time. Only documented/obvious mappings; nothing invented — the rest remains `[OPEN]`.)*

| Avatar | represents (initial mapping) |
|---|---|
| **Prof** | aggregate-consistency, provenance-fidelity, recalc/reference-integrity checks; rule-coverage/coverage-board sentinel (v0.35) |
| **Lingua** | i18n completeness; EN-derivation sentinel (v0.34/v0.36, incl. attestation) |
| **Coach** | triage/arbitration traceability; candidate: management-layer sentinel (§11.4) `[OPEN]` |
| **Aegis** | injection/abuse-protection, route-guard, break-glass sentinel (v0.32); candidate: reporting-channel shielding (§17) `[OPEN]` |
| **Aria** | contrast sentinel; demo header-band/showcase readability |
| **Vera** | data-flow/residency (with Lex), data-steward, deletion-control-loop, scan-telemetry checks (v0.37); lead data minimization |
| **Lex** | go-live/mandatory-fields gate, provider approval (with Vera), whistleblower deadlines (§17), data-subject-rights deadlines (§12.3) |
| **Chronos** | freshness/legal-status sentinel, expiry follow-up (§13), data/knowledge freshness (§12/§18), freshness anchor (v0.37) |
| **Tekton** | target, recovery, hardware-approval, E2E, offline, cross-platform sentinel; candidate: deploy gate/schema drift (v0.20 ToDo) `[OPEN]` |
| *(unrepresented)* | AI-switch/disconnection-test/tier family (§10), control-loop status/caretaker role (§11), rehearsal/incident family (§14), identity family (§15), budget (§16) — **missing avatars is an author decision, see ToDo** |

### 20.8 · The avatar perspective profile — where the avatar's intelligence lives (v0.40)

**Answer to the author's question "Who decides when an avatar must worry — and where does its intelligence live?":** (1) *When* it appears is decided by **I0** — the applicability trigger of its profile (set by the human: "financial figures → Lex") or a red gate of its representation; never the model, never ad hoc. (2) *Where* the concern lives: in the **versioned perspective profile** — `embodied_rules[]` · `check_questions/checklist` · `applicability_trigger` · `represented_sentinels[]` (20.7) · `known_failure_patterns` · `carrier_mode` + attesting person · `learning_inflow` · version + approval. The carrier (I2 or human) only speaks the profile; **under AI-off, the avatar loses nothing but speed** — the human checklist *is* the profile (the perspective remains, the carrier changes — sister to the phrase "the gate stays, the checker changes"). (3) *Where* new concerns come from: from the learning control loop (sentinel findings, review corrections, incident learning, outside voices) — but never silently: a new check question = a new profile version with approval; concerns that become enforceable migrate into the gate as a sponsored sentinel (20.3a).

## 21 · Universe-Mail — the Rule-Mirroring Application    [GLOBAL core]

*(New in v0.41. Email is the least disciplined medium in the business world — precisely why the Universe builds its own mail software: online and offline, and every Universe rule is mirrored in it. Core sentence: **email is a channel of the control-loop system, not a second filing cabinet.** Protocol/stack choice deliberately `[OPEN]` — nothing invented.)*

### 21.1 · Inbound
Verification before effect (§2 v0.7 [HARD]: no status flip from mail text; HMAC action links or a verified sender) · **case binding** via two anchors (`[REF]` subject + sub-addressing) · unbound business mail → assignment queue (control loop with a deadline) · triage as an **I1 suggestion** (category, priority, recipient role — factors disclosed, maturity path from phase 0 counting rules) · outside-voice mails (complaint, inquiry) open their §19 loop · duplicate/thread detection links rather than multiplies.

### 21.2 · Outbound
Composing with the Universe command language (`+Tag:` syntax, v0.12) and templates from the template system · **communication verification lane** (v0.40) per the template's verification profile — style/spelling I1 (token-free), legal/domain-accuracy I2 via locked-down prompts (budgeted) · **recipient language** (Lingua rule: transactional message in the recipient's language) · **mandatory signature** as a MANDATORY dimension (business mandatory disclosures per jurisdiction, Lex) · draft provenance (human/AI/mixed) on the object · approval gate before sending (v0.2); freshness anchor on attached documents per their template policy (v0.37).

### 21.3 · Offline operation
Local store: read, search, compose fully offline · I0/I1 verification dimensions local (the style DB resides at the tenant) · **outbound queue visible** with status per mail and a receipt control loop · MANDATORY I2 dimension offline: human attestation or sending waits (documented per profile) · internet disconnection test as acceptance criterion (core functions hold up without a connection).

### 21.4 · AI/ML in mail — everything switched
Summaries, reply drafts, translation suggestions = I2 behind the switch matrix (off/comments/proposes per scope), through the **data-classification gate** (crown-jewel/confidential content never reaches external providers — P3 or not at all), budgeted (§16, degradation to a checklist instead of a cost explosion) · triage/prioritization/anomaly ("this payment-request sender is new") = I1 · visible AI status in the composer (§2 v0.26).

### 21.5 · Retention & security
Business mail = data object (§12): class, steward, retention/deletion per jurisdiction (Lex) as a control loop · encryption at rest/in transit, tenant keys (KMS) · audit per write operation (v0.4) · mailbox access rights-precise (the technical role sees system state, never business content — v0.7).

### 21.6 · Management view: mail
Response times per channel/role · assignment-queue health · outbound-queue status · share of verified sends · retention overdue items — aggregate from individual status, alarm control loop (§11.4). Migration of existing mailboxes via the old→new path (v0.35): real lab, gap list, no hard cutover.

(Author decision 2026-07-13.)

*(Carry-over from v0.3 — unchanged, still open)*

- **Duplicate (related):** §5 "provenance labeling of reference data" ⟷ §2 "prefer the superordinate source" + §5 "staged handover". → Adopted as a refinement.

Its own computation-integrity sentinel.

Both kept; relationship noted.

*(New in v0.24)*

- **Refinement:** §2 v0.13 process control loop + "open is not a dead end" becomes the complete closed loop with permitted end states, a closing acknowledgment, and escalation up to the board chair; not a duplicate rule but the closing of the previously open completion side.
- **Category conflict resolved:** the previous placeholders "avatar" and "sentinel" in §3/§4 both treated them like roles. v0.24 separates them: agent = acts with its own machine rights; avatar = advisory; sentinel = check finding; human/role = responsibility/approval.
- **New orthogonal relationship:** `REPORT_TO` is not a delegation/escalation and does not replace `ASSIGNED_TO`; it is the parallel information/oversight line. Takeover or a change of responsibility remains its own audited process.

Author confirmation of this interpretation requested.

*(New in v0.27)*
- **Pattern transfer (no conflict):** §10.8 adapter architecture ⟷ §5 SCLAN portability architecture [HARD] ("domain core testable with test adapters; swapping an adapter changes no business logic"). → The same thought pattern transferred from hardware/transport to AI providers; the testability clause deliberately phrased in the same structure.

- **Duplicate (instance):** §10.10 fallback "more restrictive, never more permissive" ⟷ §2 v0.26 "the most restrictive tier in the chain wins" + §2 v0.6 "restrictive default / human-granted opening". → The same principle on the provider axis.
- **Duplicate (instance):** §2 v0.27 provenance disclosure ⟷ §2 v0.4 "forensics via audit trail" + §4.1 "audit disclosure". → The call-level instance of the same provability principle; cost evaluation as "aggregate from individual status".

- **Duplicate (generalization):** I0 separation test ⟷ AI separation test (v0.26). → The AI separation test is the special case "I2/I3 off"; the I0 separation test generalizes to "everything above I0 off". Both sentinels remain (different triggers/acceptance criteria), kinship noted.

- **Duplicate (instance):** "smallest sufficient tier" ⟷ §2 v0.6 "restrictive default" + §2 v0.26 "most restrictive tier wins". → The same principle as a selection rule for the technology itself.

- **Correction (author, documented openly):** §2 v0.28 "ML closes control loops (default deployment)" ⟷ author's correction "deterministic code closes control loops too — and is preferable at the start, when there isn't yet enough evaluation/training data". → The v0.28 rule refined inline to "target maturity tier"; new [HARD] rule "I0 closes every control loop first"; no silent rewrite (the changelog carries both versions).
- **Duplicate (instance):** "rule before model" ⟷ §2 v0.28 "smallest sufficient tier". → The same selection rule, extended along the time axis: the smallest sufficient tier is, at the start, almost always I0.

- **Duplicate (instance):** "I0 remains the guardrail" ⟷ §10.12 ladder rule 3 "every tier falls back in a controlled way to the one below" + §2 v0.26 safety net. → Made concrete as a plausibility corridor with a gap-free fallback.
- **Duplicate (instance):** "I0 operation generates the training data" ⟷ learning control loop (§2 v0.24: human decisions feed the learning process) + v0.24 privacy boundaries. → The same source, now explicitly framed as the data-collection phase of the maturity path, with a purpose-limitation duty.

*(New in v0.30)*
- **Tension resolved (reported and resolved):** §2 v0.24 "closed control loop / no task ends in limbo" ⟷ author's requirement "be able to stop/pause/declare any loop closed at any time". → No contradiction: the responsible role's deliberate, audited status decision **is** a legitimate ending — the status "closed by decision" documents the end, rather than letting the loop run dead.
- **Duplicate (pattern):** status change with reason + audit + immediate effect + notification of participants ⟷ AI-switch mechanics (§10.4/§10.5). → Deliberately the same form factor; one operating pattern for both switches and loops.
- **Duplicate (instance):** trust grant ⟷ data-maturity gate (§2 v0.29) + routine learning approval (§2 v0.24) + "proposal ≠ decision". → Data maturity = a technical condition, trust grant = a human condition; both are necessary, neither replaces the other.
- **Duplicate (instance):** handover protocol ⟷ §5 "self-generated artifacts from configuration" + safety net (§2 v0.26: cautious until acknowledged). → Generation instead of hand-maintenance; no new documentation silo.
- **Duplicate (instance):** caretaker role ⟷ "responsible role instead of person" + staffing/backup rules + directive seam (§4). → Applied to control loops as the subject; orphaned loops use the existing escalation mechanics.
- **Duplicate (instance):** management layer ⟷ §2 v0.24 "aggregate from individual status" + `REPORT_TO` dashboards + "escalation dampened, information retained". → The v0.24 thought pattern is expanded into its own layer with health signals and an alert control loop — no second data source, only the status events.
- **Duplicate (instance):** "prompts at the right place" ⟷ §2 v0.7 "software comes to the user" / context-proximate tasks. → Applied to the loop's caretaker role.

*(New in v0.31)*
- **Duplicate resolution (merger):** AI provider register (§10.9) ⟷ dependency register (§13.1). → §10.9 becomes a special-case view of the general register (type "AI provider" with additional fields); one register, not two; existing §10.9 rules remain valid unchanged.
- **Pattern completion:** supplier separation test (§13.3) ⟷ internet separation test (§5) + AI/I0 separation test (§10). → A third instance of the same thought pattern; §14.1 turns all three into recurring drills instead of one-off acceptance tests.
- **Duplicate (instance):** data steward / dependency-caretaker role / emergency role ⟷ §2 v0.30 "caretaker role per control loop" + "responsible role instead of person". → The same component applied to three new subjects.
- **Duplicate (instance):** "possibly stale" (data freshness §12.2) + expiry timer (§13) + drill/plan freshness (§14) ⟷ §2 v0.30 "paused without a follow-up date is forbidden". → One principle — nothing ages unobserved — in four manifestations.
- **Duplicate (generalization):** purpose limitation at the object level (§12) ⟷ training-data purpose limitation (§2 v0.29) + data-class gate (§2 v0.27). → Generalized from the special case (training data, AI call) to all uses; existing rules become instances.
- **Duplicate (instance):** incident status model (§14.2) ⟷ control-loop status form factor (§11.1); learning duty ⟷ preservation of learning value (§2 v0.30) + training data (§2 v0.29). → Same form factors, new subject; learning feedback as a duty instead of an option.
- **Duplicate (instance):** offline emergency plan ⟷ §5 offline-first. → The principle applied to its own failure scenario: the plan for "system down" never lives only inside the system.
- **Delineation (reported):** data-subject-rights deadlines (§12.3) are jurisdiction-specific legal knowledge → Lex/actual legal review; the master carries only the control-loop mechanics (timer, escalation), no invented deadlines.

*(New in v0.32)*
- **Delineation (not a second rights model):** §15 ⟷ rights matrix (§4). → §4 = WHAT a role may do; §15 = WHEN a person actually holds it; §15 creates no new right types, only lifecycle governance over the assignments.
- **Thread completion (not a duplicate):** mover (§15.3) ⟷ handover protocol (§11.2). → §15 adds only the rights-diff side; the protocol remains the one handover form factor.
- **Duplicate (instance):** orphan lock ⟷ §2 v0.30 "caretaker role per loop" + orphaned-loop escalation (§11/§12/§13). → The same orphaning logic, bound preemptively to the exit event instead of discovered reactively.
- **Duplicate (instance):** recertification ⟷ "nothing ages unobserved" (follow-up date v0.30 · data freshness/expiry timer/drill fidelity v0.31). → The fifth manifestation of the same principle, now applied to rights.
- **Duplicate (instance):** staged onboarding + cautious mode until acknowledgment ⟷ safety net (§2 v0.26) + handover-protocol acknowledgment (v0.30). → "New = cautious" applied to a person's entry.
- **Duplicate (instance):** break-glass review ⟷ incident lifecycle (§14.2) + drill duty (§14.1); "deactivated, never deleted" ⟷ hide-not-delete + §12 deletion rules (identity history only per legal requirement). → Existing mechanics, new trigger.

*(New in v0.33)*
- **Duplicate (pattern):** budget degradation chain (§16.2) ⟷ AI-switch tiers (§10.2) + P0 degradation (§10.10) + orderly handover (§10.5). → The same control mechanism, triggered economically rather than by governance; no new switch form factor.
- **Duplicate (reuse):** cost capture (§16.1) ⟷ AI provenance disclosure (§2 v0.27) + "aggregate from individual status" (§2 v0.24). → No second bookkeeping system; the budget reads what is logged anyway.
- **Duplicate (pattern):** reporting-channel aggregation (§17.2) ⟷ benchmark anonymization (k-anonymity, broadening, anti-differencing). → Deliberately the same protection form factor; one anonymization standard across the organization.
- **Delineation (reported):** the §17 confidentiality role stands, for this subject matter, outside the line-management directive chain — a documented **exception** to the directive seam (§4); not a contradiction, but a deliberate special path with its own backup chain.
- **Duplicate (instance):** knowledge freshness (§18) ⟷ "nothing ages unobserved" (sixth manifestation) + "one source"/reference instead of copy (§2/§5) + "software comes to the user" (§2 v0.7 → discoverability at the workplace). → Existing principles, new subject; the master itself is the reference implementation.
- **Duplicate (instance):** outside-voices loop (§19) ⟷ control-loop form factor (§11) + lead routing (§11.5) + incident learning duty (§14.2) + triage (§6). → A composition of existing building blocks; the only new part is the bidirectional closure (feedback going back out).
- **Lex delineation (reported):** whistleblower deadlines/applicability (§17) are jurisdiction-specific legal knowledge → an actual lawyer; the master carries only the timer mechanics.

Additionally, the artifact records that only MARES/Billing/Work Hub are documented as actually existing; all other apps mentioned (PitchAPP, Marketing Universe, CRM, Service Desk, lex_legal, club applications ...) are `STATUS TO BE CONFIRMED`/idea — none was assumed to already exist. - 2026-07-18 · v0.53 · **Payment audit as a human control loop + knowledge-gap control loop (Sivaji's objection: "don't rely 100% on automation/technology for payments and billing — aside from the legal aspect we need manual review capacity").** Response: the concern was already structurally provided for (§4.1 agent/role separation, §20.2 control-loop normal case, §20.3 "the human never becomes a pass-through," §10.6 control regime/spot checks, §5 v0.3 two-person check on incoming payments) — but never applied to **periodic payment/billing audits** (weekly/monthly, not just individual bookings). **New Golden Rule (§5):** `[HARD]` **Payment/billing audit as a periodic human control loop** — every billing period goes through a deterministic Sentinel integrity run **and** a genuine human inspection (at minimum a spot check, full review on anomaly) by the responsible role, witnessed, never a rubber stamp; agents prepare, they never replace the review. **§6:** Payment/billing-audit sentinel `[HARD]`. Extends §5 v0.3, an instance of §10.6 + §20.3 — nothing duplicated. **Second new Golden Rule (§5, from the same work session):** `[STANDARD]` **Knowledge-gap control loop (FAQ/help request)** — an FAQ answer not found, or negative article feedback, is not a dead-end event but generates a knowledge-gap task (separate from bug/feature/custom-development tickets) for the content-maintenance role; done only once a published article closing the gap exists. Instance of §2 v0.13 (task/to-do list per role) + "open is not a dead end," applied to the knowledge base itself. First implementation: Humpl/Marès Help Center with three-way escalation (problem/feature/custom development) + a knowledge-gap reporting path. ToDo extended by two v0.53 items. Source: exclusively this chat (Sivaji's objection, relayed by the author, 2026-07-18) + the existing v0.52.
- 2026-07-16 · v0.52 · **Generic service = switchable capability catalog (author's concern "not every app its own to-do list — chaos").** **New Golden Rule (§2):** `[HARD]` one base function = **one** service with a versioned capability catalog; every capability individually switchable via the §10.2 switch matrix (Global→Tenant→Area→Role→Action, minimum wins), role-configurable, with sensible defaults ("everything configurable, nothing has to be configured," §1.2); extension = catalog extension of the one service, never a parallel app build. Generalizes §10.2 (AI switch) to all capabilities and §5 (table capability catalog) to all services. **§6:** Capability-catalog sentinel `[HARD]`. First example: task/to-do service (an instance of A3 control-loop core; WorkHub logic gets absorbed, Mares/Francesco/… consume it). ToDo extended by v0.52 items. Source: exclusively this chat + the existing v0.51.
- 2026-07-16 · v0.51 · **Legal-norms register + regulatory horizon (author's finding "why didn't the system report the CRA on its own?").** Closes a genuine governance gap: the master governed inward (sentinels/avatars ahead of outgoing work, §19 incoming voices, §18 aging, §13 dependencies), but had no **horizon scan for applicable legal norms** — Lex was reactive. **New Golden Rule (§2):** `[HARD]` **Legal-norms register** — exactly one living register of applicable norms, rule in the master/list in the tool (§13/§18 construction pattern); per norm: applies-to · obligations · deadline timers · status · Lex review status; maintained by Lex + Chronos; proactive question "which norms apply to what we're building?" for every product profile + periodically. First entry: CRA (EU 2024/2847). **§6:** Regulatory-horizon sentinel `[HARD]`. Avatar representation: Lex (legal status/deadlines) · Chronos (timers) · Tekton (physical/conformity truth of the hardware). Builds on §13 (register/timers), §18 (freshness), §14 (incident/reporting duty ≈ CRA reporting). Legally binding classification (class, conformity path) remains Lex's to determine `[OPEN]`. ToDo extended by v0.51 items. Source: exclusively this chat + CRA requirements matrix SCLAN (team) + verified CRA timeline (EU Commission) + the existing v0.50.
- 2026-07-16 · v0.50 · **Longevity security triad + crypto agility (author instruction "officially add the security triad in a prominent place"; occasion: 25-year device commitment, key & licensing authority, CRA).** **New Golden Rules (§2, prominent):** `[HARD]` **Security triad** — guarantee · crypto disclaimer (honest, beyond the statutory CRA minimum support; wording Lex `[OPEN]`) · mechanism (firmware swap + takeover via [mn] root of trust); mnemonic "longevity is not claimed, it is delivered — the guarantee promises, the disclaimer honestly limits, the mechanism keeps it." `[HARD]` **Crypto agility** — negotiated suite with a minimum floor, agile root of trust (criterion of the vendor-neutral MCU profile), separation of the asymmetric signing layer (uniform, AES-independent) vs. AES data encryption (negotiated; shared bus = shared floor); tier floors `[OPEN]` (proposal: partner AES-256 / DIY AES-128). **§6:** Crypto-agility sentinel `[HARD]`. - 2026-07-16 · v0.49 · **Developer roles mirror the technology profiles (author question "separate Online/Web vs. Hardware/Embedded/Firmware?").** Answer: yes, in **three** zones along the existing §5 profiles, with the seam as a third element. **New Golden Rule (§2):** `[STANDARD]` **Developer roles mirror the technology profiles (three zones, one seam)** — Web/Online · Embedded/Firmware · Gateway/Edge as an **instruction seam with exactly one named owner**; what is separated is who builds, not the rule set (master/sentinels/gate/board remain one source); maintenance role per control loop zone-appropriate (§11.3), hardware release never through the web role. Rationale: disciplines, tools, and **release gates** genuinely differ (web deploy gate ≠ hardware test-plan gate); deepens the function/operations separation from v0.35 on the development side. **§6:** **Developer-zone sentinel**. Cautions documented: no hard silo formation for a small team (gateway needs both competencies; services A4/C4 reach into the gateway). Builds on the §5 profiles (WEB/SERVER · SCLAN EMBEDDED/FIRMWARE · SCLAN GATEWAY/EDGE), §2 v0.35, §11.3, glossary "instruction seam." ToDo extended by two v0.49 items. Source: exclusively this chat (author question 2026-07-16) + the existing v0.48.
- 2026-07-16 · v0.48 · **Storage-per-workload / polyglot persistence (author's fundamental question "why MySQL and not Cassandra?").** Determination: convenience is not an architectural reason — nor is felt performance; storage follows the **shape of the load, measured rather than felt**. "Pro-SQL and pro-Cassandra — each only where the load demands it." **New Golden Rule (§2):** `[HARD]` **Storage follows the load, not habit** — transactional business core relational (rule-enforced by ledger integrity v0.42, referential integrity §4, duplicate/deletion search v0.47, aggregate views §11.4); wide-column/NoSQL only as its own profile for **measured** write-heavy, distributed, eventual-consistency-tolerant load (SCLAN telemetry, mass logs); no storage switch on suspicion (I0/"Prove it, don't claim it"). **§5:** Teaching construction pattern **storage-per-workload** with full explanation (the misconception cleared up, the two storage families and their price, why the core is relational, decision matrix load→storage, five rollout examples). Concrete products remain `[OPEN]` (§5 profile choice) — the rule fixes the selection logic, not the product. Builds on §5 (stack = profile per project), §2 v0.42/v0.47, §4, §11.4, §18.5. ToDo extended by two v0.48 items. Source: exclusively this chat (author's fundamental question 2026-07-16) + the existing v0.47.
- 2026-07-16 · v0.47 · **Data-quality & duplicate governance ("no fake nonsense data," author instruction in the professional-output session):** **New Golden Rules (§2):** `[HARD]` No fake/nonsense data in live operation · `[HARD]` Duplicate watch at the moment of creation (real-time, staged: identical → similar → distantly similar; merge/correct/deliberately kept separate with a stated reason) · `[STANDARD]` Auto-completion from existing records · `[STANDARD]` Data-quality grooming control loop · `[HARD]` Deletion with similarity tier (broader deletion within the requester's identity vicinity; third-party data never deleted/disclosed; evidence dossier as an accountability record; block list `[OPEN]` Lex) · `[STANDARD]` Deletion confirmation as a construction pattern (duplicate disclaimer, explanation of the broader deletion including an apology, feedback channel `[OPEN]`). **§5:** Deletion-confirmation template (seven mandatory building blocks, recipient-language, DE lead version + EN derivation). **§6:** four sentinels (duplicate watch [HARD] · fake data [HARD] · deletion similarity [HARD] · deletion confirmation). **§12.3:** similarity tier anchored. Avatar representation **confirmed by the author (2026-07-16)**: Vera ⟷ duplicate-watch/fake-data sentinel · Lex ⟷ deletion-similarity/deletion-confirmation sentinel · Chronos ⟷ grooming deadlines. Builds on §10.13 (duplicate detection as a review task), §12.2/§12.3, §2 v0.11/v0.13, §2 v0.2 — nothing doubled. ToDo extended by seven v0.47 items.
